# Intakt user guide

The complete reference, kept as it was; the user guide starts at [docs/user/README.md](../user/README.md).

For owners who run Intakt. Each feature gets a page here, written in the same change as the feature: what it does, how to set it up, and what to do when it goes wrong.

Intakt runs on PHP 8.5.12 or later and Symfony 8.2, which is a beta until 8.2.0 is released: an install holds Symfony 8.2.0-BETA2, locked before it was 3 days old by the owner's one-time exception ([The release cooldown for dependencies](#the-release-cooldown-for-dependencies)).

The [README](../../README.md) opens with a picture of the whole team: the octopus conducts, then the Tech lead, Developer, Code reviewer, Judge, Security engineer, Designer, AI engineer, Maintainer and Technical writer, in that order. It follows your light or dark color scheme and is decoration only.

The path from a clone to a first verdict, in order:

1. **Set up:** run `docker compose up -d --wait`; no file needs editing to start, and a provider key (or a Hugging Face token, `HF_TOKEN`) goes in `.env.local` ([the demo](#the-demo-with-docker-compose)). Without Docker: `composer install`, `php bin/console doctrine:migrations:migrate`, and the keys as described in [Configuring providers and API keys](#configuring-providers-and-api-keys).
2. **Choose the panel:** copy `config/intakt.example.yaml` to `config/intakt.yaml` and set the project's tiers and its `review:` section ([Capability tiers per project](#capability-tiers-per-project)); `php bin/console intakt:review:panel <project>` shows the panel a change would get without calling a model.
3. **Start the workers:** `php bin/console messenger:consume async` ([Background work](#background-work)); the container already runs them.
4. **Review a branch:** `php bin/console intakt:review <branch> --project=<project> --task="..."` ([Reviewing a branch](#reviewing-a-branch)) and read the outcome ([Reading a verdict](#reading-a-verdict)).
5. **Watch or script it:** the [dashboard](#the-dashboard) shows each round; an agent starts and reads reviews through [MCP](mcp.md).

What the guide covers today, feature by feature; the parts marked planned arrive later:

- Running the demo with `docker compose up`: it seeds a sample project and a sample repository with a change to review (see [The demo](#the-demo-with-docker-compose)); the timing from the start to the first verdict is on the [timed run](../bench.md#timed-run) once it is measured from a clean clone
- Choosing the Code reviewers by the size or paths of a change, and the dry run `intakt:review:panel` (see below). A project's panel and its quorum policy (`all`: every reviewer approves; `majority`: more than half approve; `one-strong-plus-one`: at least two approve, one of them a strong reviewer) are set in the configuration file (see below), and `intakt:review` runs it (see [below](#reviewing-a-branch)).
- Reviewing a branch of a local repository with `intakt:review` (see [below](#reviewing-a-branch)); reviewing a pull request is planned
- Checking whether a verdict still counts for a branch of a local repository, with `intakt:verdict:identity` (see [below](#checking-whether-a-verdict-still-counts))
- Watching a project's open pull requests: the code that lists them with conditional requests and returns only those whose head or update time changed exists, as does the code that reads a head's CI state and decides to wait (CI running, or a run cancelled or queued and not run again), skip (CI red) or review (CI green); a queued workflow run that never started, or a queued check suite with no check run yet, is ignored once a newer run of the same workflow or check for the same event and pull request exists on the head (a check suite of a workflow run is the same check as another only when their runs have the same workflow, event and pull request, and only a newer check suite replaces it, never a workflow run, not even its own; a check suite of no workflow run is never replaced, but one still queued with no check run more than 30 minutes after GitHub created it is ignored, because an app that started nothing by then would otherwise keep every head waiting; a queued pull request run whose pull request GitHub does not name, as from a fork, and its check suite keep the head waiting), a queued check suite that already has check runs or shows no count keeps the head waiting, any running run (a requested rerun included) keeps the head waiting, and a cancelled run a newer one replaced is ignored as before, started or not; a `watch` section makes a tick run them every minute (see [Capability tiers per project](#capability-tiers-per-project)); the GitHub App identity reads the pull requests and CI from GitHub for a project whose `watch` section names its `github` repository
- Project mode per project: `live`, the default, or `reviewer-for-loop`; `mode: shadow` is no longer a mode and stops Intakt from starting (see [below](#project-mode))
- Reviewer-for-loop mode per project: a project set to `mode: reviewer-for-loop` has Intakt's panel review the heads of the build loop that developed Intakt in place of the loop's Claude reviewer; its verdicts count, and the only thing it writes to the forge is its verdict on each head, as a commit status `intakt/review`, one pull request comment, the `status:` label of the pull request and, only when someone changed that label, one restoration comment (see [below](#reviewer-for-loop-mode))
- Running a job in a fresh container of its own, with its own memory limit and a check at start that its folders and allowed hosts work, and cleaning up after a crash with `intakt:job:sweep`; `intakt:check:run` runs the check before a push in one, and no build or review runs in one yet (see [Job containers](#job-containers))
- Checking a build's bundle before a push with `intakt:check:run`: the project's check in a fresh clone, starting from a saved dependency install of the same lock files when one fits, and a stop for a file that lost most of its content, and checking a build's pull request text with `intakt:check:pull-request-text` (see [The check before a push](#the-check-before-a-push))
- Scanning one GitHub Actions workflow file for anything beyond a hosted runner with a read-only token and no secrets, with `intakt:forge:workflow-risk` (see [below](#scanning-a-workflow-file-for-ci-risk)), listing the changed files that are part of the CI definition with `intakt:forge:ci-paths`, and scanning the head's, main's and the merged workflows of a change with `intakt:forge:ci-scan`, and holding a risky CI change until your `approve <sha>` with `intakt:forge:push-approval`, which shows its complete diff on the task's issue; `intakt:build:task` does not call it yet, although it pushes builds (see [below](#holding-a-risky-ci-change-for-your-approval))
- Building a project's job image with `intakt:job:build-image`, from its devcontainer, its Dockerfile or `composer.json`, and checking that it has the project's PHP and extensions, and with `--kind=review` the image of a review job, which a project names in its `review_job` section; no review runs in such an image yet (see [Job images](#job-images))
- Taking tasks from GitHub issues: the code that chooses the next task exists, and running builds from it is planned; so far only `intakt:queue:show` reads it ([Showing the queue of tasks](#showing-the-queue-of-tasks)). It considers an open issue with the project's eligibility label (`intakt`, unless the project's `watch` section names another `eligibility_label`), applied by an account with write access, and passes over one that also has the `idea` label. Before it picks a task it asks GitHub again whether the issue's author and its last editor still have write access, and it passes over a task when either has lost it. It picks nothing in a round where that check fails, where the stored state cannot be read, or where the issue changed since it was listed in a way that steers the queue; the reason goes to the log. A task marked failed for good, and every task that waits for it, is never picked. Today `Waits for #n` holds a task while the named issue is open. A wait on a trusted pull request (one whose author and last editor both have write access) holds the task while the tasks that pull request closes are open, or, when the pull request names no task it closes, while the pull request itself is open; a wait on any other pull request holds nothing. Holding a task until a waited-for pull request is merged into main is planned. Intakt will learn whether a waited-for issue is closed only from the one list of open issues and pull requests it reads each round (a list that comes back incomplete releases no wait), never by reading each waited-for issue on its own. The one exception is a waited-for pull request: once a complete list no longer shows it open, Intakt reads that pull request once by its number to learn whether it merged; only a merge releases the wait, a failed read is tried again the next round, and after three failures in a row at most once a day, a pull request found open again keeps the wait and is watched in the listing as before, and while a pull request closed without a merge still holds a wait it is read again at most once a day. A worker with no room for new work will ask GitHub nothing, so routine reads do not use up GitHub's hourly allowance (see the [design](../design/05-architecture/03-choosing-work.md)).
- Running builds on their own (the build lane) is planned. Its review cap rule exists as code: only finished work that waits for a reviewer or is being reviewed counts towards the cap, and work that waits for CI, for a CI runner or to be merged does not, so Developers will not wait while no review is due. Its limit on all work in flight exists as code too: every pushed piece of work not merged yet counts, whatever it waits for, and Developers will wait when that count reaches the limit, by default twice the review cap, so merge conflicts and CI load stay bounded; one urgent build may start past the review cap but not past this limit, and a rework of an open pull request is not held by it, because it replaces work already counted (see [The build lane's review cap and in-flight limit](../architecture/lanes.md)).
- Looking at projects, reviews, the page of one review and the steps of one task in the browser (see [below](#the-dashboard)); the button that starts the next round of a change is described [below](#the-dashboard)
- Connecting an agent through MCP: the owner's server answers `list_projects`, `review_status`, `review_verdicts` and `review_act_list`, serves a round's findings, and starts a review of a branch with `start_review` when the owner's token is sent and writes are allowed (see [Connecting an agent through MCP](mcp.md)); starting a review of a pull request is planned

The highlights page (the Symfony and Symfony AI features Intakt uses) is assembled from small pieces and published from main when its pieces, its generator or its workflow change, daily and by hand, at <https://shinyredapples.github.io/intakt/highlights/> ([how](../architecture/highlights-page.md)); `bin/highlights` builds it locally as `docs/highlights.md`. The journey page is published beside it at <https://shinyredapples.github.io/intakt/journey/> ([how](../architecture/journey-page.md)). The site opens on a front page at <https://shinyredapples.github.io/intakt/>. `bin/pages` builds the front page and both pages each time the `highlights` workflow runs: daily, on a manual run, and on pushes to main that touch its paths.

For whoever maintains a copy of the Intakt repository: [Running Intakt's CI on your own runner](self-hosted-ci.md).

For moving a project from that loop to Intakt, and back: [Taking over from the earlier loop](takeover.md).

## Reading a change from a local repository

Intakt can treat a branch of a local git repository as a pull request: the branch is the change and the repository's base branch is what it is compared with. It reads the branch's latest commit, the diff against the point where the branch left the base, the list of changed files and any file as it was at that commit (by its exact name, even one starting with `:`; a binary file only by its size), ignoring git's replacement refs (`git replace`) and graft file (`.git/info/grafts`), which could otherwise show other content under the same commit or hide commits. It reads the repository with its own git settings: the repository's configuration and attributes cannot run a program (a hook, a filter, a diff or signature tool, or a fetch from a remote the repository names for an object it lacks: such an object is an error, never fetched) or change what is read, every file in the diff is shown as text except a binary file (one with a NUL byte anywhere, before or after the change), which the diff names as binary with its size at the base and at the head instead of showing its bytes (in a text file, a byte that is not valid UTF-8 is shown escaped, such as `�{E9}`; a branch that adds, changes or deletes a file whose name is not valid UTF-8 cannot be read, and the error names the file with such escapes; a file whose permissions alone changed shows only its old and new mode, never as binary), and the git configuration of the user running Intakt (`~/.gitconfig`, even a broken one) is not read. A repository whose `.git/info/attributes` file exists cannot be read, because that file could hide a change and git offers no way to ignore it: remove it first. It notices when the branch has moved to a newer commit since it was read. A name that is not a valid branch name (such as `../tags/v1`) is treated like a branch that does not exist. It only reads; it never changes the repository. `php bin/console intakt:change:summarize <branch>` prints what it reads (see [Summarizing a branch](#summarizing-a-branch)); for an invalid name it prints the same error as for a missing branch and exits with 1.

## The dashboard

Intakt serves fourteen pages in the browser (among them the Status page at `/lanes`, reached from the board's status line, a finding's own page under its review, and the demo run's `/demo`, see [Playing the demo run in the dashboard](#playing-the-demo-run-in-the-dashboard)), built from the ledger of review events and of the steps of `intakt:build:task` (no model call) and, for the forge budget on `/reviews`, from the budget GitHub's own answers last reported (no forge call). The dashboard's own address (`/`, for example `http://localhost:7781` in the container) shows the board, except on the demo (an install with the sample repository) before any review has run, where it sends you to the setup wizard's Ready step (`/setup?step=5`, see [The demo with docker compose](#the-demo-with-docker-compose)). On a phone (a browser that sends the hint `Sec-CH-UA-Mobile: ?1`, or whose user agent contains `Mobi`, as iPhone Safari and Firefox on Android do), the address opens **Waiting on you** (`/waiting`) instead, also from a link in another app, unless the install is quiet: when the board holds at least one review, every card on it is Done, nothing is running (no review and no build) and nothing waits on you, it opens the newest review instead, so a board with no review yet still opens Waiting on you; the demo's Ready step still comes first. The Now entry of the menu, **Back to the board** and **Show N more** open the board on a phone too (they link to `/?board=1`), while the Intakt logo opens the dashboard's address. The board has nine columns (Waiting on you, Backlog, Ready, Planned, Building, Checking, In review, Merging, Done) and shows them side by side, each with its name and the number of cards in it across all projects. Waiting on you, In review and Done are always shown. The other six hold the tasks of features that do not run yet, so while one of them holds no card it is left out, and one line above the columns, starting with a small **Coming next** chip, names the left-out ones instead, for example "Backlog, Ready, Planned, Building, Checking and Merging show here once a task is in one of them"; the line is text, not a button. Once such a column holds a card, the next load of the board shows it again in its place. Under the title "Board", one line in muted text says what is going on: "Nothing is running yet. Start the first review." on a new install, a link to the setup wizard; otherwise which changes the Code reviewers are reviewing (or, with no review running, "A build is running." or "N builds are running." while builds work on tasks, else "Nothing is running."); it says nothing of what waits for you, which the Needs you card says. While something waits for you (a notice the Waiting on you page, below, lists, such as an outage), a **Needs you** card under the title and any banners shows the waiting octopus, the number of decisions ("1 decision", "3 decisions") and "Oldest since" the time the oldest one was noticed (UTC, with its date when it is from an earlier day), and opens the Waiting on you page; the board's own octopus is then hidden. Between the status line (below) and the columns, **Working now** shows one card per change in progress, each review whose latest round runs and each task a build of `intakt:build:task` works on, with its title, its project, a chip saying "Reviewing" or "Building" and the path Plan, Build, Check, Review, Merge, done steps filled and later ones outlined. The role doing the work stands on the current step: the beaver on Build, the meerkat on Review, with the hedgehog beside it while the reviewer named `security` still reads; a screen reader reads it as, for example, "Code reviewer, now on Review". A review's card opens its latest round; a build's card opens nothing. With no change in progress, Working now is one line: "Nobody is working yet: reviewers appear here while they review a change". While a review runs, the part that names what the Code reviewers are reviewing is a link: clicking it opens the review page of the first change it names. It keeps up as reviewers start and rounds change while the live connection is up. On a phone (narrower than 761 pixels) it counts the running changes instead of naming them ("The Code reviewers are reviewing 3 changes."), so it stays within two lines; the Status page names them. Each change under review is one card, in In review while its latest round runs and in Done once that round is judged; the card shows the change's number, its project, once the latest round has an outcome that outcome as its headline (**Approved**, **Changes requested**, **Blocked** or **Failed**, with its icon, in larger type on its own line above the title), "Review of" the change, its round (with the word's explanation when you point at it), the number of reviewers and the cost so far, and opens the review page of its latest round. A round from before Intakt recorded its change shows its round id instead. A card whose latest round (the one it shows and opens) was stored as a replay (a recording played back for the demo) carries a **Replay** label beside its project: that round's verdicts were never given on the change and never count as one. Each round keeps its own mark: a round started before its project was marked as a replay stays live, so a card whose latest round is live has no label, and a replay round started after a live round of the same change gives its card the label. Waiting on you is pinned as the first column and is a little wider than the others; it is meant for cards that wait for you, and since no card waits there yet (the outage notices Intakt records are listed on the Waiting on you page, below, not as cards), it holds no card and counts 0. Every column on the board without a card, on a new install and after, says in one line what will appear in it and when, for example "What only you can decide appears here when Intakt asks you" in Waiting on you and "Reviews appear here while a panel reviews a change" in In review. On a new install, before any review has run, a **Set up Intakt** button beside **Open the review list** opens the setup wizard at its first step (`/setup`, below); once a review has run, the button is on the Projects page instead, and `/setup` still answers at its address. The project filter hides the cards of other projects; the counts stay the totals. A column with more than 12 cards shows its first 12 and a **Show 28 more** button (the number is the cards left) under them, which shows the rest; with a project chosen, the 12 and the number count only that project's cards, the button disappears when 12 or fewer are left, and choosing a project never folds an opened column back. When a column changes while the board is open (a review starts or is judged), the live update brings only its first 12 cards; the button is then a **Show 3 more** link that reloads the board, where **Show more** shows the rest, and its number counts the cards left out of every project, also with a project chosen; a column you opened with **Show more** shows its first 12 again. A long title wraps to at most three lines and a long change number or project name on a card is cut short with "…"; pointing at the number or the project name shows it whole. With many projects (the board is checked with six), every card, Working now card and banner names its project in words, never by a color, the project filter is a list that holds any number of them, and only the columns scroll sideways, never the whole page. Backlog (once it holds a card and is shown) and Done start collapsed, each to a 108 pixel strip with its name and count side by side; clicking the strip opens the column with its cards, and **Hide** in its heading collapses it again (each page load starts them collapsed). Only when every other column is empty, as after the demo, does Done start open, and the board starts scrolled to its right end, where Done is, so its cards show without a click or a scroll. When the columns are wider than the window, the side with hidden columns fades out (the right edge first, the left one too once you scroll right), so you can see there is more. On a phone (narrower than 761 pixels) they are not collapsed: every column shows its cards. **Open the review list** leads to `/reviews`. From 1700 pixels of width every dashboard page and its top bar leave 48 pixels at the sides instead of 32; the board has no maximum width and grows with the window at every width. On a phone the word "Round" on a card, which the keyboard reaches on its own, is 44 pixels tall like every other control. The row of columns itself takes the keyboard's focus with Tab, so the arrow keys scroll it sideways, also when it has no card yet; the board passes the UI check (below) at every width in both themes.

Above the columns, the board shows one quiet status line: "Running smoothly · N reviewers working" ("1 reviewer" for one), with ", N builds running" ("1 build") added while builds of `intakt:build:task` are in flight ("Running smoothly · 1 build running" when no reviewer runs), or "Quiet · nobody working" while neither runs. It links to the **Status** page (`/lanes`, reached from the line; Now stays marked in the menu), which repeats the line and lists the lanes: the reviewers still running in the latest round of each review that the judge has not decided and that has not ended as **Blocked** (see the review page below), newest review first; a reviewer that started an hour ago or more (the review ceiling) is no longer counted, in the lanes or in the line, even when its round was never decided; each with a green dot and a small meerkat head (only a picture, which screen readers skip) before its role and tier, the time it started (UTC), its change, its project and its round. Each lane links to the review page of the round it is working on. In a narrow lane the role, tier and start time wrap onto further lines instead of running into the next lane. With no lane, the page says what will appear there: "No reviewer is working right now: reviewers appear here while they review a change". The project filter hides the lanes of other projects, but the count stays the total over all projects. Build slots, runners and budgets are not part of it yet, so there is no idle count and no total.

Above the status line, the board shows one red banner for each pull request whose head failed in the watcher (the same heads as "Pull requests that failed" on `/reviews`): "Pull request 7 of alpha failed.", then how many failures in a row and the next try (UTC), or that it was handed over to the Maintainer, and the cause, with any credential in it replaced by `[redacted]`. **See the failed pull requests** opens that section of `/reviews`. The project filter hides the banners of other projects. The board does not update them live: a banner goes away on the next load of the board after the watcher reads the head again, starts its round or forgets the pull request. Below 761 pixels a banner is one line with the pull request and project; tapping it shows the rest, also on a board without the live connection, and tapping it again closes it.

**Waiting on you** (`/waiting`) lists every item only you can act on, oldest first. Today the only such items are usage outages: an AI account whose usage Intakt has not read for 3 hours since its last reading (the same outage it warns about in its log, see [Cooldowns, pauses and outage notices](#cooldowns-pauses-and-outage-notices)). An item stays only while the outage lasts: once a newer usage reading of that account arrives, the item leaves the page on its next load (nothing is deleted). The newest reading counts by the time it was taken, not by when it was stored: an older reading that is stored late, after a newer one has cleared the outage, never replaces the newer one, so the item does not come back. Each item is a card with the Maintainer (a tortoise, with its name "Maintainer" written beside it); a first line saying what waits ("No usage of account claude_code.main has been read since 2026-11-02 08:00 UTC"); a line with "All projects" (an outage concerns every project, so the project filter never hides it) and how long it has waited ("Waiting 2 h 5 min"); what happens meanwhile (the budget guard starts at most one call at a time on that account, and none while any of its five-hour or weekly windows that has not reset yet stands at its limit, so builds queue); and one button saying what happens next. The button opens the public status page of the account's provider (Anthropic's for Anthropic and Claude Code accounts, OpenAI's for OpenAI and Codex accounts) and says that it shows whether the provider reports trouble; other providers have none, so their button reads "See the queue on the board" and says that it shows the build queue on the board (the board has no filter for one account). Both add that the card leaves when readings return, because readings usually return by themselves. The octopus waits once at the top, beside how many things need you; a card has an amber edge. From 1100 pixels wide the button sits on the right of the words; on a narrower window everything is one column with the button last. When nothing waits, the page says **Nothing needs you**. Answering an item on the page itself comes later.

Beside the list, from 1500 pixels wide, or under it on a narrower window, the page shows two panels. **Meanwhile** lists the reviews running now, longest running first, each as "`<change>` of `<project>` is in review, round `<n>`" with a link to its review page and how long it has run ("No review is running now." when none runs); building tasks join it later. **You handled today** lists the outages that ended today (the UTC day), newest first, each as "Readings of account `<account>` came back" with the UTC time of the reading that ended it ("Nothing has left the list today." when none did). The items and both panels come from one read, so an outage that ends while the page loads is listed either as waiting or as handled, never as both. The page shows what was true when it loaded; it does not update itself yet, so reload it to see changes.

Every page sits in the same frame. On the left, the menu shows the Intakt logo, which opens the dashboard's address, and one entry per page that is built: today **Now** (the board), **You** (Waiting on you, with an amber count of what it lists when that is not zero), **History** (also highlighted on its Trends sub-tab) and **Projects**, each highlighted while you are on it (the Status page keeps Now highlighted; Team and Settings join them as their pages are built). **Collapse** at its bottom narrows the menu to its icons; this browser remembers it. The bar along the top holds the search field, with the hint `Ctrl K` (`⌘K` on a Mac): clicking it, or pressing Ctrl K (Cmd K on a Mac) on any page, opens the search window over the page (see Search, below). Beside it, the moon or sun button switches between the light and the dark look and remembers the choice in this browser. Below 761 pixels of width (a phone) the menu becomes a 68 px bar at the bottom of the screen, each entry an icon over its name (Now, You with its count on the icon's corner, and History), and the top bar holds only the logo (it opens the dashboard's address), a **Search** button (it opens the search as a full-screen sheet, described under Search below) and the theme button; the project filter is hidden there, so every project is shown (History is the exception: its toolbar has its own project picker on a phone), and the choice comes back on a wider window. Projects sits behind **More** at the end of the bar, which opens a sheet with it above the bar (tap beside it or press Escape to close it); Settings joins it once its page is built.

- An address that does not exist (for example `/settings`) shows a "Page not found" page in the same frame, with a **Back to the board** button. If Intakt itself fails on a page, the page says so in plain words and shows a short reference to quote when you report it; the details are in Intakt's log under the same reference. Under an address the dashboard does not answer (another name or another port, see below), such a page is plain: the message and the link without the frame, and without the name of any project.
- `/components` shows each building block of the dashboard (buttons, a button with only an icon, segmented tabs, filter chips, cards, banners and the one-line phone banner, inputs, a number stepper, a command with a Copy button, usage bars including a dimmed "Last known" one, the four review outcomes, and so on) in the light and the dark look side by side, with a "Switch theme" button that flips the look and remembers it in this browser. It loads no font, style or script from another website. A review outcome is one of four labels, each with its own icon: "Approved" (the only one with a green check mark), "Changes requested", "Blocked" and "Failed" (the only red one). "Judged" and "In progress" say where a round stands, not how it ended. The reviews list (`/reviews`) shows the same four labels on its rows, and the review page shows them in the one-line heading of each judged round and for each reviewer in a round's table (a reviewer still running or stopped shows that instead, and one that answered without a decision shows "answered"). Its "Characters" section shows the team's drawings in both looks: the code reviewer (a meerkat with a magnifying glass), the judge (an owl with a gavel), the maintainer (a tortoise with a toolbox) the developer (a beaver with a wrench, which the setup wizard's Ready step shows beside "To fix" and the board's Working now card shows on a build) and the security engineer (a hedgehog with a shield, which stands beside the meerkat on a Working now card while the reviewer named `security` still reads), each next to its name, and the octopus waiting, conducting, bowing and asleep. They are decoration only: screen readers skip them and they never move. The review page's "Now" block shows them too: a small meerkat head before each reviewer's line and an owl beside "The judge's call", and so do the live log (a small meerkat head before each reviewer's heading) and each round's "Findings and the judge's call" heading (a small owl), and on the Status page each lane (a small meerkat head after the green dot, before the role); the words stay as they were, and the outcome is still told only by the words and labels. The board shows a tinted block, "Give Intakt its first task", with the octopus waiting inside it, above its heading, until a review or a task has run, and again, without a reload, once the board has no review left (on the compose demo, after `intakt:demo:remove` took away the only replayed one); and the reviews list (`/reviews`) shows it above "No reviews yet" while it has no review. Once a review or a task has run and nothing is running now (every card in Done, no review in progress and no build working on a task, since a running build also keeps the octopus awake), the board shows the octopus asleep under its title instead, 144 pixels high (120 on a phone); while one project rests and another works, it shows only while the project filter shows a resting project, so never on a phone, which has no filter. The block's three steps say to choose **Set up Intakt** and add an AI provider and a project, to start the first review there (it shows up in In review, then in Done once the judge decides), and that reviewers show up on the Status page while they review it. The board keeps its one **Set up Intakt** button. The setup wizard's Ready step (`/setup?step=5`) shows the octopus conducting above its title **See a real review**, and bowing once any round of the first review was approved, even when a later round starts or is sent back. On a review's page, the newest round shows a small bowing octopus beside its outcome line once it is approved, also when it is approved while you watch; older rounds and other outcomes show none, and the "Approved" label in the round's heading and the outcome line stay what tell you the outcome. Its sample sentence explains three words when you point at them or reach them with Tab: "version" says that an approval covers that exact commit only and that a new push needs a new approval, unless it leaves what you approved exactly as it was (marked planned: for a limit like secrets, the same files, unchanged; approving a merge is planned too, and the one approval Intakt asks you for today is the push of a risky CI change, for its exact commit, see [Holding a risky CI change for your approval](#holding-a-risky-ci-change-for-your-approval)), and that a revised CI push held for you always needs its own approval. The segmented tabs, the filter chips and the stepper only show how they look for now: no page uses them yet, so pressing one changes nothing. The Copy button works, here and on the Waiting on you page: pressing the button puts the command on the clipboard and the button says "Copied", or "Copy failed" when the browser offers no clipboard (it does so only in a secure context it allows: HTTPS or a loopback address such as localhost) or refuses it; it says "Copy" again two seconds after it showed its answer, a new press while it shows one starts the two seconds again, and so does the answer to that press when it arrives, and going Back to the page shows "Copy" too. Nothing links to it yet.
- `/projects` lists the projects that started a review, one row each with its name and a line such as "2 review rounds, 1 judged." (how many rounds it has had and how many of them the judge has decided), or says "No project has started a review yet." with a **Set up Intakt** button. Once it lists a project, a **Set up Intakt** button at its top opens the setup wizard, and the primary **Add a project** button beside it opens `/projects/new`.
- `/projects/new` is Add a project: five steps, one per page, chosen by `?step=` (1 Repository, 2 Install the App, 3 Check, 4 Share and label, 5 Start; anything else opens step 1), with the same numbered markers, Back and Continue as the setup wizard, a breadcrumb back to Projects and **Cancel** on step 1. Step 1 checks a posted repository address exactly as the wizard's step 1 does. Step 2's **Install the App on GitHub** button is disabled; the installation comes from `GITHUB_APP_INSTALLATION_ID`. Step 3 lists each project whose main failed to boot, with the redacted reason (the board's boot banner reads the same lines), or "No failed boot of main recorded", and **Run again** reloads it. Step 4 checks a posted weekly share, set with a slider from 0 to 100 that starts at 20 and shows its percent beside it ("Enter the weekly share in percent.", "The weekly share is a whole number from 0 to 100."; after a refused share the slider is back at 20), and issue label, prefilled with `intakt` ("Enter the issue label.", "A GitHub label is one line of text, without tabs or line breaks.", "A GitHub label has at most 50 characters."). Step 5's **Start** is disabled. Nothing is kept: a project, its repository and its `watch.eligibility_label` are still set in the project file.
- `/trends` shows figures about the reviews of every project as tiles and three charts, and under them what is running or waiting to start now, see [The Trends page](#the-trends-page).
- `/setup` is the setup wizard: five steps, one per page, at `/setup?step=1` to `/setup?step=5`; no step, or anything that is not 1 to 5, opens step 1. Above the step, five numbered markers (1 Repository, 2 GitHub App, 3 AI account, 4 Check, 5 Done) each link to their step; the ones before the shown step are marked done, since the wizard keeps no record of finished steps. On a phone only the bars show, with the line "Step 3 of 5: AI account" under them, and the step's buttons stay at the bottom above the tab bar. Each step has **Back** (except step 1) and **Continue** (except step 5; step 3 shows **Skip** in its place while it offers the key field).
  - Step 1, **Connect your repository**: paste an address and press **Check the address**. An empty field gives "Paste the repository address."; another host gives "Only a repository on GitHub can be connected."; anything that is not `https://github.com/owner/name` (a trailing `.git` or slash and `www.` are fine) or `owner/name` gives "This is not a repository address: use https://github.com/owner/name or owner/name." A good address shows "GitHub, repository owner/name". Intakt does not keep it yet: a project's repository is set as `github` under its `watch` section in the project file that `INTAKT_CONFIG` names.
  - Step 2, **Create Intakt's GitHub App**: the **Create the app on GitHub** button is disabled, because creating the app from the page is not built yet; Intakt reads its app from `GITHUB_APP_ID`, `GITHUB_APP_INSTALLATION_ID` and `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` (see [Intakt's GitHub App](#intakts-github-app)).
  - Step 3, **Add an AI account**: which providers have a key; while none has and none is kept, one field to paste a key with **Keep the key**, which comes back to step 3 with what happened, and **Skip** (see [Running the demo on a Hugging Face token](#running-the-demo-on-a-hugging-face-token)); the variable for each provider without a key; and the first project's tiers. A kept key that cannot be read shows as the step's error.
  - Step 4, **Check the project**: for each configured project, its Code reviewers (or why it has none, marked broken) and whether a boot of its main branch failed, with the reason as the board's boot banner gives it, or "no failed boot of main recorded". With no project, "No project is configured yet: ..." is the step's error. **Run again** reads it again.
  - Step 5, **Ready to start**: **See a real review**, with **Play the demo run** as the step's one main button and "1 minute · no key" above it while the recorded review can play; once it has played, "The recorded review has played. Its rounds are in the review list."; otherwise one plain line says the install has no recorded review and, while the key field of step 3 is shown, "Add a key in step 3, AI account, to run one live.", else that a live review can be started or opened below, or that nothing below can start one yet. Then Read, Check, To fix (the reviewer, the judge and the developer), and **First review**: on the compose demo **Start the first review** reviews the sample change; once a review ran, **Open the review** opens it.

  The wizard shows no command to copy and no MCP address: connecting your own agent is described in [MCP](mcp.md). Keeping the repository address, creating the GitHub App and adding more than the one key from the browser are not built: they are done in configuration today.
- `/reviews` lists every round, newest first, one row each, titled by its change and round number ("sample-change, round 2"; "Unnamed change" or "round not numbered" for a round started before Intakt recorded them). A round with an outcome leads its row with it, before the title, as a label with its icon in larger type (**Approved**, **Changes requested**, **Blocked** or **Failed**). Under the title the row shows the project, before there is an outcome the round's state (**In progress** with a green dot, or **Judged**, without a check mark, for a judged round without a recorded quorum), the number of findings once judged, the number of reviewers, and when it started as a relative time ("just now", "5 min ago", "2 h ago", "3 d ago"; hover it for the stamp in UTC). The whole row is one link to the round's review, at least 44 px tall. The round id is below the link in small type; one click selects it for copying. `/reviews?project=<name>` chooses that project in the top bar's project filter (below); each project on the first page links there. Above the rounds, under "Pull requests that failed", a card for each watched pull request whose head failed last time it was tried names the pull request and whether it is backing off or was handed over to the Maintainer ("Pull request 7: backing off"), shows the cause in red, then the failures in a row and the next try (UTC); the card goes once the head is read, its round is queued or its pull request is closed. When the project filter hides every round or every such card, the list says "No review for this project yet" or "No failed pull request for this project" instead of leaving an empty list. The cause is redacted like a log line, so a configured key or token never appears on it. When a GitHub App installation is set (`GITHUB_APP_INSTALLATION_ID`), the section "Forge budget this hour" at the top lists it with its calls used and calls remaining this hour, from the latest answer GitHub sent, and marks the forge reserve **In force: routine reads wait** while less than the reserve is left (see [Intakt's GitHub App](#intakts-github-app)), else **Not in force**; before an answer of this hour reported the budget it says "No reading this hour". The section shows what was known when the page loaded; reload to see newer figures.
- `/search?q=<words>` answers the search window of the top bar: not a page but a piece of one, a list of results in groups, in this order: **Actions**, **Reviews** (each round titled by its change and round number, such as "sample-change, round 2", with its project and state and, once it has an outcome, that outcome first in the row as the same icon-and-words chip the review page uses (Approved, Changes requested, Blocked or Failed), and, on a line below in small type, its round id, which one click on the id itself selects for copying; a round whose change has no name reads "Unnamed change", one without a number "round not numbered"; the whole row, at least 44 pixels tall at every width, links to its review, all but the id's own text; a round id typed into the search still finds its round), **Projects** (each with its number of review rounds, linking to its reviews) and **Pages** (Reviews, Projects, Trends and Components). Words are separated by any white space, including a no-break space or an em space, and spaces of that kind at the start or end of what you typed are ignored. A result is found when every word you typed appears in its name, its line under the name, its group's name or, for a review round, its round id, in any case; each group shows at most 10. Every result names its project, or says "All projects". With no words it shows **Recent**, the 5 newest review rounds, then the actions. When nothing matches it says "No results for" your words, suggests what to try (a project of yours by name, a round id or a page), and offers **Clear the search**, which asks again with no words. The actions today are "Add a project", which links to `/projects/new`, and "Switch light or dark theme".
- **Search**: the search window opens over the page, which stays behind a dark veil and cannot be reached until the window closes. The cursor is in its field, whose focus ring follows its rounded edge with room between the ring and the text, which starts empty and shows Recent and the actions; every letter you type asks `/search` again and shows its groups. The first result is highlighted: the Up and Down arrows move the highlight (past the last result it starts again at the top), Enter opens the highlighted result (a review, a project's reviews, a page or Add a project) or runs it ("Switch light or dark theme" switches the look and closes the window), and Escape, Ctrl K (Cmd K) again or a click beside the window closes it and puts the cursor back where it was. A click on a result does the same as Enter, and pressing the mouse on a result leaves the cursor in the field, so the arrows and Enter keep working after it. The round id under a review is the exception: a click on the id itself selects it for copying and neither opens the review nor closes the window, while a click elsewhere on its line, such as on the words "Round id" or beside the id, opens the review like the rest of the row. Until the results for what you just typed have arrived, the arrows and Enter do nothing, so Enter never opens a result for other words. If you type with an input method that composes text, such as Japanese, Chinese or Korean input, Enter and the arrows go to it while it composes, so Enter confirms your text instead of opening a result. Tab stays inside the window. When nothing matches, **Clear the search** empties the field and shows Recent again. If Intakt does not answer, the window says "Search did not answer."; typing again asks again. On a phone (narrower than 761 pixels) the window fills the whole screen, shows a **Cancel** button next to the field instead of the key hints, and has result rows at least 52 pixels tall; Cancel closes it like Escape does, and Tab then also stops on Cancel. Matching words are not highlighted in the results yet.
- `/reviews/<round id>`, linked from each round on `/reviews`, shows the whole review the round belongs to: every round of the same change in the same project, in round order. A pull request's rounds count as one change whatever letter case names it (`acme/widgets` or `Acme/Widgets`), and also with a `#fragment`, a `?query` or a trailing `/` after its number, so they are numbered 1, 2, 3 in one sequence and shown as one review, also across an upgrade; rounds an older install stored under two spellings of one pull request (two letter cases, or an address with a `?query`, a `#fragment` or a trailing `/`) keep their own numbering and their own review. Such an old review kept apart is history only: its page offers no **Start round**, and new rounds of the pull request start from the review under its plain lowercase address. Two local branches whose names differ only in letter case (`feat/Widget` and `feat/widget`) are two changes, as git treats them, on SQLite and PostgreSQL; MySQL and MariaDB compare names without letter case, so there they share one numbering. While a round runs, a reviewer's heading in the live log, its role and model, appears with that reviewer's first live update of any kind: a tool call, streamed text, an answer or a retry notice. When a reviewer is asked again, the new try's text replaces the old one and the heading already shown stays as it is; a reload shows the model of the latest try. Each round stored as a replay carries a **Replay** label beside its heading in the timeline, and beside "Now: round N" while it runs; a live round has none, also in a review that mixes live and replay rounds. Only when every round of the review was stored as a replay does a **Replay** label under the title say "A recording played back: its verdicts were never given on this change and never count as one." While the latest round is not judged yet and has not ended (see the end of this item), a current state block, "Now: round N in review", shows how many of its reviewers finished, each reviewer's role, model (its tier and `model not recorded` for a reviewer started before Intakt recorded the model or whose start recorded an empty model, as in the reviewer table, so the cell is never empty) and its outcome (or, while it has none, its state in muted words), the open findings (the act list of the newest judged round: every P1 and P2 finding that passed the quote check, whatever the judge marked, and the P3 findings the judge marked `act`, each linking to its card in the Full record (see [The review page's main view and Full record](#the-review-pages-main-view-and-full-record)), which opens with that card's pass open and shows the card just under the top bar (on a phone under the Timeline and Live log switch too), and each at least 44 pixels tall on a phone; "No finding is open." before any round is judged) and that the judge has not judged the round yet; on a page the live updates reach, the finished count changes as reviewers answer, a stopped reviewer turns `stopped`, and once the judge decides the block goes without a reload: the owl's card states the call and the finding groups take the block's place, while in the Full record the round's heading shows its outcome with "Judged" in muted words and the timeline shows its findings and calls. The round's outcome line (the outcome and the quorum policy) also appears there without a reload. The Timeline, the Live log, the session summary, the line of facts and the round tables described next are on the Full record. Under "Timeline", every round the judge has decided has a one-line heading (after the round number, its outcome as a label with its icon: "Approved", "Changes requested", "Blocked" or "Failed"; then "Judged" in muted text, `outcome not recorded` when the round has no recorded outcome, its number of findings, and the time it started and the time it was judged, in UTC) ending in a chevron that turns when the round is open; a click or Enter on the heading opens or closes the round, and Escape anywhere in an open round closes it and puts the keyboard back on its heading. The newest round of the review starts open when the judge has decided it, so a judged review shows its verdict and findings without a click; every other decided round starts folded to its line, also the newest judged one while a newer round is still in review; a round judged before Intakt kept the findings says `findings not recorded` there instead of a count, with how many ids the judge marked (`2 marked by the judge`) when it marked any; the round still running stays open. While the latest round is not judged and has not ended, a "Live log of round N" panel sits beside the timeline (under it on a screen 1100 pixels wide or narrower; on a phone, 760 pixels or narrower, a Timeline and Live log switch that stays under the top bar shows one of the two, the timeline first (Tab reaches the chosen one of the two tabs and then the chosen pane, also a live log with nothing in it yet; the Left and Right arrow keys choose and focus the other tab, Home always chooses Timeline and End always chooses Live log, also when that tab is already chosen), and choosing a finding id brings the timeline back when the finding has an entry there (a finding of a round still running has none yet, so the live log stays); from 1500 pixels wide the page grows and the log scrolls within 520 pixels, and from 2200 pixels wide the session summary below becomes a column beside the log) with each started reviewer's tool calls in plain words (`read src/discount.js`, `listed src/`, `searched for "discount" in src`, never a tool's name, and `[redacted]` in place of a recognized credential, a known token or key format, in a searched text or a path) and text under its role and model, and once its answer is read, its verdict and one line per finding (severity, file and line, and text written as on the finding cards); when a reviewer is asked again (its first try broke off, was stopped at its output limit, could not reach the model endpoint, or gave no usable verdict), the second try's text replaces what the first try wrote, under one line "First try gave no usable answer, so the reviewer was asked again.", so the two answers are never read as one (the first try's tool calls stay in the list, because the log shows what each reviewer did); before any reviewer starts it says they will appear there, and once every round is judged the timeline takes the whole width. A session summary stays at the bottom of that panel while the log scrolls above it: the round's tool calls by kind in plain words ("File reads", "Directory listings", "Searches") with their total (just "No tool call yet." until there is one), the cost of the round and of the whole review so far (each figure sits under its label, and a long one such as `0.0140 USD, prices of 2026-11-13` wraps at its spaces, never letter by letter), each file the reviewers read, and the findings this round (each open finding the round checks, marked `Checked, resolved` once the round resolved it or `Open`, and each other finding it reported, marked `Reported`; reported findings appear once every reviewer is in; code the reviewer marked with backticks is shown as code and bold or italic marks are left out, as on the finding cards). Each finding id stays on one line beside its text, also on a phone; a long finding text wraps in its own column instead. Choosing a finding id there highlights that finding's entry in the timeline, opens its folded round and scrolls to it, moving the highlight off the earlier one, also on a page the live updates do not reach; a finding the judge has not marked yet has no entry, so nothing changes. The figures come from what Intakt recorded for the round, summed over its reviewers, kept up to date on a page the live updates reach: the tool calls, the cost of the round, the files read (with `[redacted]` in place of a recognized credential, a known token or key format, in a path) and the findings change as the reviewers work, without a reload (the cost of the whole review so far changes after a reload). Without the live updates the page shows them as of when you loaded it. A tool call whose record could not be saved is left out of those figures (the reviewer carries on and gets its answer as usual, and Intakt logs an error), so a total can be one short. A round Intakt holds no record of says so instead. For each round: its number with its outcome beside it ("Approved", "Changes requested", "Blocked" or "Failed", with its icon; while it has none, its state in muted words such as "In progress"; a judged round adds "Judged" in muted text after its outcome, unlike `/reviews`, whose rows show only the outcome), then its id and start; a reviewer table with one row for each reviewer that started: its role, the model it runs on (for a reviewer started before Intakt recorded the model or whose start recorded an empty model, its tier and `model not recorded`), its outcome (a green dot and muted `running`; once it answered, its outcome with its icon, "Approved" with the check mark, "Changes requested" or "Blocked", also for one recorded while the project was in the former shadow mode; "Failed" with why it failed in muted words; "Blocked" with why; or muted `stopped`, followed by the substitute that answered for it when there is one; a failure in plain words, such as `stopped at its output limit`, never an error's class name) and its number of findings (`no answer yet` while it runs, `no answer` when it gave none), with a Stop column only while a reviewer of a round that is neither judged nor ended (also by a failed judging) is still running, so no column is empty; once the judge has decided, the round's outcome line ("Outcome: Approved", "Changes requested" or "Blocked"), decided by the panel's quorum policy over all its reviewers exactly as `intakt:review` decides it, with how many reviewers failed: a failed reviewer gives no approval, so a two-reviewer panel never quietly becomes a one-reviewer approval (a round started before Intakt recorded its quorum policy says so instead of an outcome); once the judge has decided, every finding as a card: its severity as a chip (`P1`, `P2` or `P3`), its id (`F1`, `F2`, ...) and, as its title, the first sentence of the text's first line (a sentence never ends inside code the reviewer marked with backticks, so `` Keep `Ready? yes` as the prompt. More. `` gets the title "Keep `Ready? yes` as the prompt."), with the whole text below the title when it goes on, and `Text not recorded.` as the title when the text is missing or only white space; the text is written with code the reviewer marked with backticks shown as code and without the delimiting backticks (backticks quoted inside code, in a longer run such as ``` `x` ```, stay) (a backtick the reviewer did not close marks the word after it as code) and with the bold or italic marks the reviewer put around words left out (`**greater than**` reads as greater than, `*why*` as why; marks inside a word or a code span, such as `max_retry_count`, stay), its file and line (`src/A.php:7`, or `File and line not recorded`), and the judge's call on it (`Fix now`, `Dropped: deferred to a follow-up issue` or `Dropped: rejected or not marked by the judge`; a finding that plain code dropped before the judge is listed after them as "Quote not found in the code", with the quotes the check counted (under each, the lines of the change recorded as closest to it, with their file and line), a line saying file names, numbers and suggested-fix blocks are not checked, and `Dropped before the judge:` with its reason behind a closed **Quoted code** disclosure; findings at one place, or with one cause the judge named within one changed part (hunk) of one file and at most five lines apart, that are all on the act list are one card with one finding number, `F1`, and the other numbers below it as `Also reported as F4`, so a merged finding has no card of its own); the cards are one column, and on a phone each takes the full width with its text under the chip; and the tokens the round's model calls used (prompt, cache read, cache write, cached, completion and total, the same figures as the [token line](#reviewing-a-branch) of `intakt:review`). On the main view, the owl's card gives the answer first (see [The review page's main view and Full record](#the-review-pages-main-view-and-full-record)): **Blocked:** or **Failed:** is followed by the reason in plain words (for example "the judging failed", or "every reviewer failed, so nobody checked this change"), and a round judged before Intakt recorded its outcome reads "Checked, but the outcome was not recorded". While the latest round is still in review there is no card. If **Start round** is pressed for a project that has no reviewers set up, the page says "This project has no reviewers set up yet, so nothing was started." Above the card the main view shows the task the review checks (the task sentence of the latest round, or "Task not recorded." for a round started before Intakt recorded it); under its title the Full record shows one line of facts with small icons: the project, the change (in full, never cut), the number of rounds, the tokens (with a thousands separator, such as 2,800) and the cost of all of them, and when the latest round started (UTC). A few small labels say what kind of figure a number is, in the same words `intakt:review` prints: one in the summary line, `estimated` after the review's cost, and one in the column head of each table of the newest round, `counted` on the reviewer table's "Findings" and `estimated` on the token table's "Cost", so the page shows at most three labels however many rounds it lists. The tables of older rounds carry none. The figures under a head carry no label of their own, and the token counts, the number of rounds and a folded round's finding count carry none. Hovering over a label names where its figures come from (the rows of Intakt's ledger for a counted one, the configured price list for an estimated one); the labels are not keyboard stops, and the tip opens to the right of its label, or flips to the other side when the window has no room there. Each cost with a figure has the day of its prices after it (`prices of 2026-11-13`, or `price date unknown` when no `price_date` is set); an unknown cost has neither a date nor a label, and a round whose cost is unknown has no label on its "Cost" head. Tokens and cost are never added into one total: they stay in their own cells. Cost is the figure `intakt:review` prints for the round, at the prices in your settings; for a model with no price it says `unknown, no price for <model>`, and for a call whose provider reported no input tokens it says plain `unknown`, never 0 and never an estimate. A round judged before Intakt recorded the findings themselves says so and still lists the ids the judge marked `act` or `follow-up`, with `Severity not recorded` and `Text not recorded.` on their cards; it never claims the reviewers found nothing. A round started before Intakt recorded the change's name in the ledger is shown as a review of its own. A running reviewer in a round that is neither judged nor ended (a round ends too when its judging failed) has a **Stop this reviewer** button; pressed for a round that is judged or has ended, also from a page opened before, it stops nothing and gets `404`; pressed when no reviewer of the round runs any more, it stops nothing and returns to the page with the line "No reviewer runs in this round right now, so nothing was stopped." The button asks the reviewer to stop, and the reviewer ends at its next progress update with the verdict `blocked` (the finding `reviewer-stopped`) and the state `stopped`, without a reload on a page the live updates reach (after a reload otherwise); a stopped reviewer has no button. While the latest round is in review (not judged and not ended), a **Stop** button on the main view, at the top right, asks every reviewer of that round that is running at the press to stop, the same way; like **Stop this reviewer** it asks for a second click first ("Click again to stop the round"), and Escape takes the question back. A reviewer that has not started at the press is not asked, and starts as usual; the judge is not stopped either. Pressed while no reviewer is running (before the first one starts, or while the judge works), it stops nothing and returns to the page with the line "No reviewer runs in this round right now, so nothing was stopped." On a page the live updates reach, the button goes away without a reload once the judge decides. Pressed for a round that is judged or has already ended, also from a page opened before it ended, it stops nothing and gets `404`. The page of a round in review passes the UI check (see [Checking the pages with the UI check](#checking-the-pages-with-the-ui-check)) at all three widths in both themes. A round the judge never decided ends as `blocked`. `intakt:review` prints `blocked` for it in every case, and the MCP `start_review` tool returns `blocked` as the `decision` of its structured response, while the page tells the two causes apart: it shows **Failed** (with `Failed: the judging failed` under the heading) when its judging failed for good, or **Blocked** with `no outcome in time` (and the same advice to start a worker) once the round is older than the upper wait of its panel (one hour for each reviewer plus one for the judge) without being judged. Such a round no longer counts as running: it has no "Now" block, no live log and no Stop button, and its heading shows the outcome (**Blocked** or **Failed**) with the reason under it. An unknown round id gets `404 Not Found`.
- `/tasks/<project>/<issue number>` shows where a task that [`intakt:build:task`](#building-one-task) worked on is, or where it stopped: the steps of its latest run, the run whose `task` step was recorded last (each step the command records names its run, so the steps of an earlier run are not shown, also when that run is still going and ends a step after the latest run did). Steps recorded by an older Intakt name no run: for those a run starts again at `task`, after a failed step and at a step that does not come later than the one before, so no step shows twice or after a failed one, but two runs of that time that overlapped can still mix on the page. Under the title "Task 42 in alpha", a meta line names the project, the issue number and when the latest step ended (UTC), and a line says `Stopped at mirror:` with the failure's reason when the latest step failed (`Stopped at push:` with the push's reason, for example the remote's refusal, when the push failed), `Every step passed.` when the `push` step passed, `Parked at build_job: the build asked a question.` when the build ended with a question that parked the task, or `Last recorded step: task passed.` otherwise. A parked task also shows a "Parked question" section with the question, its choices, `Recommended:` and the recommended choice, and when it was parked (UTC), each text shown as plain text with any credential replaced by `[redacted]`; the section is left out when the question is no longer stored, and a later run whose build job built removes the stored question. Under "Timeline", each recorded step (`Task`, `Mirror`, `Clone`, `Build job`, `Hand back check`, `Pre push check`, `Push`, in the order the command runs them) has a **Passed**, **Failed** or (for the build job of a parked task) **Asked** label, the time it ended (UTC) and, for a failed step, its reason, with any credential in it replaced by `[redacted]`; each step after the latest one says **Not reached**. A step row the command does not write (an unknown step or outcome, a failed step without a reason, an `asked` step other than the build job) is left out. The page shows what the ledger held when it loaded; reload it to see a later step. Nothing links to it yet (the board has no build cards), so type its address. A project and issue number with no recorded step gets `404 Not Found`.

The pages answer only an address on the computer itself: `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]` with the dashboard's port, which is `DASHBOARD_PORT` (default 7780, set it to the port you publish on the host). Any other address in the request, or another port, gets `400 Bad Request` and no data, so a web page that points its own name at your computer cannot read them. These names stop other web pages, not other machines: a program that reaches the port can send an accepted name. What keeps the dashboard to your computer is publishing its port on the loopback address only, as `compose.yaml` does; publish it on another address and anyone who reaches it can use the dashboard ([Who can act](../design/05-architecture/13-dashboard.md#who-can-act)). Every page comes in a light and a dark theme: it follows the browser's light or dark setting (`prefers-color-scheme`) unless a theme was chosen and stored in the browser (`localStorage`, key `intakt-theme`) with the theme button in the top bar of every page or the "Switch theme" button on `/components`. The top bar also holds the project filter, a select of All projects and every project of `/projects` and every project a build has worked on, in name order: choosing a project hides the rows of every other project on `/projects` and `/reviews` and its failing-head cards, in the browser without reloading, and the browser stores the choice (key `intakt-project`) for every page; a stored project that is no longer listed counts as All projects. A `/reviews?project=<name>` address with the exact name of a listed project (`1` is not `01`) overrides and replaces the stored choice. That address (what a project's row on `/projects` opens) and every review page keep Projects marked in the menu and start with a breadcrumb back: "Projects / <project>" on the list, "Projects / <project> / <change>" on a review page (the round id when no change is recorded), where Projects and the project are links; choosing another project or All projects there removes `project` from the address, so a reload keeps the new choice, and removes the list's breadcrumb and the Projects mark with it. An address naming a project the install does not list shows neither. When a project starts its first review round, a worker (see [Background work](#background-work)) sends the filter's list again over the live connection (below), so an open page's filter lists the new project without a reload; a round of a project already listed sends nothing; the filter keeps the project you chose in that tab and the rows stay filtered by it, and shows All projects (with every row) only when the new list no longer names your project; the stored choice is never changed by a pushed list, and when the live connection is down the filter simply keeps working as before. On a phone every page is laid out at the phone's own width (`<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">`), so the phone layout of the building blocks (larger controls, one-line banners) applies. Its fonts (Outfit, Atkinson Hyperlegible Next, Atkinson Hyperlegible and JetBrains Mono, each with its license under `assets/fonts/`), styles and scripts are served by Intakt itself, so a page asks no other host for anything. The shared building blocks of the coming pages are built (buttons, chips, counts, cards, list and setting rows, inputs, bars, banners, sub-tabs, toasts with Undo, explained terms, loading, error and empty states): a danger button asks you to click again before it acts and forgets that after 4 seconds or when you press Escape, a toast disappears after 5 seconds unless the pointer or focus is on it, and on a phone a banner shrinks to one line that opens when tapped. The `/components` page shows them all, light and dark side by side, with the hover, focus and pressed look of buttons, cards, inputs and tabs held on show (no other page does that), and gives every field, select and switch on it a name a screen reader reads out. `/projects`, `/reviews` and the review page use them: each opens with its title and a short line under it (a review page and a project's list of reviews first show the breadcrumb described above), projects are list rows, failed pull requests are cards, states are labels, tables have quiet headings and lines between rows (on a narrow screen a wide table scrolls sideways, and Tab stops on each table so the arrow keys scroll it), an empty list shows an empty state, the start button is a primary button and "Stop this reviewer" a danger button, which asks "Click again to stop" first, also on a page without the live connection. The list pages are plain HTML, apart from the small script in the page head that picks the theme, the one after the page that runs the menu, the theme button, the search hint and the project filter, the one in the page head that makes a danger button ask first and opens or collapses a board column and a phone banner and switches a review page between its timeline and live log on a phone (all answer clicks; the danger button also Escape, and the switch the arrow keys, Home and End, and Escape in an open round), fades the board's side with hidden columns (on load, scrolling, clicks and resizing) and runs the search window (it asks `/search` only while you search), and, when the live-update hub can authorize a listener, Turbo and Stimulus from the import map for the live connection (see below); they never refresh themselves: reload them to see newer rounds. The review page of a round that is not judged yet and has not ended listens to the live-update hub and changes by itself (see below). On `/reviews`, two rounds of one change appear as two rows of the same project; both link to the same review page. On a review page, one action is offered at a time: while a round is in review (until the judge has called it or it has ended) the next round cannot be started and only "Stop" shows, also before the first reviewer starts and while the judge works; otherwise the button "Start round N" (N is the number of the next round) starts the change's next round from the repository and base recorded when its latest round started, then returns to the page with a short notice floating at the bottom of the window, "Started round N.", that leaves the page where it was and goes away after five seconds on a page with live updates (or a line above the page with the reason it could not start; a failure Intakt has no reason for shows the error page instead, with a reference the log carries too). The button asks for the number after the latest round the page knows, so pressing it twice, or pressing it on a page opened before another round started, starts nothing and says that another round took the number. The repository is recorded as an absolute path, so a round started with `--repository=.` restarts from the same repository. Rounds started before Intakt recorded the repository, base and task show a note instead of the button. The round runs in the workers; the new round's page shows its progress live, and a reload always shows what the ledger holds. Its reviewers read a snapshot of the head in the system's temporary directory (`intakt-kept-review-*`), which Intakt removes once the round is judged; a round that is never judged keeps it, and `git worktree remove --force <path>` removes it. The dashboard has no login: anyone who can reach the dashboard's address on the computer can press the button. Each reviewer that is still running in a round that is neither judged nor blocked has a "Stop this reviewer" button in its row. Pressing it records a stop request in the ledger (`reviewer_stop_requested`, naming the round and the reviewer) and returns to the page with a line saying so; a reviewer that has already answered, failed or been blocked, or a round that is judged or has ended, has no button and a post for it is refused with `404`, except that a post while no reviewer of the round runs returns to the page with the line "No reviewer runs in this round right now, so nothing was stopped." The worker running that reviewer looks for the request each time the reviewer reports progress (a call to the model, a tool call, a piece of streamed text) and stops it at the next one; a stop pressed while the reviewer waits to be tried again, or before its call to the model starts, still lands at its next progress update. A model that reports nothing for a long time (one long answer that is not streamed) stops only once it reports progress or answers. The stopped reviewer's verdict is `blocked` with the reason `reviewer-stopped`, and the ledger records `reviewer_stopped` (round and reviewer); a stop request that was answered this way never stops a later run of that reviewer. The page then shows the reviewer's state as `stopped` and no button, live while it is open and after a reload (a stopped reviewer still counts as `blocked`); a reviewer that the model reports as stopped without your request is never shown as `stopped`. Like the start button, it needs no login.

On a review page, "Start round N" and "Stop this reviewer" share one lock per review, whichever of the review's round pages you press them on, so two of these presses for one review never run at the same time. A press that arrives while another one for the same review is still being handled does nothing and returns with the line "Another press for this review is still running, so nothing was started." (or "... nothing was stopped."); press again. The round-wide **Stop** button does not take this lock.

The review page uses plain words, while the CLI, the MCP tools and these docs keep the technical terms: **Review pass** is a round (so "Now: round 2 in review" reads "Now: Review pass 2 in review"), **To fix** is the act list (the open findings), **Checked** means judged, and **Changes needed** is the outcome Changes requested. In the state block and the Full record, pointing at one of these words, or at its "?", shows the technical term; the owl's card and the finding groups use the same words without a "?", and a finding row's tooltip names the finding's severity (P1, P2 or P3).

### The review page's main view and Full record

`/reviews/<round id>` is the review's main view, with one message. Under the title it shows the task sentence, then the owl's card once the latest round is judged or has ended: `Pass N · Checked` (just `Pass N` for a pass that ended without the judge's call), then **Approved**, **Changes needed**, **Blocked:** or **Failed:** with the reason, then, on a judged pass, the number to fix in large type (`0 to fix` on an Approved pass), an urgency bar with one segment per finding, urgent first, and the counts in words under it, each with its shape (a triangle for urgent, a dot for should fix, a ring for nice to have; a finding whose urgency was not recorded is counted on a line of its own), and on the card's right one meerkat per reviewer with its model's AI lab (the model's id when Intakt does not know its lab, "Model not recorded" when none was recorded) and marked with a check (approved) or a cross (changes needed); a face without a verdict shows a dash and its status in words ("Failed", "Blocked", "Stopped" or "No verdict yet"). Under the faces is one line saying whether the reviewers agree ("Both say changes needed", or "1 of 2 says changes needed" when the other reviewer failed or gave no verdict; a failed reviewer is never counted as agreeing). The line appears only when at least one reviewer gave a verdict. The card is amber when changes are needed and carries a **Replay** label when the latest round is a replay. Under the card comes the path of the change: every earlier pass as done, each followed by a done Fix when it ended with changes requested, the step it is on now (bold, ringed, with the role whose turn it is standing on it: the meerkat while a pass is in review, the beaver when there is something to fix, the tortoise once it can be merged), then the steps still to come. Pass 2 in review reads Pass 1, Fix, Pass 2, Merge with Pass 2 current. A pass that was blocked or failed shows no path, because there is no next step to name. Then come the start or stop button with a line saying what happens next, the state block while a round is in review, the findings to fix in their groups while none is, and one **Full record** link at the bottom.

The findings to fix of the last judged pass stand in three groups, **Urgent** (P1), **Should fix** (P2) and **Nice to have** (P3), each headed by its shape from the card's legend and its count. They stand side by side from 1024 px wide and stack, Urgent first, on narrower screens. A group with no finding keeps its place and says "None"; findings whose urgency was not recorded get a fourth group, **Urgency not recorded**, so none is hidden. Each finding is one row: its first sentence and its file and line, with the P number in the row's tooltip. There is no explanation text and nothing folds; a kind symbol is not shown, because Intakt does not record a finding's kind yet. The whole row opens the finding's own page.
