The state block lists the findings to fix while a round is in review, so the groups wait until no round is in review and the findings never show twice on one screen.

When the judge's call arrives while the main view is open, it changes without a reload, as a reload would show it: the owl's card comes in, the path moves on to Fix or Merge, the state block goes and the finding groups take its place.

`/reviews/<round id>/record` is the **Full record**, reached by that link: the Replay line, the line of facts, the Timeline with each round's tables, findings and the judge's call, and the Live log, as described above, with the same live updates, and a **Back to the review** link. It opens the newest round when the judge has decided it, and also the round its address names, so a link to a finding's card, such as those of the state block, never lands inside a folded pass. An unknown round id gets `404 Not Found` on both views.

`/reviews/<round id>/findings/<finding id>` is a finding's own page: its urgency, how many reviewers reported it, its first sentence, its file and line, a "Where it sits" map when the finding names a file or quotes code (the file name over a few placeholder rows with the finding's line marked and "Line N" under them, or five unmarked rows and "Line not recorded" when the line is not known, and beside it, under "Quoted by the reviewer", every code span of the finding's text as the reviewer wrote it; left out when there is neither), its whole text when that goes on, each reviewer who reported it with the model, the role and the severity they gave (also when they reported it under an id the judge merged into it; a reviewer who gave several severities shows each), and the judge's call with the merged ids and how many reports it kept once (one per reviewer and per id that reviewer reported; the number of ids instead when Intakt did not record who reported what). A pass judged before Intakt recorded who reported what says so. It answers `404 Not Found` for an unknown round, a pass that is not judged and an id that is not one of the pass's judged findings (a merged id has no page of its own). The code around the finding is not shown, because Intakt does not store it: the rows of the map stand for lines without their text. The page is as it was when opened; it takes no live updates of its own.

### The Trends page

`/trends` (the **Trends** sub-tab of **History** in the menu) shows how the reviews went, read from the ledger's review rounds of every project, whatever the project filter shows. When the install's [bench page](../bench.md#review-bench) (`docs/bench.md`) has its review bench table, the page opens with the bench result in words, such as "The review panel found 24 of 30 planted defects", then "N false alarms in all" (with the number of runs when the bench page states it once), a "measured" label and the date of the run; once a review has run, the octopus conducts beside it, or stands alone in that place when the bench page has no bench table (the only octopus on the page). Under it come tiles, three per row on a phone, each giving one figure above a short label with a few words saying what it counts, and a small role character in its corner (the meerkat for the passes and the findings only one reviewer raised, the owl for the tiles of the to-fix count, the time to verdict and the cost per pass); point at a label or reach it with the Tab key and a tooltip says more. A tile shows only when there is a figure for it, so a page with few reviews shows fewer tiles:

- **Passes** (caption "Reviews run so far"): every review round started.
- **To fix** ("Serious, kept by the judge"): the findings of severity P1 or P2 the judge put on a round's list of things to fix. A round with a finding on that list whose severity was not recorded (kept only as the judge's mark) is left out, because it may have been a P1.
- **One reviewer only** ("Findings no one else raised"): the share, as a whole percent, of the findings the judge kept to fix (one per entry on a round's list of things to fix) that only one reviewer of the round reported, over the rounds with a full record of who reported what. An entry counts with every finding the judge joined into it, so an entry that joins two findings of one reviewer still counts as one reviewer's, and two reviewers that reported the same known finding again both count. When a round completes, Intakt writes down which reviewer reported which finding; only rounds with that record for every reviewer that answered are counted (rounds completed before Intakt wrote it, or with a record that cannot be read, are left out), and the tile's tooltip says so. While no judged round has that record (or its rounds have no entry to fix), the tile is left out.
- **Time to verdict** ("Median, start to verdict"): the median over the judged rounds, from the round's start to the judge's call, such as `4 min 12 s`.
- **Cost per pass** ("Mean over priced passes"): the mean of the rounds that made a model call and whose cost is known, with the review page's "estimated" label; the label's tooltip names the price list's date.

Under the tiles, up to three charts, each with a short title and a chip saying whether its figures are counted or estimated, and, for a screen reader only, a table of the same numbers and a one-line takeaway; neither is shown on screen, and there is nothing to open. A chart with no data, or with only zeros, is left out, and so is one reviewer's small chart with only zeros:

- **Catches by reviewer:** one small chart per reviewer role (such as correctness and security), with a bar per severity (P1, P2, P3) counting the findings that role reported, all on one scale. A legend names the solid part of each bar, and the number in brackets above it ("3 (2 alone)"), as the findings only one reviewer reported (no other reviewer of the same round reported them); a finding the judge joined into an entry counts with the entry's other findings, and under the severity its own reviewer gave it, even when the entry took a higher one from a finding it joined. A finding dropped before the judge (it quotes code the change does not hold) still counts for its reviewer. The takeaway names the reviewer with the most P1 findings and how many of them only it found ("The security reviewer found the most P1 findings, 3, and 2 of them no other reviewer found."), names every reviewer tied at the top with how many only each of them found, or says "No reviewer found a P1 finding.". Each reviewer counts under the severity it gave, so two reviewers that report the same finding at different severities each count their own. Only judged rounds with a full record of who reported what, and at which severity, count, as for the "One reviewer only" tile; rounds from before Intakt wrote down those severities are left out.
- **Checked findings:** one bar per outcome over every judged round: **Kept to fix** (on the act list), **Merged** as a duplicate into another finding's entry, **Deferred** to a follow-up issue, **Rejected**. The takeaway reads like "The judge kept 9 of 14 findings and rejected 2.". Rounds judged before Intakt recorded the findings themselves (only the judge's marks are kept) are left out, because their rejected findings cannot be told apart, and so are rounds where the judge's answer could not be read (Intakt then puts every P1 and P2 on the act list and gives the P3 findings no call, so none of that is the judge's) and rounds from before Intakt wrote down whether that happened.
- **Passes and cost per day:** two small charts on one row of days, **Passes** and **Cost (USD)**, one bar per day on which a round started (days are counted in UTC; days with no round are not drawn). It shows only once rounds started on 3 or more days; before that it is left out, never an empty grid. The cost chart and the table's cost column appear only when at least one day has a cost; with none, the one chart is titled **Passes per day**. Rounds and cost are two charts, not two lines on one, so one color serves both. Each has its own takeaway ("Reviews ran on 4 days; the busiest was Oct 27, with 5 rounds.", "The costliest day was Oct 26, at about 0.0125 USD."), and the cost is marked estimated, as the cost tile is. A day's cost counts the rounds that made a call and is known only when every one of them has a price; a day without that reads "No data" (no bar, in the tooltip and in the table), never 0, and a positive cost reads "less than 0.0001" when it would round to 0.0000 at four decimals (0.00007, for example, reads 0.0001). A day with a positive cost always draws a bar, however small, so it never looks like a free day or a day with no data. Once the days span more than one year, each day carries its year. With many days, the numbers and day names under and above the bars show on every second, third or later bar so they do not overlap; the tooltips and the table still give every day. The screen-reader table lists each day with its rounds and estimated cost. With the **Demo review** toggle off, the demo's days are left out too, so the count of days can fall below 3.

Each bar can be reached with the Tab key, and pointing at it or focusing it shows a tooltip with the count first; it stays while the pointer is over a bar or a bar has focus, and Escape hides it, wherever the focus is. On a phone the charts stack.

A figure with nothing to read it from (no judged round, no judged round whose act-list severities are all recorded, no judged round that records who reported what, no priced round) has no tile, never a 0; a chart without data is left out. The recorded demo review fills the reviewer and judge charts on its own, so a played demo run shows them without a key; the per-day chart is then left out, since the replay is one day. A round counts in the reviewer and judge charts only when every finding the judge named is among the findings it recorded; the per-day chart counts every started round in the selected data. When the ledger holds rounds played back into the replay project (`intakt:demo:replay`), a **Demo review** toggle sits next to the title. It is on (pressed, with a check mark) while those rounds are counted; pressing it opens `/trends?demo=0`, which counts without them and shows the toggle off, and pressing it again counts them again. Before any review has run the page shows the waiting octopus and one button, **Start the first review**: on the compose demo it starts the sample review exactly as the setup wizard's Ready step does; on an install without the sample change it opens the first review on that step (`/setup?step=5#step-first-review`), which says what is missing.

At the bottom of the page, **Up next** lists the review rounds the board shows in review now, from the same state the board reads, so it lists them also before the first round is judged and with the demo review left out of the figures. A round that ended without the judge's call is the one exception: the board keeps it under In review, Up next does not list it. Under **In progress** are the rounds where a reviewer has started, each with the roles of the reviewers that started (a round stays there after its reviewers answered, until the judge decides it); under **Waiting to start** are the rounds no reviewer has started yet, then the rounds the watcher has queued for a pull request whose review has not opened yet ("Pull request 7", "Queued, not started yet"). Within each group the rounds come newest first, and in Waiting to start the queued-only rows come after the rounds whose review has opened. Each started round's row names the change ("Unnamed change" for a round started before Intakt recorded it), the project and, when one was recorded, the round number, and is a link to the round's review page that the Tab key reaches. A queued round's row has no link, because its review page only exists once its review opens; from then on it is listed with its link. The watcher usually opens the review as it queues the round, so such a round is listed with its link at once, under Waiting to start until a reviewer starts. With no round in review or queued it reads "Nothing running or queued.". The list shows what the ledger held when the page loaded; reload to see newer rounds.

### Live updates (in progress)

While a review runs, Intakt sends each reviewer's tool calls, its text and each round's progress ("1 of 2 reviewers finished", "Judged"; sent when the round opens and on every change) to the live-update hub built into FrankenPHP. The round's state also counts how many reviewers started, from the same state as the finished count, and each update changes both together, so a Full record you opened before the reviewers started says "2 of 2 reviewers started" under Reviewers and "2 of 2 reviewers finished" in the round once the judge decides, without a reload (the main view's state block counts the same way until the judge decides, when it gives way to the owl's card and the finding groups). A reviewer's text before a tool call (what it says it reads next) is sent in one piece when it calls that tool. Its answer is never sent as the text it wrote. Once Intakt has read it, the review hands the live updates the verdict and each finding's severity, file and line and title (the first line of the finding's text; the live log shows it without its bold and italic marks and shortens what it shows to 160 characters ending in `…` when it is longer), or, when the answer could not be read as a verdict at all, only a note that it could not be read; the hub gets that and never the reviewer's raw answer, and the review page adds it to the reviewer's live text as a verdict line and one line per finding, or as the single note. The checks Intakt runs on a verdict it could read (a malformed tool call, a finding id reserved for failed reviews such as `reviewer-stopped`, an id that was not open, an id marked both resolved and repeated) come after that, so an answer they refuse still shows its verdict line and findings in the live log; the reviewer is then asked once more (the second try's text replaces it, as below) or, on a second refused answer, fails, and the refused verdict never counts. The review page of a round that is not judged yet and has not ended (blocked with no outcome in time, or because its judging failed) shows them without a reload: the round's "N of M reviewers finished" line and its "N of M reviewers started" line under Reviewers, each finished reviewer's outcome with its icon ("Approved", "Changes requested", "Blocked" or "Failed"; muted "answered" when its outcome could not be read, muted "stopped" for a stopped one) in the reviewer table as a reload shows it, with why a failed or blocked one ended (the finding count of a reviewer the table already listed appears after a reload) and in the current state block, the same count in the block (the block goes once the judge decides, and the round's head turning from muted "In progress" to its outcome, or to muted "Judged" when it has no recorded outcome), the round's findings with the judge's call on each once the judge decides, and, in the live log beside the timeline, each started reviewer's tool calls and text. A reviewer that starts after you opened the page appears with its first live update of any kind (a tool call, streamed text, an answer or a retry notice), under its role and model, and gets a row in the current state block named the same way (`running`, or its outcome once it finishes, or `stopped`); once it finishes or is stopped, it also gets a row in the reviewer table with its role, model, outcome and number of findings, as a reload shows it, without a stop button. When Intakt cannot read the reviewer's start from its ledger as it sends the update, the reviewer shows as "A reviewer (role not read)" in the live log and the current state block, and its table row says `role not read`, `model not read` and "shown after a reload", until you reload; such a row, and a reviewer the table already lists, then shows its state as a muted word ("answered", "failed", "blocked" or "stopped") instead of an outcome. The reviewer table of a round that is not judged yet and has not ended is shown also while no reviewer has started; an ended round without a started reviewer has no reviewer table. Every dashboard page (the project list, the review list, the review page and the Waiting on you page, whose items change only on the next load) also opens one live connection that listens to the shell topic `dashboard/shell`, and gets the same permission on every page, which covers the shell topic and every round's updates, so opening a list does not stop a review page in another tab from updating; only the project filter's list (when a project starts its first review round) and the board (each time a reviewer starts and each time a round's progress changes: its headline line, its **Set up Intakt** button, its first-day octopus, Working now, its In review and Done columns and its status line) are published on it so far, and the board changes those parts to the pushed ones while the page is open, so a review that starts shows as a card in In review and in Working now, and the status line counts its reviewers, without a reload; a build sends no update of its own yet, so a build's Working now card and the build count in the status line change only with such a push or a reload (a card you reached with the keyboard, or an explained term on it, keeps the keyboard focus when its column changes, and the page does not scroll); when the dashboard runs without live updates (for example without a hub key), the board shows these parts as they were when the page loaded, and reloading the page shows the current one; the filter gains the new project and keeps the project this browser tab chose, so the lists do not change by themselves, and the review list and the project list show only what the ledger holds, also when the hub is down. The page needs a browser with JavaScript for this; without it, or when the hub is not running or `MERCURE_JWT_SECRET` is not set, the page shows the same state as the ledger on every reload and nothing else breaks. When the page loses its connection to the hub, a line at the top of the page says "Reconnecting." with the time it was lost, and counts down to the next try, every 5 seconds (Try now tries at once; a try still waiting for the hub's answer is left to finish for about 15 seconds, then replaced by a fresh one); the top bar shows "Offline" (not on a phone). After two minutes the line turns red and says "Can't reach Intakt." Both go away once the connection is back, and if the page had received an update before the drop, the updates sent in the meantime follow. While the line shows, it adds that actions wait until the connection is back: the buttons that change something (Start round N, Stop, Stop this reviewer) are disabled, and enabled again once the connection is back; a button that was already disabled stays so. An elapsed time, such as "42 min", shows the time of the drop beside it ("42 min at 11:12") until then; no page but the components page shows one yet. To run the hub, set `MERCURE_JWT_SECRET` (at least 32 characters) in `.env.local` and start FrankenPHP from the repository with `frankenphp run --config Caddyfile --envfile .env.local --envfile .env`. FrankenPHP does not read the `.env` files on its own, and the first file that sets a value wins, so `.env.local` comes first. The same command also serves the dashboard in worker mode on port 7781 (`SERVER_PORT`), in the `dev` environment `.env` sets; because `.env` sets `DASHBOARD_PORT=7780`, its pages answer `400 Bad Request` there until `.env.local` sets `DASHBOARD_PORT=7781`. FrankenPHP's admin API answers on `localhost:2019` only, where `intakt:server:status` reads it. If the hub is not running, reviews are not affected: the update is dropped and a warning is logged. Intakt also has a general way to send updates to the other dashboard pages, which the project filter and the board's headline use (the Waiting on you count is planned to); it works the same way: when the hub is not running or refuses the update, the update is dropped, the warning "A live dashboard update was not published." is logged, and the page or work that sent it carries on. A `docker build` or an install from the repository needs `php bin/console importmap:install` once, to download Turbo for the dashboard pages that listen for live updates.

### The status document

Intakt can describe what it is doing as one JSON document, the status, which the owner's own dashboard reads until Intakt's dashboard replaces it. Its fields are documented in [status.schema.json](../user/status.schema.json), a JSON Schema, and Intakt's tests check every document they build against that file. Print it with `php bin/console intakt:status --format=json` (JSON is also the format when `--format` is left out, and any other format is refused with an error): the command writes the document as one line and exits with 0. The dashboard serves the same document at `/status`, for example `http://localhost:7780/status` with the default `DASHBOARD_PORT`, as `application/json`; like every dashboard page it answers only when the address names `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]` with the dashboard's port, and answers `400 Bad Request` under any other name, so another site cannot read it through your browser. It answers `GET` (and `HEAD`, which returns the headers and no body); any other method is refused with `405`. The status is read from Intakt's database only, so it still answers while no worker runs; a stopped worker then shows as messages waiting in a queue.

- `generated_at`: when the status was read (UTC).
- `holds`: the install-wide holds in effect (`drain`, `failure-back-off`, `pause`, `budget-back-off`), the one that wins first. Only the name is given, never when it ends.
- `queues`: each background queue with the number of messages waiting for a worker (`async`, `failed`), or `null` for a queue that cannot count them.
- `projects`: one entry per project the watcher watches or a section names, by name. Each has:
  - `mark`: `replay` for a project stored as a replay (a recording played back for the demo, whose verdicts never count), else `live`. Each entry of `lanes.review`, `waiting_for_owner` and `last_outcomes` carries the `mark` of the round it names, as that round was stored: `lanes.review` and `waiting_for_owner` that of the latest round, `last_outcomes` that of the last ended round. A round started before its project was marked stays `live`, and a replay round started after a live round of the same change is `replay` while the live round's outcome stays `live`.
  - `lanes.review`: the review rounds in progress, newest first, each with its change, round id and number, start, its number of reviewers and one step per reviewer a worker has started (role, tier and `running`, `verdict`, `failed`, `blocked` or `stopped`). Fewer steps than reviewers means the others still wait for a worker.
  - `lanes.build`: each task `intakt:build:task` worked on, newest first, with the latest step of its latest run (`task`, `mirror`, `clone`, `build_job`, `hand_back_check`, `pre_push_check` or `push`), whether that step `passed`, `failed` or `asked`, the failure's reason (or `null`), when the step ended and `question`. A task parked on a question shows its `build_job` step as `asked`, and `question` then holds the question it is parked with: its `text`, its `choices`, the `recommendation` and `parked_at`, each text with any credential replaced by `[redacted]`; `question` is `null` for every other step, and for an `asked` one whose question is no longer stored. A failed step is where the run stopped; a passed `push` means every step of the command passed. A task built again shows its latest run.
  - `guard_decisions`: the pull requests the watcher holds back: `pending` (CI still runs, with when it reads again), `red` (CI failed) or `failed` (the head could not be read, with the cause, the failures in a row and the next try).
  - `waiting_for_owner`: the changes on the board's Waiting on you column; empty for now, since Intakt places nothing there yet.
  - `needs_you`: the pull requests handed over after repeated failures, with the cause and the failures.
  - `last_outcomes`: the last ended round of each change, newest first by when it was judged or ended (a judging parked in the failure transport without a ledger record still counts as failed when it is among the 500 parked messages Intakt reads there, with `end` `judging_failed`, and sorts by when its round started), at most ten. `end` says how it ended: `judged`, `judging_failed` (the judging failed for good, after the worker's retries; the dashboard shows such a round as **Failed**) or `no_outcome_in_time` (neither judged nor failed in its judging within its upper wait). A judged round has its outcome (`approve`, `changes`, `blocked`, or `null` for a round started before Intakt recorded its quorum policy) and when it was judged; a round that ended without the judge has `null` for both and is placed by when it ended (when its failed judging was recorded, or when its upper wait ran out). Such a round is no longer in `lanes.review`, and it stays the change's outcome while a next round of the change runs. A judging that failed for good is read from the ledger (event `judging_failed`), not from the `failed` queue, which no longer holds it.

The status carries no secret: a failure cause is redacted like a log line, so a configured key or a token in it shows as `[redacted]`, and holds and back-offs carry no end time.

### Checking the pages with the UI check

`bin/ui-check` opens the dashboard pages listed here in a headless browser, in the light and the dark theme, at 1440, 1920 and 390 pixels wide: `/` (the board), `/components`, `/history`, `/projects`, `/reviews`, `/setup` (the setup wizard, every step: `?step=1` to `5`), `/projects/new` (Add a project, every step: `?step=1` to `5`), `/lanes` (the Status page), `/trends`, `/waiting` (Waiting on you), the search window open on `/reviews`, and the page of the first round `/reviews` lists. For each it reports the accessibility violations axe finds, counted by impact (critical, serious, moderate, minor) with the first elements each one names, and at 390 wide every visible text under 15 px (the text typed into a field, or its placeholder, too) and every button, link, field, search result or other control under 44 px tall (a control counts its label's height; a link inside a sentence is not counted). It saves a full-page screenshot of each as `var/ui-check/<page>-<theme>-<width>.png`, replacing those of the last run. It ends with exit code 0 when nothing serious or critical, no floor finding and no page error (a page that did not answer with success, a script error, or the wrong theme) was found, 1 otherwise, and 2 when it could not run (no browser, nothing answering at the address, or no review round to open).

It needs Node.js 22 or later, Playwright and axe-core, and a running dashboard with at least one review round:

```sh
npm install --global playwright axe-core
npx playwright install chromium
NODE_PATH="$(npm root --global)" bin/ui-check http://localhost:7780
```

The address defaults to `http://localhost:7780`. `CHROME_PATH` names a Chrome or Chromium to use in place of Playwright's own browser.

### Walking user journeys with the journey check

`bin/journey-check <dashboard address>` walks user journeys in a headless browser against a running Intakt, each journey from a fresh page (its own cookies and storage), and prints one line per journey (a query string or fragment on the address is dropped): `passed <journey>`, or `failed <journey> at step "<step>": <what broke>`. A failed journey also gets a screenshot where one can be saved, `var/journey-check/<run>/<position>-<journey>.png` (the run is named by its start in UTC and eight random hex digits, for example `2026-11-24T09-05-07-012Z-1a2b3c4d`, and the file by the journey's place in the run, two digits, and its name, for example `01-read-a-verdict.png`); when the folder cannot be created or the page cannot be captured, the line `no screenshot: <reason>` takes its place and the journey still counts as failed. The last line counts the journeys, those that passed and those that failed. The check has no journey of its own yet, so on a running dashboard it reports `Journey check: 0 journeys, 0 passed, 0 failed.`

Before it opens a browser it asks the address for an answer and waits at most 10 seconds, the startup deadline. The same 10 seconds is the default timeout of each page action (a page load, a click, waiting for a text); the check has no deadline for a whole step. It ends with exit code 0 when every journey passed, 1 when one failed, and 2 when it could not run: no address, an address that is not an http or https URL (its text is not printed back), nothing answering within that time, or no browser. Any output names the address without a user name or password.

It needs Node.js 22 or later and Playwright, like the UI check:

```sh
npm install --global playwright
npx playwright install chromium
NODE_PATH="$(npm root --global)" bin/journey-check http://localhost:7781
```

`CHROME_PATH` names a Chrome or Chromium to use in place of Playwright's own browser.

## Running the container image

The `Dockerfile` builds an image with Intakt on FrankenPHP in worker mode, with the live-update hub built in. To check an image you built, run `docker/smoke-test.sh <image>`: it starts the image with throwaway secrets and checks that the dashboard answers, that MCP requests go to their own worker, that the server runs as an unprivileged user and that the admin port stays inside the container, then removes what it started. CI builds the image and runs this check on main and on every pull request that changes more than `docs/`, `tests/` or `upstream/` (the `image` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`). Every release also publishes a checked image, `ghcr.io/shinyredapples/intakt:<version>` and `:latest` (see [Releasing](../releasing.md#the-container-image)); use it instead of `docker build` when you run a release. Between two requests the worker clears the signed-in user, the database entities it loaded and the other per-request state of Intakt's own services, so one browser never sees another's flash messages or data. Each request also starts with the file permission mask (`umask`), locale and environment variables the worker started with, whatever the request before changed. The same reset runs between two messages of a consumer, so a reviewer's model, tools and conversation stay with its own review: the next one connects to the review tools again and sends only its own model, tools and messages (checked by `tests/Application/SymfonyAiResetTest.php`). A test in the repository (`tests/Application/WorkerModeTest.php`) checks the rest, both as the image runs and with `FRANKENPHP_RESET_KERNEL=1` (a fresh copy of Intakt's kernel for each request). The image does not set it.

```
docker build -t intakt .
# fails if the base image's PHP is below 8.5.12
docker run -d --name intakt -p 127.0.0.1:7781:7781 -v intakt-data:/app/var/data \
  --env-file intakt.env intakt
```

- The secrets are never in the image. `intakt.env` (yours, not in the repository, and left out of the build like `.env.local` and `.env.local.php`) must set `APP_SECRET` and `MERCURE_JWT_SECRET` (at least 32 bytes); the container refuses to start without them, and names the one that is missing. Nothing else is required: the image sets defaults for `MERCURE_JWT_ISSUER` (`http://localhost:7781`), `SERVER_PORT`, `DASHBOARD_PORT` and the other values in its `ENV` lines. When you serve the dashboard from another address, set `MERCURE_JWT_ISSUER` and `MERCURE_PUBLIC_URL` to the address your browser uses. Before it starts the web server, the container asks FrankenPHP to check the Caddyfile and prints the reason when it is rejected.
- Publish the port on `127.0.0.1` only (`-p 127.0.0.1:7781:7781`): the dashboard has no login (whoever reaches the port can act), and a plain `-p 7781:7781` opens it to every network the host is on, where a client can send `Host: localhost:7781` itself. The image listens on 7781 (`SERVER_PORT`). If you publish another host port (`-p 127.0.0.1:8080:7781`), set `DASHBOARD_PORT` to that host port (8080): the dashboard answers only `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]` with that port (see [the dashboard](#the-dashboard)), and the health check keeps calling the port inside the container.
- The database (SQLite) and the hub's history live in `/app/var/data`. Mount a volume there (`-v intakt-data:/app/var/data`): the image declares none, so without it they are lost with the container. The container runs as the user with id 10001, so a bind-mounted folder must belong to that user. It applies the migrations on every start, then serves `/projects`.
- Keep the project file on that volume and name it with `INTAKT_CONFIG` in `intakt.env` (for example `INTAKT_CONFIG=/app/var/data/intakt.yaml`). The image was built without your projects, so the container builds its cache again on every start, after the migrations and before the web server and the workers. A change to the project file, or its removal, therefore applies at the next start (`docker restart intakt`), with no `cache:clear`. A project file the cache cannot be built from stops the start with the reason in the log. A file at `/app/config/intakt.yaml` is read the same way when `INTAKT_CONFIG` is not set, but it is lost with the container unless you mount it.
- Two worker pools serve requests: `app` for the dashboard, and `mcp` for `/mcp/*` with its own thread limits (`MCP_WORKERS`, `MCP_MAX_THREADS`), so a streamed MCP answer cannot take the dashboard's threads. `APP_WORKERS` sets the dashboard's pool, `MAX_WAIT_TIME` how long a request waits for a free thread.
- FrankenPHP's admin API listens on `localhost:2019` inside the container and nothing publishes it; read it with `docker exec intakt php bin/console intakt:server:status`.
- A worker is replaced after `FRANKENPHP_LOOP_MAX` requests (default 500) and when it holds more than `WORKER_MEMORY_CEILING_MB` megabytes (default 256) after a request; it finishes sending the answer first, so no request fails. A crash is restarted by FrankenPHP itself.
- The queue workers and the scheduler (see [Background work](#background-work)) run in the same container, beside the web server: `MESSENGER_CONSUMERS` workers (default 3, so the reviewers of a panel of three run at once) and one scheduler. When one of them stops, for any reason, it is started again a second later and the container logs `intakt: async ended with status 137, starting it again` (`scheduler_watcher` for the scheduler). A worker also ends on purpose after an hour or once it holds more than `WORKER_MEMORY_CEILING_MB` megabytes, between two messages, and is started again the same way. `docker stop` lets each worker finish the message it is on for up to 8 seconds, then stops what still runs; a message cut off is handed out again later.
- Start the container with `--restart unless-stopped`, so Docker starts it again after a crash or a reboot. When the web server itself stops, the container stops the workers and the scheduler and exits, so this restart brings all of them back. The image has a health check (`intakt-healthcheck`) that runs `php bin/console intakt:server:health` (see [Checking the server](#checking-the-server)) and asks the dashboard every 30 seconds and, in the demo, also waits for the seed; read it with `docker inspect --format '{{.State.Health.Status}}' intakt` (`healthy` or `unhealthy`).

## Checking the server

When Intakt runs under FrankenPHP, `php bin/console intakt:server:status` reads FrankenPHP's admin API and prints how many worker threads are busy and idle, how many requests wait in the queue, how often workers were restarted, and whether the Mercure hub is healthy:

```
worker threads: 0 busy, 2 idle
queue depth: 0
worker restarts: 2
hub: healthy
```

Run it inside the container, because the admin API answers only there. It reads `FRANKENPHP_ADMIN_URL` (default `http://localhost:2019`), which must be plain `http` on `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]`; any other address is refused before anything is sent. A value that cannot be read shows `down` with the reason (the server did not answer, answered with an error status, or answered something Intakt cannot use), and the command then exits with a non-zero code. A thread that is starting or restarting counts as neither busy nor idle.

The board shows the same reading in a row under its columns, read once each time the board loads (it is not pushed by live updates): **Threads busy**, **Threads idle**, **Queue depth**, **Worker restarts**, **Hub** and **Forge**. A source that cannot be read shows one red `down` figure with the reason in place of its values (**Worker threads** for both thread counts, **Server metrics** for the queue depth and the restarts), and an unhealthy hub shows `unhealthy` in red; the other figures still show. A refused `FRANKENPHP_ADMIN_URL` shows all three server sources `down` with the reason instead of failing the board. **Forge** is the forge check of `intakt:doctor` below: `connected` when it passes, `not configured` when no GitHub App is set, `checking` while another board load runs the check, or red `down` with its problem. The board runs that check at most once a minute, in one load at a time, and shows the last result in between, so a fix can take up to a minute to show; the load that runs it can wait up to 15 seconds for GitHub. Any credential in a reason is blanked out.

`php bin/console intakt:server:health` reads the same and decides whether the server is healthy; the container's health check runs it. It prints `healthy` and exits with 0, or one `unhealthy:` line per problem and exits with a non-zero code: the hub reports unhealthy, no worker thread is ready (all of them starting or restarting; busy threads count as ready), or a source could not be read, with the reason. For example:

```
unhealthy: the hub reports unhealthy
```

`php bin/console intakt:doctor` runs the same checks for a person at the terminal (in the compose stack: `docker compose exec intakt php bin/console intakt:doctor`), and adds two the board cannot report about itself: the `web server` and the background `workers`. It prints one line per check, `web server`, `worker threads`, `server metrics`, `Mercure hub`, `workers` and `forge`: `pass: <check>`, or `fail: <check>: <problem>; fix: <what to do>`, with any line break or other control character in the text turned into a space. It exits with 0 only when every check passes. It changes nothing and calls no model. A refused `FRANKENPHP_ADMIN_URL` fails the three server checks as `cannot run`, without a request; the other checks still run.

The `web server` check asks the dashboard's `/status` page at `http://localhost:<SERVER_PORT>` (7781 when unset), named as `localhost:<DASHBOARD_PORT>` (the same `Host` header the container's health check sends, though the health check asks `/` and the doctor asks `/status`), and waits 4 seconds at most. It fails when nothing answers or the answer is not a 2xx status, and as `cannot run`, without a request, when `SERVER_PORT` or `DASHBOARD_PORT` is not a port number from 1 to 65535; the other checks still run. Its fix says to set both where your install gets its environment: `.env.local`, or the env file or variables you give the container (`intakt.env` in the `docker run` example). The `workers` check looks in the process list for PHP running this install's `bin/console messenger:consume` with at least one `async` consumer and the `scheduler_watcher` consumer the container starts (a `grep` for those words, another program's `console` script or another install's consumer does not count), and fails naming each one that is missing. A worker of another user started with a relative `bin/console` may be yours, but the doctor cannot see which folder it runs in: the check then fails as `cannot run` and says to run the doctor as the user that runs the workers; a consumer that is restarting at that moment (each ends after an hour and starts again a second later) can show as missing, so run the doctor again. Run it inside the container, because outside it the process list is the host's. When the process list cannot be read, or none of its command lines can, the check fails as `cannot run`.

The `forge` check reads the GitHub App installation back, read-only, and compares it with `GITHUB_APP_PERMISSIONS` and `GITHUB_APP_REPOSITORIES`; it stops after 15 seconds at most. With `GITHUB_APP_ID`, `GITHUB_APP_INSTALLATION_ID` and `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` all empty (no App) it passes without a request. It fails when `GITHUB_APP_ID` is empty while the installation ID or the private key is still set, when the App ID is not a number (spaces around the number are ignored), when the installation ID is empty, when the settings cannot be read, when the private key is not an RSA key that signs, when the installation does not match the settings, and as `cannot run` when GitHub refuses, does not answer in time or is rate limited, or while Intakt's own GitHub calls are paused for a rate limit (then it sends nothing). The board shows the same check as its **Forge** figure. For example:

```
pass: worker threads
pass: server metrics
fail: Mercure hub: the hub reports unhealthy; fix: check the FrankenPHP log for the Mercure hub's error, for example a transport file under MERCURE_BOLT_PATH it cannot write
pass: forge
```

The Caddyfile turns on Caddy's `metrics` option, because FrankenPHP reports its queue depth and worker restarts only then.

## Background work

Intakt has two internal message buses, one for commands and one for questions. Each bus accepts only its own kind of message: a question sent on the command bus, or a command sent on the question bus, is refused before any handler runs. This covers messages Intakt sends itself; a message a worker takes back from the queue is not checked again.

A review round sends one message per reviewer to a queue, and workers take them from it, so the reviewers of a round run at the same time. It also queues one message that has a worker send the dashboard's project filter again when the round is its project's first; a round that is undone before it is stored leaves none of these messages behind. The queue is a table in your database (`MESSENGER_TRANSPORT_DSN`, `doctrine://default` unless you change it in `.env.local`), created by `php bin/console doctrine:migrations:migrate` together with the other tables. Start as many workers as you want reviewers to run at once:

```
php bin/console messenger:consume async
```

The state of a round and of each reviewer is stored in the database, and so are the waiting messages. If a worker stops, start it again: the reviewers still waiting run, and a round whose last reviewer has finished is completed. A reviewer that was in the middle of its review when its worker stopped keeps its place for one hour from its start, so a message that reaches another worker in that hour (the same message is handed out again after ninety minutes) waits until the hour is over; then the reviewer is started again once. If its worker stops a second time, the reviewer is recorded as blocked (`reviewer_blocked` in the ledger, readable with `php bin/console intakt:ledger:events`) and the round goes on without it. A review that takes longer than that hour counts as failed too: an answer that arrives after the hour is dropped and the reviewer is recorded as failed, even when no other worker got the message. A reviewer whose call to the model fails for any reason is recorded as failed too, with the kind of failure (for example a refused key), and the round goes on. A reviewer whose result could not be written to the ledger goes back to the queue and is reviewed again. A reviewer whose call the provider limits (a rate limit or quota) goes back to the queue and its message comes back after the time the provider named, or, when it named none, after a wait that starts at about one second and doubles each time up to at most one minute; a failed attempt does not use up the one restart of a stopped worker. At most 3 calls run at the same time for one provider account (`intakt.vendor_account_parallel_calls` in `config/services.yaml`); a further reviewer waits in the queue the same way. While Messenger will still try a failed message again, its reviewer is left as it is and its next try can still give a verdict. A message that Messenger gives up on, once its tries are used up (it lands in the `failed` queue, shown by `php bin/console messenger:failed:show`), makes its reviewer blocked in the same step, before the message is parked, which counts as finished, so the round still completes. If the reviewer cannot be blocked for any reason (the ledger cannot store it, the reviewer is no longer in the database, the database fails), the worker logs an error naming the reviewer and keeps running; the message still lands in the `failed` queue, and `php bin/console messenger:failed:retry` runs it again. `php bin/console intakt:review` starts a round and waits for these workers (see [Reviewing a branch](#reviewing-a-branch)).

## Checking that an install boots

After `composer install`, `php bin/instance` starts Intakt on a local port with a scratch SQLite database, checks that it answers, stops it and removes everything it created. Without `TMPDIR` it keeps its scratch files in `var/`; a `var/` folder that the run created is removed again when it is empty, and one that existed before is left in place. If it cannot create its scratch folder, the reason names the cause, for example `cannot create the scratch directory: mkdir(): Permission denied`, or `unknown reason` when the system gave none. It needs no network. Descriptor 3 receives one line, `{"status":"ready"}` or `{"status":"failed","reason":"..."}`, for example:

```
php bin/instance 3>&1 >/dev/null
```

The exit code is 0 only when the status is `ready`. The server log goes to standard output.

A failed boot's reason says what went wrong (`the server exited:`, `HTTP <code>:` for an answer of 500 or more, or `no answer within 30 seconds:`) followed by the first line of the server log that mentions an error or exception. When no line does, it is the last line of the log that is not empty, so a server that the system stops, for example with a bare `Out of memory`, reads `the server exited: Out of memory`. The built-in server's own lines (its start line and the lines it writes for each request) are skipped there, since they only restate the status code; when nothing else is in the log, the reason reads, for example, `HTTP 500: the server log names no reason`. A server that exits without writing anything reads `the server exited: the server log is empty`.

When `frankenphp` is on your `PATH` (as in the container image), `bin/instance` serves the app with FrankenPHP in worker mode instead of PHP's built-in web server, and the line says so: `{"status":"ready","mode":"worker"}` or `{"status":"failed","mode":"worker","reason":"..."}`. A line without `mode` means the built-in server.

Descriptor 3 can be a pipe, a file or a memfd (an anonymous in-memory file, as a supervising program may pass). A memfd named exactly `opcache_lock` is not accepted, because PHP's OPcache uses that name for its own lock; any other name works. When descriptor 3 is not open, or is not accepted, no line is written and the exit code is 1.

Intakt can boot a commit of a project's local repository the same way: in a scratch copy of the commit it runs `composer install` (without the commit's Composer scripts) and then `bin/instance`, taken from main for a pull request's head, so a head that changes `bin/instance` cannot pass its own check. Both get only basic environment variables (such as `PATH` and the proxy settings) and an empty scratch home instead of the install's `HOME` and Composer home, so neither Intakt's own keys and tokens nor the Composer `auth.json` of the install are handed to them. This is not a sandbox: the commit's code still runs as the same user on the same machine and can read its files on purpose; isolating it is a later part ([Boot step](../architecture/boot-step.md)). The result is booted, failed (with the reason `bin/instance` or Composer gave, or, when `composer install` passes its limit of 600 seconds or `bin/instance` its limit of 180 seconds, Intakt's own reason naming the step and the limit, because a head that hangs counts as a head that does not boot) or crashed (the boot step itself could not run, for example because the commit is missing); a crash never stops the review. Main's result is kept per project, main commit and tool fingerprint, so main is booted again only when its commit or the tools change, and the ledger records `boot_result_computed` each time it is computed, and once more, marked `read_from: store`, when a kept booted or failed result is answered and differs from what the board shows for the project (a crashed boot is kept only for 5 minutes: within them main's crash is answered without booting again, and after them main is booted again). The watcher boots a pull request head whose CI is green before its review starts, but only when the pull request's author is trusted: a person with write access, Intakt's own identity or the maintainer's identity, and only when the head is a branch of the project's own repository. A head from a fork is never booted, also when a trusted author opened it and also when the fork was deleted, because the fork's owner can push new heads to it. Any other author's head (also when the trust check fails or cannot answer) is never booted either: its review still starts, and the ledger records `head_boot_skipped` with the cause `boot_skipped_untrusted_author` ([Boot step](../architecture/boot-step.md#in-the-watcher)). Be aware of the remaining risk: until reviews run in isolated job containers (a later part), a trusted author's head runs `composer install` (with the Composer plugins it allows) and `bin/instance` directly on your machine as Intakt's user, next to your install's home, keys and database. For a trusted author's head, a head goes back, like red CI, only when it fails while main boots: no review starts, the ledger records `head_red` with the cause `boot_failed` and the reason, and a review already made on that same head is withdrawn (its status on the forge becomes `failure`, see below), so an approved head that does not boot keeps no approval. When main does not boot (also when the project has no `bin/instance`) or the step crashes, the review goes ahead without the boot check, and when main itself fails to boot the ledger records one `main_boot_failed` alert for that main commit and tool fingerprint, so a tool change can raise another alert for the same commit (when the ledger cannot write it, or Intakt stops before it is written, the next time main's result is asked for, before a green head is reviewed, it tries again; a main whose failure was already kept before the alert existed gets it the next time main's result is asked for after `doctrine:migrations:migrate`) and the board shows a banner `Main of <project> does not boot` until a new main commit, or main after a tool change, boots (a crash raises no alert, because it is the boot step's fault and main is booted again after it), and a check that breaks never stops a review. The banner follows the current main: when main or the tools go back to a commit and tool pair whose result is already kept, the banner shows again if that pair failed and clears if it booted, while the alert stays one per pair (a pair whose alert was recorded raises none again).

## Walking the user journeys

`bin/check --journeys` checks the product as a whole: for each journey in `tests/Journeys/` it boots a fresh instance with `bin/instance`, walks one path a user takes through the pages and the console, and checks what the user sees. The full list, with what each journey checks, is in [Journeys](../architecture/journeys.md); the main ones follow. First install and setup: the database is created, the board says "Nothing is running yet. Start the first review.", the projects page is empty, the sample project is seeded (a second seed keeps it), and the reviews page is empty. Live review progress on the dashboard: the watcher starts a review round on a recorded pull request of the sample project, the review page shows it in progress, every live update the round sends fits that page until the judge has ruled, the board headline sent once a reviewer started names the change and links to that round's review page, the page shows it judged, the page opened while the round ran, with those updates applied, shows the judge's call, the findings, the round's tokens and cost and the review's totals as that fresh load does, without any text asking for a reload, and the board then says "Nothing is running. Nothing needs you." (whether the browser subscribes to those updates is checked by a separate test). First run of the demo without a key: the demo starts without a key, `/` sends you to the setup wizard's Ready step, which opens on the recorded review with no start button yet, and step 3 offers the key form. First run of the demo with a key: the same start, then a placeholder Hugging Face token pasted on step 3 is kept, a restart gives the sample project its reviewers, **Start the first review** opens the live review page with "Started round 1.", and once the round has run that page shows it judged with its outcome. Second start from the dashboard: once the first review is judged, **Start round 2** on its review page shows "Started round 2.", and pressing the same start again shows "Round 2 was asked for, but the next round of this change is round 3." and starts no further round. They need no network and no real keys. Each journey has up to 240 seconds, and anything it leaves running is stopped with its instance.

The option runs alone (`--fast`, `--only`, `--skip`, `--base` and `--test-merge` are refused with exit code 2), and neither `bin/check` nor `bin/check --fast` runs the journeys. A passing run ends with `{"result":"pass","failed":[],"report":"var/check/report-<16 hex digits>.json"}` (see [Reading the whole report of `bin/check`](#reading-the-whole-report-of-bincheck)); a failing journey is one finding that names its file, the journey, the step that broke and the first line of the reason, for example `FirstInstallJourneyTest.php: journey "first install and setup" failed at step "open the reviews page": Failed asserting that ...`. The plain-words guide is [Checking the whole product](../guide/checking-the-whole-product.md).

`bin/journey-alarm [--run=<https link>] [<file>]` reads that output (from the file, or from stdin) and, when journeys failed, files one task on the repository named by `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` through `gh` (labels `priority:urgent` and `ready`, the failing journeys, their steps and the last 40 lines of the output, and the run link when given). A later red run replaces the body of that task, found by the hidden line `<!-- intakt:journey-alarm -->` in it, instead of filing another. Two runs at the same time on one computer take turns, and a run that finds an older task filed meanwhile closes its own as a duplicate. A green run files nothing and exits 0; output with neither a failing journey nor a pass, or a failed journeys gate whose findings name no journey, exits 1 with a message, as does a failing `gh` call or a missing token; a file it cannot read, an unknown option or a `--run` that is not an https link exits 2. Set the token in `GH_TOKEN` or `GITHUB_TOKEN`: without one the command stops before calling `gh`, so a stored `gh` login is never used.

`bin/journey-alarm --timed-out [--run=<https link>] [<file>]` is for a run stopped at its time limit.
It skips parsing the output, so a cut-off or empty output files or updates the same task instead of exiting 1.
That task is titled `fix(journeys): the journeys run timed out on main` and says the run timed out instead of listing journeys.
Without `--timed-out`, the command behaves as described above.

`bin/journey-alarm --install-check=<report.json> [--run=<https link>]` reads the `report.json` of `bin/install-check` instead of journeys output; `bin/install-check` runs it itself when `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` is set ([Trying the install the way a stranger does](../user/08-when-something-goes-wrong.md#trying-the-install-the-way-a-stranger-does)).
It takes no other file and no `--timed-out`: with either, or a report file it cannot read, it exits 2; a report that is not a list of named steps with `passed`, `failed` or `skipped` exits 1, as does a failing `gh` call.
Each failing step gets its own urgent task, titled `fix(install): install check step "<step>" fails`, with the step and the last 40 lines of its log; a later failure of the same step rewrites that task, and a run where the step passes closes it, while a skipped step changes nothing.
An install command is known by its place in the README's install block, so fixing a broken command closes its task.

Intakt's CI runs the journeys on every push to main whose commit did not already pass as the head of a pull request into main with exactly the same files, and on a pull request that changes more than `docs/`, `tests/` or `upstream/` or touches the journeys' test code (`tests/Journeys`, `tests/Support`, `tests/Core/Watcher/RecordedPullRequests.php`), as the `journeys` job of the `ci` workflow (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). It also runs them on main every night and whenever someone starts the `journeys` workflow (`.github/workflows/journeys.yml`) by hand ("Run workflow", or `gh workflow run journeys.yml`). The two differ in what a failure does. When the `journeys` job of `ci.yml` fails, that pull request's or push's CI run fails, and no task is filed. Only a red nightly or hand-started `journeys.yml` run files one urgent task, or updates the one already open while the journeys stay red ([Running Intakt's CI on your own runner](self-hosted-ci.md)). The nightly run and its alarm stay for now; they are removed once the `journeys` job of `ci.yml` has been green on main for a week.

## Regenerating the journey page

`bin/journey` rewrites the "At a glance" table, the milestone table and the charts of `docs/journey.md` (the page that tells how Intakt was built) and leaves every other line of the page as it was. It draws five charts as SVG files in `docs/journey/` (tokens per day by lane and by account, tokens per merged pull request, issues created and closed per day, open issues per day) and puts each on the page with a table of the same numbers below it, folded away under the chart. Every figure it writes is followed by its kind, like the review report: the token figures `measured` (the providers' usage answers, the `tokens` field of `usage.jsonl`), the merges and issues `counted` (from the GitHub API), and the tokens reserved for steps that were killed before they reported usage `estimated` (the `reserved` field of `usage.jsonl`). Each chart names its kind in its title, in the picture's description and in the columns of its table, and a line under the drawing says where its numbers come from; the "At a glance" table and the milestone table (whose done and open issues are `counted`) end with such a line too. Tokens per merged pull request shows the measured tokens and the counted merges beside it, each with its own kind, and the estimated reserved tokens apart from them when there are any. The charts and every token total hold only measured tokens: a reservation is never added to them. The page shows no cost. It also draws the page's Mermaid timeline as `docs/journey/timeline.svg`, for GitHub Pages, which does not show Mermaid diagrams. Accounts appear only as Claude account 1, Claude account 2, Claude API key and GPT subscription. It reads the milestones, issues and pull requests of the repository named by `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` (`<owner>/<name>`) through `gh`, and the token counts in `docs/journey/usage.jsonl`, where every line holds both `tokens` and `reserved`. Set the token in `GH_TOKEN` or `GITHUB_TOKEN`: without one the command stops before calling `gh`. `bin/journey --recorded=<folder>` reads `milestones.json`, `issues.json` and `pulls.json` from that folder instead, with no network and no token. It prints `<file> regenerated` for each file it changed, or `docs/journey.md and its drawings are current` when nothing changed, and exits 0. The token charts change only when `usage.jsonl` does: the merges on its days are counted when it changes and kept on the page in a hidden comment below the tokens per merged pull request table, until it changes again. If that comment was edited so that it no longer reads as counts (a count of zero, for example), the merges are counted again and the comment is written anew. A missing marker pair, a page without exactly one Mermaid timeline, a timeline line it does not understand, a GitHub answer or usage line it does not accept (an unknown account, vendor or lane among them), a failing `gh` call or a missing token exits 1 with a message and writes nothing; a file it cannot write exits 1 before the page is written; an unknown option exits 2. The technical description is [The journey page](../architecture/journey-page.md).

`bin/pages <folder> [--recorded=<folder>]` builds the whole GitHub Pages site into a folder: it runs `bin/highlights` and `bin/journey` (passing `--recorded` on), then writes the highlights page as `highlights/index.md` with a link to the journey page, and the journey page as `journey/index.md` with its charts and the timeline drawing beside it, the timeline shown as that drawing because Pages does not show Mermaid diagrams. When `issue-states.json` in the `--recorded` folder supplies usable states (at least one issue number with the state `open` or `closed`), the highlights page shows a feature row written `in progress` or `done` as `done` when every issue and pull request its Where cell names is closed, as `in progress` when one is open, and says so in one sentence under the journey link; rows written `planned`, rows naming no issue, a row naming a number the file lacks (unless another is open) and the Found in the beta table keep their written status, and a missing, empty or invalid file keeps every status as written and omits that sentence. If either command fails, it exits 1 and writes nothing into the folder. It also copies the front page, `docs/pages/index.html`, and everything under `docs/pages/assets/` unchanged to the folder's root. It exits 1 and writes nothing when a source file cannot be read, naming the file. It also refuses a folder that holds an `index.md` at its root, left from the old layout: the front page replaces it, so remove it or build into a fresh folder. A rerun with the same inputs changes nothing and prints `site is current`. It never deletes a file: a drawing removed from `docs/journey/` stays in the folder's `journey/` when you build into the same folder again, so build into a fresh folder to leave it out. Apart from the folder, it changes only what the two commands regenerate under `docs/`.

`bin/journey-record <folder>` saves the milestones, issues and pull requests of the repository named by `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` through `gh` as `milestones.json`, `issues.json` and `pulls.json` in the folder, ready for `--recorded`. It is meant for a machine that has `gh` and the token, such as the CI runner. It writes the three files only once all three answers arrived: a failing `gh` or an empty `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` exits 1 and leaves no new file. Then it writes `issue-states.json`, the number and state of every recorded issue and pull request, with `jq`, for the highlights statuses of `bin/pages`; if `jq` fails it warns, leaves no `issue-states.json` and still exits 0, if a folder named `issue-states.json` is in the way it warns, leaves it as it is and still exits 0; and if an earlier `issue-states.json` file cannot be removed it exits 1, saying stale states would be published (the three other files are already written). It never prints the token.

## The log

Intakt writes its warnings and errors to the service output (the journal, or `docker logs`). `INTAKT_LOG_LEVEL` sets the lowest level written: `debug`, `info`, `notice`, `warning` (the default, set in `.env`), `error`, `critical`, `alert` or `emergency`, in lower case. Any other value, an empty one included, stops Intakt at start with "INTAKT_LOG_LEVEL is set to a value that is not a log level: use one of" and these levels; the message leaves the value out, so a key set there by mistake is not printed. The log is one JSON line per record, redacted as below at every level: `time`, `level`, `message` and `context`. The context names the item a failure belongs to (for example `reviewer`, `round`, `call`, `project` or `account`), and a failure carries the exception's `class`, `message`, the `at` line and the `trace` (the file, line and function of each call, never the values passed to it), with the earlier exception it wraps under `previous`. Before a line is written, the credentials Intakt holds, whether set in the environment or in Symfony secrets (`APP_SECRET`, `MERCURE_JWT_SECRET`, `MCP_OWNER_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `HF_TOKEN`, and the same names with an account name appended, such as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY_TEAM`), the userinfo of a URL (`https://user:pass@host` is written `https://[redacted]@host`), the values of authorization and cookie headers, and secret-shaped strings are replaced with `[redacted]`. A held credential is found with or without its leading and trailing spaces or newlines, so a value saved with a newline at its end is still replaced where a line quotes it without that newline. This applies to context names too; when two names end up the same, the later one gets a number (`[redacted] 2`), so no value is lost from the line.

## Finding the slowest tests

When the `tests` gate of `bin/check` runs, its line also lists the ten slowest test files under `slowest`, slowest first, each with its `file` and its `seconds` (the times of all its tests added up, over all test passes, including the run of the `serial` tests). For example:

```
{"gate":"tests","ok":true,"findings":[],"omitted":0,"slowest":[{"file":"tests/Application/InstanceTest.php","seconds":45.722},...]}
```

The time of every test file, not only the ten slowest, is kept in `tests/durations.json` in the gates cache: the folder `INTAKT_GATES_CACHE` names, or `intakt-gates` in the system temp folder when it is not set. Each run replaces the file. A test file outside the project folder is left out of both the file and the `slowest` list, so no path of your machine shows up in them. The times never change whether the gate passes; when the cache folder cannot be written, the file is simply not written. The gates cache also holds file selections: the `rector` gate's list of the files it checks, in `rector-paths/` (a relative `INTAKT_GATES_CACHE` is taken from the folder you run `bin/check` in); a run that cannot write that list fails the `rector` gate.

In CI, the `check` job restores the analyser caches (PHPStan, PHP CS Fixer and Rector) from the latest matching full run on main, so the analysers check only what changed since then. Only full runs on main save them (a push that skips the full run saves none); a pull request run uses them but never saves its own. A change to `composer.lock` or to anything under `config/gates/` starts from empty caches. The caches hold analysis results, test durations and the lists of files a Rector run checked, no secret and no token.

## The PHP profile and the environment gate

The profile of the PHP that builds, reviews and CI use is in the repository: `config.platform.php` and the `ext-*` entries under `require` in `composer.json`, and the ini settings in `config/gates/php/check.ini` (for example `zend.assertions=1`). Load the settings by adding `config/gates/php` to `PHP_INI_SCAN_DIR`, after the directories PHP already scans. A leading `:` keeps PHP's own directory first, so from the project folder:

```
PHP_INI_SCAN_DIR=":$(pwd)/config/gates/php" bin/check
```

`bin/check` runs the `environment` gate first in every run, the full one and `--fast` (not `--journeys`, which keeps its own selection). The gate (the `EnvironmentGate` class) checks the running PHP against that profile. `bin/check` neither restarts PHP nor changes its ini scan path, so a run without the profile loaded fails the `environment` gate as soon as your PHP differs from it. Each finding names one difference: a PHP older than the platform version, a missing extension, a setting that differs from the check ini (for example `PHP setting zend.assertions is -1 but config/gates/php/check.ini says 1`), or a setting in the check ini that the gate does not allow. When a setting differs, the gate also says how to load the profile.

## Running the tests in parallel

The `tests` gate runs the test files in several workers at once: one for each processor it may use, at most four, and no more than a CPU quota of its container allows (rounded up). Each worker takes the next file from one shared list, which starts with the files that have no recorded time yet (new ones), then the files that took longest in the last run (from `tests/durations.json` above). Set `INTAKT_TEST_WORKERS` to choose the number of workers yourself (from 1 to 127; any other value fails the gate); `INTAKT_TEST_WORKERS=1` runs each test pass in a single process, as before. A finding names the pass and the file it came from, for example `sqlite tests/Core/ReportTest.php: ReportTest::testRuns: ...`. A failing, skipped, risky or incomplete test, or a warning, notice or deprecation, fails the gate in any worker, as does a warning PHPUnit gives while it lists the test files (for example a test file without its class), and so does a file whose run crashes (`phpunit exited with code ...`) or takes longer than 600 seconds (`timed out after 600 seconds, last output: ...`). All files together get at most 3600 seconds: past that, the files still running are stopped and the ones not yet started never start, and each fails the gate with `timed out: the test pass reached its overall limit of 3600 seconds` (a stopped file also shows `, last output: ...` after it).

## Running the gates at the same time

`bin/check` runs the selected gates at the same time, as many at once as there are processors it may use (capped by a CPU quota of its container, rounded up), but fewer when its container has a memory limit (its own control group, each group above it and the top group it can see are all read): each test worker and each gate gets 2 GiB of it, the test workers first, because a gate such as `phpstan` may use that much (at least one gate runs). The gates that run your change's code (`tests`, `container`, `journeys`, and `phpstan`, which starts your change's kernel to dump the test container) start only after every other gate has finished, because a test could otherwise change the files or tools another gate is still checking. The other gates start first, in the usual gate order as earlier ones finish; then the `tests` gate starts first, so it never waits, and `phpstan` and `container` follow, in that order. Because the test workers need processors too, the `tests` gate counts for as many gates as it runs workers, but always leaves room for one other gate beside it. When the `tests` gate runs and more than one gate runs at once, the `phpstan` and `container` gates beside it run at the lowest processor priority, so they only use processor time the test workers leave idle; every other gate (and every gate with `--only=phpstan`, or `INTAKT_GATE_SLOTS=1`) keeps its usual priority. Set `INTAKT_GATE_SLOTS` to choose how many gates run at once (from 1 to 999); `INTAKT_GATE_SLOTS=1` runs them one after another. Any other value stops `bin/check` with exit code 2 before any gate starts. The lines, the result line and the exit code are the same however many run at once.

The `rector` gate starts at most 4 workers of its own, each allowed 300 s (`withParallel` in `config/gates/rector.php`), whatever the processor count. With Rector's defaults (a worker per core, 120 s), several `bin/check` runs at once on one machine starved each other's workers until they timed out, and the gate failed with `Child process timed out after 120 seconds` and no finding of its own. The gate hands Rector its files in a list file named by `INTAKT_RECTOR_PATHS_FILE`, not on the command line, because Rector's workers failed to start with `Argument list too long` once the list of every file passed 128 KiB. A list file that is missing or incomplete fails the gate; it never checks fewer files. To run Rector by hand, name the paths on its command line.

## Reading the whole report of `bin/check`

A gate's line shows at most its first ten findings, each cut to 300 characters. After the gate lines, `bin/check` writes everything into one report file and names it in its result line, so you never need to run the check again to see the rest:

```
{"result":"fail","failed":["dashes"],"report":"var/check/report-3f9c0a1b2d4e5f60.json"}
```

The file lies in the checked folder (`--root`, else the repository), under `var/check/`, with a new random name on every run, so an earlier report is never overwritten. It holds one JSON object: `gates` lists every gate's whole result in gate order (every finding, uncut). Each whole result is copied as the gate wrote it, with nothing decoded and written again. A gate whose whole result is not available is listed with its summary line instead; so is a gate that crashed, timed out or printed no valid line, so its line names why it stopped, even when it saved a result before that, and so is a gate whose saved result gives another verdict or other findings than its line, or is not written exactly as Intakt writes JSON (a key twice, extra spacing, an empty object `{}`, an object with numbered keys, a number too large to read), and `whole` is then `false` only if that line leaves something out (more than ten findings, or a finding cut or as long as the limit). Characters beyond ASCII are written as `\u` escapes. The file gets the permissions your umask gives. A `var/` or `var/check` that is a link is not followed: then there is no report. When no report can be written, the result line has no `report` key: when the system temp folder is missing or not writable (or lies inside the checked folder), so the gates cannot hand over their whole results, when `var/` or `var/check` is a link, the folder cannot be created or lies outside the checked folder, the file cannot be opened, or the write fails or stops short (the partly written file is removed). None of these prints a PHP warning between the JSON lines, and a scratch folder that cannot be removed afterwards is left in the system temp folder without changing the result. The report never changes the result, the `failed` list or the exit code. Intakt's own `.gitignore` leaves out `var/`. After writing its report, `bin/check` keeps only the 20 newest generated reports in `var/check/` (by modification time) and removes the older ones. The report it just wrote always stays, even when 19 others are dated in the future. Only files named exactly like the generated ones (`report-` plus 16 lowercase hex digits, `.json`, nothing more) are ever removed: a file you named yourself, such as `report-manual.json`, and links stay. When no report could be written, nothing is removed.

## Listing the static gates and the gate order

`bin/check --static-gates`, with no other argument, runs no gate. It prints one JSON line with two lists and exits with 0:

- `static`: the gates that do not run your change's code, so they can check a copy of the change on their own (every gate except `phpstan`, `tests` and `container`);
- `order`: every gate of a run without `--journeys`, in gate order (the order of the gate list in `bin/check`).

For example:

```
{"static":["cs","rector",...,"ratchet","workflows","left-undone"],"order":["cs","phpstan","rector",...,"container","ratchet","workflows","left-undone"]}
```

With any other argument beside it, `bin/check` runs no gate: it prints an `error` line with its usage and exits with 2. A normal `bin/check` run is unchanged: it still starts the static gates before the gates that run your change's code.

## When `bin/check` reports a missing tool

Before it runs any gate, `bin/check` checks that the programs the selected gates call are installed. When one is missing, it runs no gate, prints one line naming every missing program, such as `gitleaks not found, see docs/architecture/requirements.md`, and exits with 2. A Composer older than 2.11 is reported the same way, naming the version found, because it would ignore a release cooldown set in `composer.json`. It also needs PHP's `pcntl` and `posix` extensions, so that a Ctrl-C reaches the gates' processes; a missing one is named the same way (`PHP extension pcntl not found, ...`). The same message appears when a PHP function they need is disabled (`disable_functions`), and PHP must be able to read the process table under `/proc` (not shut out by `open_basedir`), else each gate fails with `cannot run ...: the stop cannot read /proc/<pid>/stat`. Install the program or the extension (the [requirements](../architecture/requirements.md) name the version), point `INTAKT_GITLEAKS` at gitleaks if it is not on the path, or leave the gate that needs it out with `--skip=<gate>`. `bin/probe` reports a missing `unshare`, `sh` or `ip` the same way, with exit code 2, before it starts anything.

The `workflows` gate is the exception: it needs zizmor (the [requirements](../architecture/requirements.md) name the version), and without it only that gate fails, with `missing tool zizmor` in its own result line; `--skip=workflows` leaves it out.

`bin/check` looks for the programs on `PATH`. When `PATH` is not set at all, it looks where the system shell `/bin/sh` then looks, since the gates start their programs through that shell (on Debian and Ubuntu: `/usr/local/sbin`, `/usr/local/bin`, `/usr/sbin`, `/usr/bin`, `/sbin` and `/bin`); if the shell cannot run, it looks only in the project folder. A `PATH` that is set but empty (`PATH=`) is taken as it is: only the project folder is searched, so every other program is reported missing.

## When the `workflows` gate reports an ignore without a reason

A zizmor finding in a YAML file under `.github/` may only be silenced with a comment `# zizmor: ignore[<audit>]` followed by the reason on the same line, for example `# zizmor: ignore[artipacked] the job needs the token to push`. The `workflows` gate fails with `<file>:<line> zizmor ignore without a reason: # zizmor: ignore[...]` when the last ignore on a line has no words after it (blanks or punctuation do not count, and neither do words before it, for example in a quoted value that happens to contain such a comment), and with `<file> cannot be read for zizmor ignores` for a YAML file it cannot read. To resolve it, fix the finding the ignore silences, or write on the same line why the pattern is safe. A file with Windows (CR LF) or old Mac (CR) line endings is read line by line the same way.

## The release cooldown for dependencies

`composer.json` sets a release cooldown of 3 days: `composer update` leaves out a package version published less than 3 days ago and locks the newest older one.
