The `audit` gate of `bin/check` also fails when `composer.lock` holds a release inside that period. Wait until the release is 3 days old; do not shorten the period to make the gate pass. The only exceptions are one-time decisions of the owner: the `symfony/*` packages at version `8.2.0-BETA2` (released 2026-11-13) and `twig/twig` at version `3.31.1` (released 2026-11-24, the first release without the security advisories published that day) may be locked before they are 3 days old. They are written in `config.policy.cooldown.ignore` in `composer.json`, a passing `audit` gate names them in its note (`cooldown exceptions in force: symfony/* 8.2.0-BETA2, twig/twig 3.31.1`), and each is removed again once its release is older than 3 days. No other package has one. This needs Composer 2.11 or later (see above). Dependabot has its own cooldown, which only decides when its pull requests open: 7 days for the GitHub Actions the workflows use, 3 days for images and Composer packages. It also holds for the `FROM` images of `Dockerfile`, `.github/ci/Dockerfile` and `.github/ci/databases.Dockerfile` (the database images CI starts for the persistence tests), updated together in one pull request, with one exception: the images from GitHub's container registry, such as the gitleaks image. Dependabot cannot see there when a version was published, so an update of such an image can open with no wait.

The dashboard's JavaScript packages in `importmap.php` follow the same 3-day rule: `bin/console importmap:update` keeps a package at its pinned version while the newer one is less than 3 days old (the pin can also move to an older release that is old enough while the newest one is held back), and takes the newest version that is at least 3 days old once there is one. `bin/console importmap:outdated` lists a version it holds back in its "Withheld" column. Each module in the dashboard's import map also carries a sha384 integrity hash of the file Intakt serves, so the browser refuses to run a module whose bytes changed after the import map was computed (on a deployed install, after `asset-map:compile`). Both are set in `config/packages/asset_mapper.yaml`; do not shorten the age or remove the hash to get a fresh package in sooner.

## When `bin/check` reports a gate process that ended without a result line

`bin/check` runs each selected gate in a process of its own, several at the same time, and prints their lines in the usual gate order once all have finished, then the result line. A gate whose process crashes, runs longer than 3600 seconds, or prints anything but that gate's own result line fails with the finding `gate process ended with exit code <n> and no valid result line: ...`, followed by what the process wrote to its output and then to its error output; when that does not fit the finding's 300 characters, only its end is kept, after `...` (for a timeout, it ends with `... timed out after 3600 seconds`; for a process killed by a signal, with `... was killed by signal <n>`). When a gate's process ends or is stopped, or `bin/check` is interrupted (Ctrl-C), any program the gate started and left running is stopped with it. One exception: a program the gate started in a contained run of its own (such as a journey's instance) may keep running if the gate's process is killed outright (signal 9), because then it has no chance to stop it. Exit code 124 means it timed out, 255 usually a PHP fatal error, 128 plus the signal number a kill by that signal (137 for signal 9). Warnings and notices a gate's process gives appear on `bin/check`'s error output, not in its lines. To resolve it, read the quoted output, fix its cause, and run the gate alone with `bin/check --only=<gate>`.

## When `bin/check` reports a stateful service

Intakt keeps one process alive across many requests and messages, so a service that keeps a value in a property would carry it into the next request. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` therefore reports a finding `intakt.statefulService` for every concrete class under `src/` with a property which is not `readonly`, including properties from a parent class or a trait under `src/`, and static ones, because the container registers every class under `src/` as a service. A property a vendor base class declares, such as those of Symfony's `Command` or `AbstractController`, is framework wiring rather than request state and is not reported. A virtual property, a hooked property without a backing store such as one with only a `get` hook that computes its value from other properties, keeps no value and is not reported either; a hooked property with a backing store is. The finding names the property. To resolve it, do one of:

- make the property, or the whole class, `readonly`;
- implement `Symfony\Contracts\Service\ResetInterface` and clear the property in `reset()`, which Symfony calls between requests and messages;
- if the value must last the whole process on purpose (a notice logged once per start, for example), mark the property with `#[Intakt\Core\Platform\Contract\KeptForTheProcess]`; the marker exempts only the property it sits on, so a reviewer sees each such property, and an attribute of the same short name from another namespace does not count;
- if the class is a value object or a data transfer object rather than a service (a console input object for `#[MapInput]` included), mark it with Symfony's `#[Exclude]` attribute so the container does not register it. An `#[Argument]` or `#[Option]` property alone does not exempt a class, because the container still registers it.

Doctrine entities, embeddables, mapped superclasses and column types, and exceptions are not services and are never reported. Neither is the kernel, which performs the reset, nor any class outside `src/` (tests, gate code, scripts), which the container never registers.

## When `bin/check` reports a deprecated reference

Deprecated code is not used. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` already reports a call, a `new` or a signature type that uses a deprecated symbol, and reports a finding `intakt.deprecatedReference` for every place that names a deprecated class, interface or trait: a `use` import (also inside a group import such as `use Acme\{Legacy, Modern}`), an `instanceof`, a `catch` type, a `::class`, an attribute, a `new`, a type, and a type in any docblock tag (`@var`, `@param`, `@return`, `@property`, `@method`, a `@template` bound and the others) on a class, trait (also a trait no class uses, reported once from its own file), function, method, property, statement or closure, also inside a union such as `object|Legacy` or a constant type such as `Legacy::*`. Some places get both findings. A class is deprecated when it carries a `@deprecated` tag, or when its file calls the global `trigger_deprecation()` (in any letter case, also through a `use function` alias; a function of that name in the file's own namespace is not the global one) outside any class, function or closure (also inside an `if`), as some Symfony classes do to warn when they load; a first-class callable `trigger_deprecation(...)` is no call and deprecates nothing. The finding names the class. To resolve it, switch to the replacement the class names (its `@deprecated` text or its deprecation message says which); do not silence the finding. The `tests` gate also fails when a test (a gate test under `tests/Gates/` too), a helper under `tests/` or a class under `src/` causes a deprecation, including one that only loads a deprecated vendor class; only a deprecation that one vendor package causes by calling another is ignored.

## When `bin/check` reports a flag parameter

A boolean parameter that switches what a function does hides two behaviors behind one name, so the `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` reports a finding `intakt.flagParameter` for every parameter typed `bool`, `true` or `false` (also nullable or in a union) of a function, method, closure or constructor, except in `tests/`. The finding names the parameter. A `readonly` service is reported like any other. To resolve it, do one of:

- replace the boolean with a backed enum whose cases name the modes;
- split the method in two, one per behavior;
- if the boolean is data of a value object, make the class `readonly`, mark it with Symfony's `#[Exclude]` attribute (Doctrine entities, embeddables and mapped superclasses count as excluded too) and keep the boolean as it is, as a promoted constructor property or assigned unchanged to a property in the constructor body. Only such a constructor parameter passes, and only when the constructor body does nothing but assign variables to properties the class itself declares (as a property, a promoted constructor parameter or a property of a trait the class uses, since PHP counts a trait's property as declared by the class that uses the trait), one plain `$this->name = $variable;` per statement. An assignment to any other property (one the class never names, or one a parent class declares) is reported, since PHP hands an undeclared property or a parent's private one to `__set()`, which could read the boolean. A promoted boolean must appear nowhere in the body, and a stored one exactly once, as the variable of one of those assignments. Any other statement in the body (a condition, a call, `new`, `eval`, `include`, a `parent::__construct()` call) could read the boolean, so every boolean of that constructor is reported then, even in a value object, while the class's other methods may read the stored property, and the exemption follows the attribute, not the folder, so an unmarked `readonly` class under `bin/` or `config/gates/src/` is reported too.

## When `bin/check` reports the order of methods

A class reads from the top: what it offers first, then how it does it. Two checks keep that order.

- The `cs` gate reports `ordered_class_elements` for a class whose parts are out of this order: trait uses, enum cases, constants, properties, the constructor, public methods, protected methods, private methods. Methods of one visibility may stay in any order among themselves. To resolve it, run `vendor/bin/php-cs-fixer fix --config=config/gates/php-cs-fixer.php` on the file; it moves the parts and keeps each method's comments with it.
- The `phpstan` gate reports `intakt.helperOrder` for a private helper, in a class, an enum, an anonymous class or a trait, that sits above the method that first calls it (the topmost method that calls it, through `$this`, `self::` or `static::`, also as `$this->helper(...)` or inside a closure), or above another helper that this caller calls before it. A helper called from several methods sits below the first of them. A method calling itself does not count as its own caller. A private method that no public or protected method and no private constructor leads to starts its own group: nothing places it, but its own helpers are still checked. Property hooks, `[$this, 'helper']` callables and `$this::helper()` calls are not recognized, so a helper reached only that way counts as unreached and is never reported. The finding names the helper and where to move it, for example `Private helper framed() sits above render(), its first caller: move it below render().` To resolve it, move the helper as the message says.

## When `bin/check` reports a class that is not final

A class is closed for extension unless it is built to be extended, so the `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` reports a finding `intakt.finalClass` for every class declared neither `final` nor `abstract` in a file the gate analyzes under `src/`, `tests/`, `bin/` or `config/gates/src/`: the `.php` files there plus `bin/check`, `bin/instance` and `bin/probe`, but not the gate test fixtures under `tests/Gates/Fixtures/` or other files without an extension, such as `bin/console`. Services, `readonly` value objects, Doctrine entities and test classes are all reported alike. An abstract class is not reported, and neither is an anonymous class (`new class { ... }`), an interface, an enum or a trait. The finding names the class. To resolve it, add `final` to the declaration and keep everything else as it is (`final readonly class` stays readonly, a mutable service that implements `ResetInterface` becomes `final class`), or declare it `abstract` when it is meant to be extended.

## When `bin/check` reports a static property

Intakt keeps one process alive across many requests and messages, and a test run shares one process between many tests, so a value kept in a static property outlives the request or the test that set it. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` therefore reports a finding `intakt.noStaticProperty` for every static property declared in a class or a trait in a file the gate analyzes under `src/`, `tests/`, `bin/` or `config/gates/src/` (the same files as `intakt.finalClass` above), whatever its visibility, and one finding for each property when one declaration names several. Services, Doctrine entities and test classes are reported alike. Abstract classes and anonymous classes (`new class { ... }`) are exempt. Static methods, `static fn` closures, `static` variables inside a function and class constants are not static properties and are not reported. The finding names the class and the property. To resolve it, keep the value in an instance property, a service or a local variable; a test builds what it needs in `setUp()` and removes it in `tearDown()` instead of sharing it through `setUpBeforeClass()`.

## When `bin/check` reports a public setter

An object that can be changed after it is built can be seen half set up, and any caller can change it, so the `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` reports a finding `intakt.noPublicSetter` for every public method whose name is `set` or starts with `set` followed by an uppercase letter or an underscore (`setName()`, `set_name()`), in any letter case (`SetName()` too, since PHP matches method names case-insensitively), declared in a class, an interface, an enum or a trait in a file under `src/`, `tests/`, `bin/` or `config/gates/src/` (the same files as `intakt.finalClass` above). A trait's setter is reported once, in the trait. It is exempt only when at least one analyzed class uses the trait and the exemption below holds in every class that uses it (as `setUpBeforeClass()` of PHPUnit's `TestCase` in `tests/Gates/UsesSandboxes.php`); a trait no class uses has no exemption, and `#[Override]` makes no difference. This holds for an abstract trait setter too, which a vendor parent of the using class may implement. A class that renames a trait method to a public setter (`use Renames { rename as setName; }`), or makes a protected trait setter public (`use Titled { settitle as public; }`, judged by the name the class gets, `setTitle()`, whatever the letter case in the clause), is reported on that line, unless the class declares a method of the same name itself: PHP then uses the class's method, so that method is judged instead (a protected one passes, a public one is reported once). A trait that does so (`trait WrappedSetter { use Lookup { find as setLabel; } }`) is judged at the trait: the alias is reported once, under the trait's name, whether or not a class uses the trait, and without the exemption below; to override a vendor method through an alias, write the alias in the class that uses the trait, where the exemption applies. A `bin/check --fast` run skips the trait setters only when it analyzes fewer than all PHP files in scope, since the classes that use a trait may then be missing; a full `bin/check` and CI always judge them. Methods of an anonymous class declared inside a trait method are a known gap: the rule does not check them. Methods such as `settle()`, `setup()`, `settings()`, `Settings()` or `set2()` are not setters and are not reported, so a setter written all in lowercase (`setlabel()`) is not recognized either, and neither are private and protected setters. A setter that a vendor interface or parent class declares is exempt, since PHP does not let the class rename it: for example `setMarking()` of Symfony Workflow's `MarkingStoreInterface`, or an override of `setName()` of Symfony Console's `Command`. A setter that an interface or class of the repository itself declares is reported, both there and in every class that implements or extends it, so a subclass gets its own finding only when it redeclares the setter; a subclass that just inherits the method gets no extra finding. A parent or interface under `migrations/` grants no exemption either, so a class in the folders above that overrides its setter is reported, while the declaration under `migrations/` is outside the folders the rule checks and is not. The finding names the class and the method. To resolve it, pass the value to the constructor, or return a new instance from a `with*()` method (`clone()` with overrides).

## When `bin/check` reports a test method name

A test name that reads as a sentence says what behavior broke when it fails, so the `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` reports a finding `intakt.testMethodSentence` for every public method whose name starts with `test`, or that carries PHPUnit's `#[Test]` attribute, and that is not named `test` followed by at least three camel-case words, in a class under `tests/` that extends PHPUnit's `TestCase` (directly or through a parent, abstract classes included). `testFormIsSent()` passes; `testFormSent()`, `test_it_sends_mail()`, `testsendsmail()` and a `#[Test]` method named `works()` are reported. A run of capitals (`HTTP`) is one word, also when it starts with `A` or `I` (`testASCIIWorks()` and `testAURLWorks()` have two; write `testAUrlIsSent()`), and digits belong to the word before them (`testA96PxIconIsShown()` has five), so a name whose first word starts with a digit (`test2xxAnswersAreFine()`) is reported. Interfaces, anonymous classes and classes that do not extend `TestCase`, such as a double with `testConnection()`, are not judged. A data provider is exempt when a `#[DataProvider]` or `#[DataProviderExternal]` attribute names it on a test that a class PHPUnit runs (a concrete one) declares or inherits (an attribute on a method PHPUnit does not run as a test, such as a private helper, or on a parent's test that every concrete subclass overrides without it, does not count), so a subclass may implement `testCases()` for its abstract base, and a subclass's test may name a provider its parent class declares. A concrete class that runs the provider without naming it itself ends the exemption: its own tests must name it with `#[DataProvider]`, or a `#[DataProviderExternal]` must name exactly that class and the provider. PHPUnit also runs an exempt static provider whose name starts with `test` as a test; renaming it without that prefix avoids this. Only a static method without `#[Test]` that may return provider rows can be exempt: an instance method, a method that carries `#[Test]` and a static method returning `void` are judged as tests, since PHPUnit runs them as tests even when another method names them as a provider. When `bin/check` checks only the changed files, a static method that may return provider rows (one without `#[Test]` and not declared to return a type that cannot be iterated, such as `void`) is not reported, since the test that names it as a provider may not be among them; the full check judges it. A trait's method is judged in the test classes that use it and reported once, in the trait. To resolve a finding, rename the method to state the behavior, such as `testTheFormIsSent()`.

## When `bin/check` reports a magic method

PHP calls a magic method implicitly, so what it does (a property or method that exists only at run time, a copy, a serialization) is invisible to static analysis, the IDE and readers. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` therefore reports a finding `intakt.noMagicMethod` for every method whose name starts with `__` (PHP reserves those names for magic methods), such as `__get()`, `__set()`, `__call()`, `__clone()`, `__destruct()` or `__serialize()`, whatever its visibility and in any letter case (PHP matches method names case-insensitively), declared in a class, an interface, an enum or a trait in the same files as `intakt.finalClass` above. A trait's magic method is reported once, in the trait, whether or not a class uses it, and a class that renames a trait method to a magic method (`use Lookups { find as __call; }`) is reported on that line. A trait that does so (`trait WrappedLookup { use Lookup { find as __get; } }`) is judged at the trait: the alias is reported once, under the trait's name, whether or not a class uses the trait. Methods of an anonymous class declared inside a trait method are a known gap: the rule does not check them. `__construct()` and `__invoke()` are allowed, and so is `__toString()`, which implements PHP's own `Stringable` interface. The finding names the class and the method. To resolve it, declare the property or the method, or give the class a named method for the lookup.

## When `bin/check` reports a service locator

A class that pulls its services out of a container at run time hides what it depends on from its constructor, from static analysis and from the container's own checks. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` therefore reports a finding `intakt.noServiceLocator`, in a PHP file under `src/`, for every constructor parameter typed `Psr\Container\ContainerInterface`, Symfony's `ContainerInterface` or `ServiceLocator` (also nullable, in a union or an intersection, or under an import alias), including the constructor of an anonymous class or a trait and a trait method a class takes as its constructor (`use SomeTrait { initialize as __construct; }`), and for every `#[AutowireLocator]` attribute. Tests, scripts and gate code may use the container and are not judged. Reading a container constant, such as `ContainerInterface::IGNORE_ON_INVALID_REFERENCE` in an attribute, is fine. The finding names the class, the parameter and the type. To resolve it, inject each service the class needs through its constructor, or a tagged set of services as an `iterable` with `#[AutowireIterator]`.

## When `bin/check` reports a `#[Required]` attribute

A service whose dependencies are set after it is built can be used half wired, and its constructor no longer shows what it depends on, so Intakt uses constructor injection only. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` reports a finding `intakt.constructorInjectionOnly` for every Symfony `#[Required]` attribute (`Symfony\Contracts\Service\Attribute\Required`, also under an import alias), on a method or a property, in the same files as `intakt.finalClass` above. An attribute of another class that is also named `Required` is not reported. To resolve it, take the dependency as a constructor parameter.

## When `bin/check` reports a re-spelled name

A container service id, a parameter name or a provider name is written once, so that renaming it cannot leave a second spelling behind. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` therefore reports a finding `intakt.reSpelledName` for a string literal in a PHP file under `src/` or `config/` (but not `config/gates/`) that starts with `intakt.`, `%intakt.` (a parameter reference) or `@intakt.` (a service reference) outside a module's ids class (a class, not a trait, interface or enum, ending in `Services` in a `Contract` namespace, such as `AiServices`), and for a literal equal to a provider's value (`codex`, `anthropic` and the others) outside the `Provider` enum. Only a whole literal counts: a message that mentions `codex` is not reported. A double-quoted string with a variable in it, such as `"intakt.ai.platform.{$name}"`, is reported when it starts with one of these. Code in a trait is checked too, also when no class uses the trait, and so is code in an anonymous class (`new class { ... }`), which counts as part of the class it is written in. Tests, the gates' own code and the YAML of `config/intakt.yaml` are not checked. To resolve it, use the constant of the ids class (add one there for a new name), or derive the name from the case (`Provider::Codex->value`, or a `Provider` method such as `platformId()`).

## When `bin/check` reports a provider literal

A model id or a provider's host is written in one place, so that a retired model or a moved endpoint is one edit. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` therefore reports a finding `intakt.providerLiteral` for a string in a PHP file under `src/` that holds a model id (such as `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `gpt-6.1-terra`, `o4-mini`, `text-embedding-3-small`, `deepseek-chat` or `gemini-2.5-pro`, also inside a longer string such as a router id) or a provider host (`anthropic.com`, `claude.ai`, `claude.com`, `openai.com`, `chatgpt.com`, `huggingface.co` or a subdomain of one, also inside a URL), with the message `'claude-sonnet-5-5' names a model: name it in a provider descriptor of the catalog or in the demo data file, not in code.` Provider names and values such as `Anthropic`, `claude_code` or `codex` pass, and so does prose such as `o2 sensor` (a bare `o3` counts only where an id stands: as the whole string, in a router id such as `openai/o3`, `openai:o1` or `openai/o3:online` (also with text after it, as in `Use openai/o3 for this request.`), after `=` as in `--model=o3 --format=json`, or in quotes as in `{"model":"o3"}`). A class that implements `ProviderDescriptor` (the catalog in `src/Plugin/SymfonyAiVendor/Infrastructure/`) is not reported, and tests, `config/` and the demo data file `config/sample-project.yaml` are not checked. To resolve it, take the model from the project's tiers or the demo data file, or the host from the provider's descriptor. A few hosts of the Claude Code and Codex adapters are still listed in `ratchet/provider-literals.neon` until they move; that list may only get shorter (see [When `bin/check` reports a ratchet finding](#when-bincheck-reports-a-ratchet-finding)).

## When `bin/check` reports a bare serializer format

The JSON format of Symfony's serializer is named by its constant, so code never spells the format a second way. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` therefore reports a finding `intakt.bareSerializerFormat`, with the message `'json' as a serializer format is a magic string: pass JsonEncoder::FORMAT instead.`, when a PHP file passes the plain string `'json'` as the format to a serializer, normalizer, denormalizer, encoder or decoder of Symfony's Serializer component (`serialize()`, `deserialize()`, `normalize()`, `denormalize()`, `encode()`, `decode()`, their `supports...()` checks and `getSupportedTypes()`), also as a named argument (`format: 'json'`, or the name a class of your own gives that parameter), inside an unpacked array (`...[$data, 'json']`, read the way PHP passes it, so an entry a later key overwrites does not count) and in a call to the parent class (`parent::serialize($data, 'json')`). Only the format argument counts: stored JSON, a `'json'` array key or a method of another class with the same name pass. To resolve it, pass `JsonEncoder::FORMAT` (from `Symfony\Component\Serializer\Encoder\JsonEncoder`).

## When `bin/check` reports a highlights finding

`docs/highlights.md` lists the Symfony and Symfony AI features Intakt uses, and the `highlights` gate of `bin/check` checks its tables. The page is not in git and not edited by hand: `bin/highlights` builds it from `docs/highlights.d/` (run it to read the page locally), where `layout.md` holds the intro, the headings and the table headers, and each change adds one fragment named after its issue, such as `docs/highlights.d/1045-highlight-fragments.md`. A fragment holds only rows, each under the `## <section>` heading of its section in `layout.md`: table rows starting with `|`, or list items starting with `- ` in "PHP 8.5 in use". To change a row, add a fragment with the whole new row; it replaces the earlier row with the same first cell (or, for a list item, the same text before its closing status parenthesis such as `(done, T6)`, so an item can change its status) at its place, because fragments apply in the order they were merged, whatever their issue numbers: the order git added them to the branch, a fragment you have not committed yet last, and fragments added in the same commit (or outside git) in the natural order of their numbers (fragment 10 after fragment 2). The gate checks the page as `bin/highlights` would build it, so two changes never touch the same file, and a copy on disk that differs from that build fails. The order needs the full git history: in a shallow clone the gate fails until you run `git fetch --unshallow`. Each finding names the line: a fragment finding the line of the fragment, a table finding the line of the built page. To resolve it:

- **docs/highlights.md is committed:** the page was added to git (for example after a hand edit); run `git rm --cached docs/highlights.md` and put the change in a fragment;
- **docs/highlights.md is not the assembly of docs/highlights.d/:** your local copy was edited by hand or is older than the fragments; put any change in a fragment and run `bin/highlights`;
- **the history of docs/highlights.d/ is shallow:** run `git fetch --unshallow`;
- **cannot write docs/highlights.md** (from `bin/highlights`, which then exits with status 1): something else is in the way, such as a folder of that name or a read-only file; remove it or make it writable and run `bin/highlights` again;
- **a fragment is named `<issue>-<slug>.md`:** rename the file to its issue number, a hyphen and a slug in lowercase letters, digits and hyphens;
- **section is not a section of `layout.md`, or the line is outside a section:** put the rows under a `## ` heading spelled exactly as in `layout.md`; a new section is added to `layout.md` first;
- **section holds table rows (or list items), or the table header is in `layout.md`:** write only the rows of the section's kind; the header and its `---` line stay in `layout.md`;
- **row appears twice in this fragment:** keep one row per first cell (one list item per text before its status) in a fragment;
- **status is not one of planned, in progress, done:** write exactly one of those three words in the Status column, and move any remark (such as "optional") to another column;
- **Where cites a pull request:** name the task and its issue instead (`T9 (#14)`); a pull request number counts in every form (`PR #12`, `PR: #12`, `PR (#12)`, `PR12`, `pull request 12`, `pull/12`), while text inside backticks, such as a path `upstream/pr1-repro/`, does not (a span opened with two backticks ends only at the next two, so it may hold a single backtick, and a backtick written with a backslash before it, `` \` ``, is plain text that starts no span);
- **Where names a path which does not exist on the branch:** fix the path, add the file to git, or remove the backticks when the text is no path. Anything in backticks with a `/`, or ending in an extension like `.php` or `.md`, counts as a path and must be tracked by git; package names and commands with a `/` therefore go without backticks. A `|` in a path is written `\|` inside the table, and the gate checks it as `|`;
- **row is planned, but every path its Where names exists:** the change that built the row's feature left its status behind; set the Status to `in progress` (an open pull request) or `done` (on main). A planned row that names no path, or a path not built yet, gets no such finding;
- **Where cannot be checked, shorten the cell:** the cell is too long for the gate to read (usually a stray backtick before a very long text); close or remove the backtick, or split the text.

Tables inside a fenced code block (three or more backticks or tildes, indented at most three spaces) are examples and are skipped. The block ends at a line holding only a fence of the same character, at least as long as the opening one; a fence line with text after it stays inside the block. A block that is never closed runs to the end of the file.

A fragment may hold `{{` or `{%`, in a code span for example: the published page shows them as written.

## When `bin/check` reports a reproductions finding

Every folder directly under `upstream/` is a reproduction of a likely library bug, and the `reproductions` gate of `bin/check` checks that each one is listed. A finding such as `upstream/messenger-lost-message/ has no row in "Found in the beta" of docs/highlights.md` names the folder. To resolve it, add a row to the "Found in the beta" table of `docs/highlights.md` (through a fragment under `## Found in the beta` in `docs/highlights.d/`; the gate reads the page built from the fragments, see the highlights finding above) that names the folder in its text, for example `` `upstream/messenger-lost-message/` `` in the Where column. Only a row of a table in that section counts (a table needs its header and its `---` line, not indented four or more spaces, and it ends at the first line that is blank, has no `|`, or starts a heading, a list item, a blockquote or an HTML comment, even when that line holds a `|`; only the cells under the header's columns count). A line without a `|` ends the table even where GitHub would still show it as a row: this is stricter than GitHub and can only make the gate fail, never pass wrongly. A mention in its prose, on a line that merely starts with `|`, under a heading or in a list right after the table, in another section or inside a fenced code block does not, and neither does a longer path such as `docs/upstream/messenger-lost-message/`. A file directly under `upstream/` (such as its `README.md`) needs no row. The gate reads the folders git tracks or would add (ignored files do not count), and the working tree when there is no git repository.

The same gate checks the reports. A finding such as `upstream/a-bug/REPORT.md is ready to file but has no "Checked" line` or `... is older than 14 days` means the last `Decision` or `Status` line of `## Upstream status` says `ready to file` without a dated `Checked` line. The gate reads such a line however it is written: with any list marker or none, with or without backticks, in any case, and with more text after the phrase. Check the report against upstream again, add or update `- **Checked:** YYYY-MM-DD ...` (commits or tags, duplicate search, preferred form and target branch; see `upstream/README.md`), or change the status. A finding `... has no "Pull requests and commits" line` means the section does not say which upstream pull requests and commits the check found: add `- **Pull requests and commits:** ...` naming them (a `#` number, a URL or a commit hash), or `none found` with the date of the search as `YYYY-MM-DD`. A line that starts with `none`, `nothing` or `no` always needs that date, even when it names the searched pull requests or a search URL. A line that names none of these and holds no date, such as `none`, `-` or `No pull requests or commits found.`, gets `... names no pull request (#n), URL or commit hash and no date of the check as YYYY-MM-DD`. A finding `... has no "## Upstream status" section` means a `ready to file` line stands in a report whose status heading is not written exactly that way: fix the heading. A report has only one such section (`... has more than one Upstream status section`), and its status is a list item at the top level of that section: a line that says `ready to file` in another section, in a quote or indented four or more spaces gets `... write the status as a list item in the Upstream status section`.

## When `bin/check` reports a container finding

The `container` gate of `bin/check` runs `bin/console lint:container` for the `dev`, `test` and `prod` environments, also with `--fast`. It catches a wrongly wired service before the first request does: a service argument of the wrong type, or a reference to a service or parameter that does not exist. A finding names the environment and Symfony's message, for example `prod: Invalid definition for service "App\Mailer": argument 1 of "App\Mailer::__construct()" accepts "App\Transport", "App\Clock" passed.` To resolve it, fix the service definition or the constructor type it names, then run `php bin/console lint:container --env=<environment>` to see it pass. Environment variables are not resolved, so a missing API key does not fail the gate.

The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` knows the service container too: before it analyzes the code it builds the `test` environment's container of the checked tree in that tree's own `var/cache/` folder, whatever `APP_CACHE_DIR` or `APP_BUILD_DIR` says (`php bin/console cache:warmup --env=test`, so a tree without `bin/console` fails the gate) and reads the service ids and parameter types from it. A `get()` with a service id the container does not define fails the gate, for example `src/Lookup.php:17 symfonyContainer.serviceNotFound Service "kernal" is not registered in the container.`, also when the id is picked from several fixed strings and only one is wrong, and so does a `get()` of a private service outside the test container, also when it is one of several fixed strings. A `has()` with such an id fails the same way (`intakt.containerUnknownHasId`), even when the id is picked from several fixed strings and only one is wrong, and so does a `getParameter()` with a parameter name the container does not define, for example `src/Settings.php:17 intakt.containerUnknownParameter Parameter "kernal.debug" is not defined in the container.` To resolve it, fix the id or name, or use the ids class constant that names it. A service locator's own keys and the parameter names asked of a container bag are not checked, as long as the locator is typed as `ServiceProviderInterface`: a locator typed as `Psr\Container\ContainerInterface` looks like the container itself, so its keys are checked as service ids. With `--fast`, a change to `config/`, `src/Kernel.php` or a `services.yaml` under `tests/` checks every PHP file, so removing a service in configuration alone still finds its callers. A test that sets a service during the test declares it `synthetic` in `tests/Support/services.yaml`; a test that boots a kernel with other settings reads that kernel's services through `tests/Support/RebuiltContainer`.

## When the `left-undone` gate reports a TODO comment, a skipped test or a not-implemented throw

The `left-undone` gate of `bin/check` (also with `--fast`) also fails when your change adds, under `tests/`, a `markTestSkipped()` or `markTestIncomplete()` call (in any letter case) without a message, a first-class callable of one such as `$this->markTestIncomplete(...)` (its later message cannot be checked, so call the method directly with its message), or a `#[Skip...]` or `#[Incomplete...]` attribute without one (also `#[\Vendor\SkipIf]`) (`tests/OrderTest.php:5: markTestSkipped call without a reason; ...`), or, under `src/`, a `throw new LogicException('Not implemented')`, a `throw new NotImplementedException()` or similar, also with the message as `message:` or built with `sprintf()` (`src/Order.php:5: not-implemented throw left in the change; ...`). To resolve it, give the skip a message naming the missing tool or outside condition on the same line as the call (`markTestSkipped('python3 is not on the PATH')`, also as `message: '...'` or `"needs $tool on the PATH"`), make the test run, or implement the code. A message of only escapes or nothing, such as `"\t"`, `'\n'` or `b''`, is no message. A not-implemented throw passes only with a comment on the same line that starts with `//` or `#`, then `TODO`, `FIXME` or `XXX` (any case), then `(#<issue>):` and a reason: `// TODO(#123): <reason>`, `# fixme(#123): <reason>` or `// XXX(#123): <reason>`. Reviewers judge whether the message names a real condition. The rest of this section describes the comment rule.

The gate fails when your change adds a PHP, Twig, JavaScript, shell or YAML comment that starts with `TODO`, `FIXME` or `XXX`, for example `src/Order.php:5: TODO comment left in the change; finish the work, or open an issue and write TODO(#<issue>): <reason>` or `templates/order.html.twig:3: TODO comment left in the change; ...` for `{# TODO: show the total #}`. In a JavaScript file (`.js`, `.mjs` or `.cjs`), a `// TODO` line comment and a `/* FIXME */` block comment fail (`assets/app.js:3: TODO comment left in the change; ...`), also after code on the same line and also when a no-break space or another Unicode space follows the `//` or `/*`, while the same text in a string, a template literal such as `` `// TODO` `` or a regular expression such as `/TODO/` passes. Telling a regular expression from a division is a guess with [known limits](../architecture/quality-gates.md#quality-gates) (see the `left-undone` row). In a shell script (`.sh`), a `# TODO` comment fails (`docker/fetch.sh:3: TODO comment left in the change; ...`), also after a command on the same line, while the same text in a quoted string such as `echo '# TODO'` passes, and so does a `#` that does not start a word, such as in `${#list}` or `${path#prefix}`. A `# TODO` line inside a here-document body (`cat <<EOF` up to `EOF`) is text and passes, while a comment after the body fails, and so does a comment inside a `$( )` substitution or after a function such as `f() { (( x <<= 1 )); }`, whose `<<` shifts. The shell scanner has [known limits](../architecture/quality-gates.md#quality-gates) too: backquotes and a few rare forms, such as a `case` right after `time -p`, are not fully read yet. In a YAML file (`.yml` or `.yaml`), a `# TODO` comment fails (`config/packages/shop.yaml:3: TODO comment left in the change; ...`), also after a value on the same line and on the header line of a block scalar (`run: | # TODO`), while the same text in a quoted value such as `title: '# TODO'`, a `#` joined to the text before it such as `url: a#TODO`, and a line in the body of a block scalar (the lines indented under `run: |`) pass. The YAML scanner has [known limits](../architecture/quality-gates.md#quality-gates) as well, such as a block scalar right after `---`. In a template, a Twig comment is `{# ... #}` (also `{#- ... -#}` and over several lines), a documentation comment that opens with `{##` and closes with `##}` or `#}` (`{## TODO explain the total #}` fails), or a `#` or `##` comment inside a `{{ }}` or `{% %}` tag (`{{ total ## TODO round it` fails too); plain template text, an HTML comment `<!-- TODO -->`, a string such as `{{ 'TODO' }}` and a `{% verbatim %}` block are not Twig comments and pass. An HTML comment does not hide a Twig comment inside it: `<!-- {# TODO finish this #} -->` fails. A marker behind nested comment syntax counts too: `// // TODO later`, `# # FIXME the total` and `/* // XXX breaks on empty orders */` all fail, while `// // TODO(#12): drop once the forge sends the total` passes like the form below. In a Twig comment, PHP or shell comment syntax is text, not Twig syntax, so `{# // TODO is the PHP syntax #}` passes, and so does `{# # TODO is shell syntax #}`: a hash after `{# ` and a space is text, not the `{##` opener; only a `*` followed by whitespace at the start of any Twig comment line is skipped, so a line ` * FIXME the total` inside a star-framed multi-line `{# ... #}` fails, and so do a one-line `{# * TODO show the total #}` and an inline `{{ total # * TODO round it` (a `#` comment runs to the end of its line, so the closing `}}` goes on the next line); a star right before the word is Markdown emphasis, so `{# *TODO* is shown in italics #}` passes. It reports only the lines your change adds against the target branch (committed or not, and every line of a new file git does not track yet); it reads each changed PHP, Twig, JavaScript, shell or YAML file whole, to tell a comment from a string, but only an added line can fail. So a comment that was already there, or that only moved with a renamed file, is not reported. The same text inside a string is not a comment and passes. Markdown, files other than PHP, Twig, JavaScript, shell (`.sh`) and YAML (`.yml`, `.yaml`), `tests/Fixtures/`, `fixtures/`, `tests/Gates/Fixtures/`, `upstream/`, `vendor/`, `var/` and `node_modules/` are not read. To resolve it, finish the work, or open an issue and name it with a reason on the same line: `// TODO(#123): drop once the forge sends the total` passes this gate (it checks the form, not that the issue is open), and so does `{# TODO(#123): show the total once the forge sends it #}` in a template. The `comments` gate accepts the same form when the comment also gives a reason, with a word such as *because* or *needs*: `// TODO(#123): drop once the forge sends the total, because it is computed twice` gets a change through `bin/check`. Without a target branch the gate fails with `no base branch found, pass --base=<ref>`; a folder without git history skips it.

## When the `phpstan` gate reports a prompt message finding

Every message Intakt sends to a model is built by one class, `Intakt\Core\Review\Contract\PromptAssembly`, so no text from outside reaches a model unfenced. The `phpstan` gate of `bin/check` (also with `--fast`) fails when code under `src/`, `bin/` or `config/` builds a model message anywhere else: a call of `Message::ofUser()`, `Message::forSystem()` or `Message::ofAssistant()`, or `new UserMessage(...)` or `new SystemMessage(...)`. It also catches the less direct forms: a first-class callable such as `Message::ofUser(...)`, a call through an object or a class-name string (`$message::ofUser()`, `new $class(...)` with `$class` typed `class-string<SystemMessage>`) and a method name given as a known string (`Message::{'ofUser'}()`). It also refuses a literal callable of a factory: an array with exactly the keys `0` and `1` in any written order, `Message` at `0` and at `1` either a factory name or a method name known only at run time that may be a string (`[Message::class, 'ofUser']`, `[1 => 'ofUser', 0 => Message::class]`, `[Message::class, $method]` with `$method` typed `string` or `?string`; a known name that is not a factory passes), and a callable string such as `'Symfony\AI\Platform\Message\Message::ofUser'`; an array with other keys, such as `['class' => Message::class, 'method' => 'ofUser']`, passes. Code in a trait is judged by the file the trait is written in, so a trait under `src/` is judged even when only a class under `tests/` uses it. The finding names the call, for example `src/Sample/Report.php:13 intakt.rawPromptMessage Message::ofUser() builds a model message outside PromptAssembly: build it through Intakt\Core\Review\Contract\PromptAssembly, with Intakt's own text as a literal and untrusted text as a FencedText from Fence.` To resolve it, call a method of `PromptAssembly` instead: write Intakt's own instructions as literal strings, and wrap any text from a model, a forge or a user with `Fence::wrap()` (or `PromptCut::fenced()`) and pass the resulting `FencedText`. Code under `tests/` is not checked.

A second finding, `intakt.unprovenRolePrompt`, appears only in `ReviewWithReviewerHandler` and `SymfonyAiVendor`. In the handler it means a `new ReviewerConversation(...)` takes a role prompt that is not `$reviewer->rolePrompt()` of the round's `RoundReviewer`. To resolve it, pass that call as the role prompt (first argument, or the argument named `rolePrompt`), not a string, and unpack no argument in that call (not even after the role prompt), because the role prompt is the one text that enters the system message verbatim. In the vendor, whose retries start a new conversation, it means the role prompt is not copied from the stopped conversation: pass `$stopped->rolePrompt` of an existing `ReviewerConversation`.

Two more findings limit where the objects that carry a role prompt are built, because a role prompt must come from the owner's panel configuration or from the fixed roles of the sample project. `intakt.reviewerRole` means a `new ReviewerRole(...)` outside `ConfiguredPanels` and `SampleProject`, or a `new RoundReviewer(...)` outside `ReviewRound`. `intakt.reviewerConversation` means a `new ReviewerConversation(...)` outside `ReviewWithReviewerHandler` and `SymfonyAiVendor`. They also catch an aliased import, a class-name string and another letter case, and a class name that may stand for two classes is checked for both. A class string typed with an interface or parent class of one of these objects, such as `class-string<HasPlace>`, counts as naming that object; one typed with several (`class-string<HasPlace&Countable>`) counts only when the object has every one of them. Code in a trait counts as outside, also when an allowed class uses it or no class uses it. A trait that a class uses may build an object whose class sits in a variable only when the check can tell every class the variable may hold and none of them is one of these three (for example `$wrapped ? ArrayObject::class : stdClass::class`); a plain string is refused. A trait no class uses may build an object only of a class it names in the code, or an anonymous class (`new class () {}`, accepted in any trait); `new self`, `new static` and a class in a variable are refused there, except `new self`, `new static` or `new parent` (also written as `new (self::class)()`, `new (static::class)()` or `new (parent::class)()`) inside such an anonymous class, which builds that anonymous class (or the parent it names) and passes. To resolve one, do not build the object there: take the role from the panel configuration, let `ReviewRound` add the reviewer, or pass the existing conversation along. The finding names the classes that may build the object. Code under `tests/` is not checked.

## When `bin/check` reports a ratchet finding

A new PHPStan rule can be switched on before the code is clean: the violations it finds that day go into a ratchet list, one file per rule outside `config/gates/`, included from `config/gates/phpstan.neon`. The `ratchet` gate of `bin/check` (also with `--fast`) makes sure such a list only gets shorter. It compares each list with the list on the target branch and fails when an entry was added, a `count` went up, or an entry's identifier, message pattern or path changed, for example `ratchet/loose-conditions.neon: count rose from 2 to 3: [...]`. To resolve it, fix the new violation in the code instead of listing it; deleting entries and lowering counts always pass. A list also needs a line `# Cleanup issue: #<number>` in its header, may hold nothing but `parameters:` with `ignoreErrors:` (`ignoreErrors: []` once its last entry is gone), and arrives only in the change that adds its include. A violation that is not on the list, or a list entry that no longer matches anything (for example after a fix or after moving the file), fails the `phpstan` gate: delete the entry in the same change as the fix. A tree without any list passes with the note `no ratchet lists`. The gate reads the includes of `config/gates/phpstan.neon` only as plain `- path` lines under an unindented `includes:` key (the lines may be indented or not); any other form, such as `includes: [...]` or a configuration file that is indented as a whole, fails with `cannot read the includes` rather than overlooking a list.

## GitHub's test merge and the `commits` gate

On a pull request, GitHub checks out a merge of the branch into the base branch, with a subject such as `Merge <sha> into <sha>`, which is not a Conventional Commit. CI names that merge and the pull request's head with `--test-merge=<merge>:<head>`, and the `commits` gate skips it when its second parent is the pull request's head and its first parent is on the base branch, also when the base branch moved after the pull request's last update. Every other commit, merges included, still needs a conforming subject.

## Running a probe against an install

`php bin/probe tests/Probe/NAME.php` boots the same scratch instance and runs one small PHPUnit test (a name ending in `Test.php`) or PHP script against it, with the network cut, so anything that reaches for the network fails. The script or test finds the instance's address in the environment variable `INTAKT_INSTANCE_URL`. Its output is printed, followed by a line `exit code: N`, and the command exits with that code. A probe still running after 60 seconds is stopped with exit code 124. The instance, its files and anything the probe started are removed afterwards. It needs Linux with `unshare` and `ip`, which cut the network for the run.

The instance and the probe do not inherit your whole environment, so a token you have set in your shell is not visible to them. They get only `PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `LC_CTYPE`, `TZ` and `TMPDIR` (when you set them), the application variables `bin/instance` sets itself (`APP_*` and `DATABASE_URL`) and `INTAKT_INSTANCE_URL`; the server also gets `FRANKENPHP_ADMIN_URL`, never your value: in worker mode its own admin address, with the built-in server `http://admin-api.invalid`, which the board refuses to contact, so the board never asks an admin API on `localhost:2019`; in worker mode also `INTAKT_INSTANCE_ADMIN_URL` (FrankenPHP's admin API) for the probe, and `XDG_DATA_HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (inside the scratch folder) for FrankenPHP. `PWD` is also present: the `sh -c` wrapper in `bin/instance` sets it, it is not taken from your environment. `APP_ENV` and `APP_SECRET` keep your value when it is set and not empty or `0`.

A caller that already runs `bin/probe` without network access and in its own process namespace (as the build loop's sandbox does) sets the environment variable `PROBE_ISOLATED=1`. `bin/probe` then starts the instance directly and needs neither `unshare` nor `ip`, but first checks that the run really is offline: when `/proc/net/dev` cannot be read or lists something unexpected, when it shows a network interface other than `lo`, or when `lo` is down, it prints the reason and exits with code 2 before anything starts. The caller is responsible for ending whatever the probe leaves running. Any other value, or no value, keeps the `unshare` way.

## Configuring providers and API keys

Intakt knows five providers: `anthropic`, `openai`, `openai_compatible` (any endpoint that speaks the OpenAI API), `claude_code` (a reviewer or the judge on a Claude subscription, see below) and `codex` (a reviewer on a Codex subscription, which uses a login file instead of a variable, see [A Codex subscription login](#a-codex-subscription-login)). Each of the first four reads its key from Symfony secrets (`php bin/console secrets:set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) or from the environment (`.env.local` or the real environment), never from a plain committed file; a non-empty environment value wins over the secret:

| Provider | Variables it needs |
|---|---|
| `anthropic` | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` |
| `openai` | `OPENAI_API_KEY` |
| `openai_compatible` | `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_BASE_URL` and `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_API_KEY` (for Hugging Face, see [Running the demo on a Hugging Face token](#running-the-demo-on-a-hugging-face-token)) |
| `claude_code` | `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` |

A provider whose variables are not all set (empty counts as not set, and so does a secret Intakt cannot decrypt when the container is built), neither in the environment nor in the secrets, is switched off, and Intakt still starts. A provider with several accounts (see below) stays on as long as one of its accounts is fully set. `php bin/console intakt:ai:tiers` and `php bin/console intakt:vendor:probe` print a line on standard error for every switched-off provider, and the demo's first start prints them once in its log, not again on a restart with the same volume (when printing them fails, the start goes on with a one-line note and the next start tries again; other commands print none), for example `Provider "openai" is disabled: set OPENAI_API_KEY to enable it.` The same text is in the container parameter `intakt.ai.disabled_providers`. `php bin/console debug:config ai` lists the providers that are on, except `claude_code`, which is not part of the AI Bundle's configuration: `php bin/console debug:container intakt.ai.platform.claude_code` finds that service only when it is on. Whether a key is set is decided when the container is built, so after you add or remove a key, run `php bin/console cache:clear`. Without an enabled OpenAI account, project memory has no embedding model: reviews go on without memory, and the log says so once per start at `info` level, without a stack trace (the default log level, `warning`, hides it; `INTAKT_LOG_LEVEL=info` shows it). Resolved findings are then not remembered either: each review round that resolves findings logs the same `info` line once, and nothing is retried or kept as a failed message.

### More than one account of a provider

A provider can have several accounts, for example a personal and a team API key. List them under `accounts:` in `config/intakt.yaml`, in your order of preference (see `config/intakt.example.yaml`):

```yaml
accounts:
    anthropic: [default, team]
```

Before each model call Intakt ranks the provider's accounts by measured room: an account at its five-hour or weekly ceiling (or cooling down) is left out, the others rank by the window that resets soonest, an account whose last reading is older than 30 minutes or missing after every account with a recent reading, and a paid account (an API key) after every subscription account; your order breaks a tie. When every account is left out, no call is sent and the reviewer waits for the earliest reset (at most one day). A call refused after it started still moves on to the next ranked account.

The account `default` reads the variables in the table above. Any other account reads the same variables with its name in capitals appended: `team` reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY_TEAM` (for `openai_compatible`, `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_BASE_URL_TEAM` and `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_API_KEY_TEAM`; for `claude_code`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN_TEAM`, with its own folder `var/claude-code-team/`). A name starts with a lowercase letter and holds only lowercase letters, digits and underscores, and is listed once; anything else stops Intakt from starting. A provider you do not list has its `default` account only. An account whose variables are not all set is switched off with a line such as `Account "team" of provider "anthropic" is disabled: set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY_TEAM to enable it.`; the provider is switched off only when none of its accounts is set.

Intakt can tell the kind of a key from its form: `sk-ant-` is an Anthropic key, any other `sk-` an OpenAI key and `hf_` a Hugging Face token; any other form, including an Anthropic key cut off too short, is refused without echoing it, and a key that is shown as configured shows at most its last four characters. Intakt can also check a key with one small call to its provider (a one-word question with an answer of at most a few words: one token, or 16 for OpenAI, the least it accepts): a key the provider turns down is refused, and a key whose provider could not be reached is not kept as checked. The call and its refusals never print the key. Typing a key in the browser and keeping it are planned and not built yet: keep using the variables above.

When a provider limits a call (a rate limit or quota) or fails it with a temporary error, the account that made the call **cools down** until the reset time the provider sent, at most one day and at least one second (a reset of zero or already past waits one second). When the provider sent none, the account cools down for one minute. The one exception is a subscription account (`claude_code` or `codex`) whose plan limit came without a reset time, as with a Codex plan whose usage limit is reached: it cools down until the latest reset of a usage window Intakt last measured as used up (100 percent or more) for that account, and for one minute when no such reading has a reset still ahead or the readings cannot be read. Today nothing outside the demo's replay records these readings, so on a real install that cooldown is one minute too. Every reviewer on that account waits out the same cooldown, also in other workers on this computer, and the reviewer starts again at once on the next free account of the same provider: it stays the same reviewer, so no catch-up review is owed. Only when no account of the provider can serve the model right now (each one either cooling down from its own limit or with the router provider the model names paused on it) does the reviewer wait, until the first account is free again; this holds for a temporary error too, so such a reviewer is not marked failed. A temporary error or a limit that arrives after a reviewer's streamed answer has started (its first text, thinking or tool call, not only the token counts a provider sends first) is different: the answer cannot be resumed and the account is cooling down, so the reviewer is not asked again and ends `blocked` with the finding `incomplete-stream` and the reason `The provider failed after the answer had started streaming.`, and the tokens the call spent are counted once. Every `incomplete-stream` finding carries evidence for finding out why: the provider and model of the call, what cut it (`platform` when the provider's stream ended early, `provider-failure-mid-stream` for such an error, `unfinished-answer` when the stream ended normally without a whole verdict), how many pieces the stream had sent and the last five of them (the first 80 characters of each piece of text). That failed answer still cools its account down, like any other account error: a limit, a rejected key or a temporary error that arrives while the answer is being read, before or after it has started, keeps the next review off that account (a rejected key cools it for a minute and counts as a rejection). A failed account is logged (`Account claude_code.default failed.`) with its name and the kind of error, never with the provider's error text, which can repeat a key. Only a limit, a rejected key or login, or a temporary error of the provider counts as an account error. An answer Intakt could not read (for example a reviewer's answer without one readable verdict) is not one: the account answered, so it is not logged as failed, does not cool down and takes the next call. Any other failure that stops a call from being sent is logged as `Account claude_code.default could not send the call.`, with the same redaction, and does not cool the account down. Each cooldown is recorded in the ledger as an `account_cooled_down` event with the account (`anthropic.team`) and its end, and each usage row names the account the call ran on. A rejected key or login is an account error only you can fix, so it is also reported to you once, as an `account_error_reported` event in the ledger with the account and the moment: the same error is not reported again while it lasts, however often the account is tried after its cooldown. Once the account answers a call sent after that report, its next error is reported again; an answer to a call sent before the report does not count. Cooldowns and these reports are kept in Symfony's cache, so `php bin/console cache:clear` ends them (an account error that lasts is then reported once more).

A key the provider rejects (revoked or wrong) puts its account into cooldown for a minute like a limit, so the call moves on to the next account of the provider; the reviewer ends `failed` when every account of the provider rejected its key (now or in a cooldown still running, also for a review that starts during that cooldown, without sending a call), and waits when another account is only cooling down from a limit.

**Substitutes.** A tier can list `substitutes` (a provider and a model each, see the commented line in `config/intakt.example.yaml`). Only when the tier's provider cannot answer (every account is cooling down or has paused the router provider the model names, every key was rejected, or the provider is disabled because its settings are not set) does a review go to the first substitute that can, in the order you listed. Only the first model you list on a provider counts for it: a substitute on the tier's own provider, or a second one on a provider listed before, is skipped. A tier without `substitutes` never leaves its provider: its reviewers wait as described above, and a tier on a disabled provider is refused. When the provider and every substitute are out, the review stops with a reason that names each provider and why it is out (disabled, keys rejected, or the wait in seconds). A call that failed (a refused request) never goes to a substitute. A review that went to a substitute stays on it to the end, counts against that provider's limit on parallel calls, and its approval counts only when that provider can read the project's checkout. A reviewer whose provider is not listed under `accounts:` (so it has its default account only) ends `failed` when that key is rejected and the tier has no substitutes. Because another provider reviewed, the ledger gets a `catch_up_owed` event with the reviewer's role, tier, the substitute and the substitute's model (the first substitute of the tier on that provider), and the reviewer's row keeps the substitute; the dashboard then names the reviewer by the substitute's model when the event names one, also in a row an open review page already shows, so the model and the substitute name the same provider (an event without a model leaves the model the reviewer started on); a move to another account of the same provider owes nothing. The catch-up review itself is planned. When every provider of the tier is out, the substitutes too, the reviewer ends `blocked` with the reason (`Every provider of the tier is out: anthropic disabled, openai keys rejected, openai_compatible for 60 s.`: a wait in seconds, or why no wait helps), shown in the `reviewer_blocked` event, and the round completes without waiting.

**Output limit.** Each tier has a `max_output` (a whole number of at least 1, the most tokens one answer may have), so a long verdict is not cut off by a model's or a provider's own default. When you leave it out, `strong` gets 16000, `standard` 8000 and `light` 4000; a substitute of a tier uses the tier's limit. Intakt sends it with every call of a reviewer or the judge on that tier, to Anthropic, OpenAI and OpenAI-compatible providers; the Claude Code and Codex subscription logins take no such limit, so none is sent there. When a provider stops a reviewer's answer at the limit, Intakt runs that reviewer once more with twice the limit (twice the default above when the tier sets none); when the answer is stopped again, the reviewer ends `failed`, and its `reviewer_failed` event names `MaxOutputTokensException`. Each of the two calls has its own usage row. The judge is not run again.

**Listing the team.** `php bin/console intakt:team` prints each account once, one line each: its key (`provider.account`), its state (`ready`, `cooling-down`, `rejected` or `no-key`) and the roles it serves, or `no role`. An account serves **Developer** when a project's build routing can pick its provider: for the default tier, for the strong tier that a sensitive path, a confirmed escalation or a setback routes to, and for each tier a `model:` label can name. A tier the project maps no model to adds no role, and the project's other tiers keep theirs. It serves **Reviewer: <role>** for each reviewer of a project's panel (with every rule's reviewers counted) on its provider. Every account of a provider serves that provider's roles. A Developer provider other than `claude_code` is marked `(the build runtime cannot run it)`, because the build runs on the Claude Code CLI alone. A project with no routing or panel adds no role. The listing shows no reset time and no ceiling. It prints the providers its routing or panel picks, and nothing from a provider key.

### A reviewer on a Claude subscription

Map a tier to `provider: claude_code` and a Claude Code model name (`opus`, `sonnet` or `haiku`) to run that reviewer through the `claude` command-line tool, which must be installed where Intakt runs. Create the login token with `claude setup-token` and store it as `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`. The tool gets only that token, your `PATH` and its own folder `var/claude-code/` (where it runs and keeps its state); every other variable of Intakt's environment reaches it empty, so neither an API key nor any other secret does, and the subscription is used. It is given none of its own tools, only the read-only reading tools of the review's own server (file, directory and search), reached with a credential made for that one review; it can read the files of the head under review through those tools, and nothing of the checkout or of Intakt's files otherwise. Its `approve` counts like any other reviewer's when the tool's own report of that call shows the review's server connected and at least one read through it (one of its reading tools, on that server) that the server answered without an error. A call whose report does not show that server connected, or that read nothing through it (no tool call, only calls the server refused or never answered, or calls to another server) or that was started without the server (a review without a round) counts as not having read the change: when it answers `approve`, or `changes` with only P3 findings (which can count as approval, see [Reviewing a branch](#reviewing-a-branch)), its verdict becomes `blocked` with the reason that it approved a change it could not read. When no credential can be made for the server (an empty `APP_SECRET`), the tool is not started and the reviewer is `blocked` for want of tools. Any other `changes` verdict from it stays `changes`. Intakt gives the tool the verdict's JSON schema, so the tool holds its answer to it, and reads the answer the tool returns held to that schema as the verdict (one that is not a verdict is refused like any unreadable answer, below). When the tool returns no such answer, Intakt reads its text instead: a few sentences and then one fenced `json` block holding the verdict; Intakt reads that block as the verdict when it is the only `json` fence opening anywhere in the answer (three or more backticks or tildes, then `json`; one that is indented, quoted, in a list or only mentioned in the prose counts too), it opens at the start of a line with exactly three backticks and `json`, it closes on a line of exactly three backticks, and it does not sit inside another fenced block. A block stays open until a fence line with the same prefix closes it (up to three spaces of indentation count as none): a quoted, listed or deeper indented fence line inside a block is content and closes nothing. A closed block of another language beside it, such as `php`, is fine. A bare JSON answer works too. An ambiguous answer is refused rather than guessed: one with no `json` block, with two or more openings, with the one block in another form or nested in another block, or with a block that is not a verdict is refused, asked for once more, and when the second answer cannot be read either, the reviewer fails because its answer could not be read as a verdict. Intakt reads the environment again for every call, so a variable added while Intakt runs also reaches the tool empty. Its usage is recorded in the ledger like any other call. If its output ends before the final result (the tool stopped, was killed or ran past its 300-second limit), the reviewer's verdict is `blocked` with that reason, and its usage is recorded once as unsettled.

The judge can run on the subscription too: map the `light` tier to `provider: claude_code` (for example `model: haiku`). The judge's call goes through the same tool with the same limits (no tools of its own, and no review server, since the judge reads no files). Intakt gives the tool the judgement's JSON schema and reads the marks from the answer the tool returns held to it. When the tool returns no answer held to the schema, the text of its answer is read instead: a judgement given there as plain JSON text is read and checked the same way. An answer that is not a judgement, or that does not mark each finding exactly once, is not used: every P1 and P2 finding stays on the act list and no P3 finding is added, as for any judge whose answer cannot be read. Its usage is recorded in the ledger like any other call.

Which plans may be used this way is the vendor's decision, not Intakt's. Anthropic's [Claude Code legal and compliance page](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance) says that Free, Pro and Max plans fall under the Consumer Terms of Service and that Team and Enterprise plans fall under the Commercial Terms of Service, that the subscription login is meant for ordinary use of Claude Code by the plan's own purchaser, and that routing requests through Free, Pro or Max credentials on behalf of other users is not permitted. Use your own plan for your own reviews, read those terms for your case, and use an API key (`anthropic`) when in doubt. Intakt does not check your plan.

To decrypt the secrets, Intakt reads `SYMFONY_DECRYPTION_SECRET` the way Symfony does: a base64 value, in the standard or the URL-safe alphabet (`-` and `_`). When it is not set, or decodes to nothing, the vault's own private key file in `config/secrets/<environment>/` is used. A secret that cannot be decrypted (a wrong key, or no key at all) counts as not set, so its provider is switched off and named on standard error as above. A key kept from the browser stands in for such a secret only when decrypting it fails quietly, not when the decryption key is of the wrong length or there is no key at all (see the kept key below, under the review bench). When the vault fails while Intakt builds its configuration (for example a decryption key of the wrong length), standard error shows `Another env loader failed, so its variable counts as set and the kept key does not stand in for it.`: the vault's variables count as set, so a kept key does not replace them. Repair the vault or its decryption key, then restart the app or clear its cache.

### A Codex subscription login

Codex has no long-lived token, so Intakt keeps each account's login file itself. Log in with the `codex` command-line tool, then copy its `auth.json` (in `~/.codex/`, or in `CODEX_HOME` when you set it) to `var/codex/logins/<account>/auth.json`, for example `var/codex/logins/default/auth.json`; the account name follows the same rule as above. Keep that file readable by Intakt's user only (`chmod 600`). Pin the model and the reasoning effort every call uses in `config/intakt.yaml`, both required once the section is there:

```yaml
codex: { model: gpt-6.2-sol, effort: high }
```

The effort is one of `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high` and `xhigh`; anything else stops Intakt from starting. Before each call Intakt refreshes the login when it was last refreshed seven days ago or more (the `codex` tool would do so itself after eight), writes the new login back to the same file, and then gives the call a read-only copy in the account's own folder `var/codex/homes/<account>/`, which is also the tool's home and working folder. Whatever the call writes there never reaches the kept login, and the next call gets a fresh copy. Like the Claude tool, it gets your `PATH`, its home folder (as `HOME` and `CODEX_HOME`) and, in a review with a round, the review server's credential (`INTAKT_REVIEW_SERVER_BEARER`); every other variable of Intakt's environment reaches it empty. A login that cannot be read or refreshed stops the call with a message saying so.

Every call runs the tool with its shell, web search, browsers, computer use, apps, plugins, other agents, image tools and hooks switched off, in its read-only sandbox, without a saved session and without reading a settings or rules file from its folder. These limits are fixed: neither a project nor a reviewer can lift them. The tool refuses a feature name it does not know, so a `codex` release that renames one makes every call fail with that message instead of leaving the feature on.

`php bin/console intakt:codex:probe <account>` (the account defaults to `default`) makes one such call and prints the account, the pinned model and effort, and the answer; it needs the `codex` tool where Intakt runs.

### A reviewer on a Codex subscription

Once the `codex` section is there, map a tier to `provider: codex` and the model you pinned to run that reviewer through the `codex` tool; a tier or substitute on `codex` that names another model, or one without the `codex` section, stops Intakt from starting, because every Codex call runs on the pinned model. List the accounts under `accounts:` as for the other providers (`codex: [default, team]`); each needs its login file, and an account without one fails its call with a message naming the file. Without the `codex` section Codex is simply left out, with no "disabled" line.

Like the reviewer on a Claude subscription, it reads the change through the review's own server: for that call only, the tool is started with that server as its one MCP server (its own settings, which could name others, are still not read), allowed just its read-only reading tools (file, directory and search) and given a credential made for that one review, which reaches it in its environment, never on its command line. The tool is told to read the files it needs through those tools before it gives a verdict; it reads nothing of the checkout or of Intakt's files otherwise. When the server cannot be started, the call fails instead of going on without it. Its `approve` counts like any other reviewer's when the tool's own report of that call shows at least one completed read through the review's server (one of its reading tools, on that server). A call that read nothing through it (no tool call, only failed ones or ones the server refused, or calls to another server) or that was started without the server (a review without a round) counts as not having read the change: when it answers `approve`, or `changes` with only P3 findings (which can count as approval, see [Reviewing a branch](#reviewing-a-branch)), its verdict becomes `blocked` with the reason that it approved a change it could not read. When no credential can be made for the server (an empty `APP_SECRET`), the tool is not started and the reviewer is `blocked` for want of tools. Any other `changes` stays `changes`. Its answer is read as for the Claude subscription: bare JSON, or prose around exactly one fenced `json` block. Its answer must be a complete run of the tool: an answer that is not a valid verdict, including text that only says a limit was reached, is asked for once more and then fails the reviewer ("the reviewer's answer could not be read as a verdict"), never counts as a limit, and the account is not paused. A run that ends before the tool reported the turn complete is `blocked` (`incomplete-stream`). When the tool itself reports that the plan's usage limit is reached, the account is paused like any limited account (for one minute, because the tool's message gives no reset Intakt reads), and the review waits or moves to another account. When it reports that the plan does not include the model or that the login is lost (it could not be refreshed, or the service refused it), the account is taken out like one whose key was rejected; so is a login whose refresh the sign-in service refuses as unauthorized, before the tool runs. Neither is a failed review or `changes`, and only these reports of the tool count, never the reviewer's own text. Each of these account errors is reported to you once, as described for a rejected key under [Cooldowns, pauses and outage notices](#cooldowns-pauses-and-outage-notices); a usage limit is not reported. Any other run the tool reports as failed fails the reviewer, and so does a run it reports complete without an answer.
