The use the tool reports (input, cached input and output tokens) is recorded once per review in the ledger, in a usage row that names the account it ran on (`codex.team`), also when a run without an answer fails the reviewer and when the tool reported the turn complete and then exited with an error. A failed run that never reported its turn complete records no use.

### Capability tiers per project

Each project maps three capability tiers (`strong`, `standard`, `light`) to a provider and a model. Copy `config/intakt.example.yaml` to `config/intakt.yaml` (or point `INTAKT_CONFIG` at another file), edit it, and run `php bin/console cache:clear`. Project names are kept exactly as written, dashes included, and a model must be a text value that is valid UTF-8 and not blank (a value made only of whitespace, including Unicode spaces such as a no-break space, counts as blank). A project can also carry a `watch` section (`repository`, `base` and `task`, all required text that is not blank, `github`, the project's `owner/name` on GitHub, which is required for the watch to read pull requests from GitHub, and the optional `eligibility_label`, the issue label that will let Intakt build a task from an issue, `intakt` when you leave it out, so an issue that carries only the label of another loop is not Intakt's (a blank value, also one made only of Unicode whitespace such as a no-break space, stops Intakt from starting; the work source that chooses the next task reads the label, and `intakt:queue:show` prints the order it picks in, see [Showing the queue of tasks](#showing-the-queue-of-tasks)); see `config/intakt.example.yaml`): Intakt then reads its open pull requests every minute (run `php bin/console messenger:consume scheduler_watcher` to keep the schedule going) and starts a review round for the head of a pull request once its CI has finished green. The round reviews exactly that commit, read from the local repository named by `repository`, so the commit must be there (fetched); until it is, the start fails and that head alone backs off, as does any head whose CI cannot be read or whose start failed, without holding back the other pull requests: the log gets an error with the cause, the ledger records `head_failed` (the cause, the failures in a row and the next try), and the head is tried again a minute later, then after twice as long each time, but never more than 30 minutes later. A head whose CI Intakt could not read because GitHub's hourly allowance is used up (see [Intakt's GitHub App](#intakts-github-app)) is not failing: it is read again at the reset time GitHub named, with no `head_failed` line, no card and no count toward the hand-over. Only when Intakt cannot save that reset time (a database error) does the head count as failing and back off as described above; when even that back-off cannot be saved, the error is logged, nothing about the head changes, and the next tick tries it again, while the other pull requests go on either way. After four failures in a row the ledger records `head_handed_over` once (the Maintainer that takes it over is planned); the head is still tried at those waits, so it starts its round once the cause is gone, without a new push. The latest failure of each head is shown as a card at the top of `/reviews`. Such a failure holds every account (a [failure back-off](#cooldowns-pauses-and-outage-notices)) only when no single pull request causes it: GitHub refuses Intakt's credentials, the disk is full, a second pull request fails with the same error within an hour, or the hand-over itself cannot be recorded. One pull request that keeps failing never starts it. A head whose CI is still running starts nothing and is read again a minute later; a head whose CI failed is only recorded; a pull request closed before its CI finished is no longer read, and the ledger records `head_forgotten` for its queued head, which also takes its card off `/reviews`; a head gets one round, however often it is seen, also when two ticks run at once, except that a head that returns after its round was reported stale (the pull request moved to another head and back) gets a fresh round, because the old verdicts no longer count. The ledger records `round_queued` for each round; when the ledger cannot take that line, the round still starts, the tick reports the failure, and a later tick writes the line once. When a pull request gets a new head while an earlier round's verdicts exist, the ledger records `verdicts_stale` for that round once, also when a round was still starting as the head moved, unless the change cannot affect them (for example documentation only); when the ledger cannot take that line, a later tick checks the round again and records it. That check also reads the new head from the local repository: while its commit is not fetched, a head with running or failed CI is still recorded as such, but the check fails and that head alone backs off as described above: it is tried again once its next try has come (a minute later, then after twice as long each time; a head with failed CI is then read and recorded as failed again), and after four failures in a row it is handed over. The `task` text is given to every round of the project. Several projects can map the tiers differently; a tier you leave out is reported as not mapped. A model name the provider's built-in list does not know is added to it, so newer models work too. An invalid file stops Intakt from starting and names the bad key. `php bin/console intakt:ai:tiers` lists every project with the provider and model of each tier; a tier whose provider is switched off (see above) shows the reason instead of a model.

A tier you leave out has no model. Before a review round starts, Intakt checks that the project maps a model to every tier the round needs: each reviewer's tier and the judge's `light` tier. When one is missing, the round is refused before any reviewer starts, with `Project "<name>" maps no model to the "<tier>" tier, which this round needs: map a model to it in the project's tiers and review again.` (`intakt:review` prints it and ends with exit code `1`). So map every tier the project's reviewers and judge use. The check looks only at the mapping, so a tier whose provider is switched off still counts as mapped, even when no other provider stands in for it; such a round starts, and its call fails when it asks the provider.

A project can also carry a `review:` section that sets its Code reviewers (see `config/intakt.example.yaml`): `quorum` is `all`, `majority` or `one-strong-plus-one`, and `reviewers` lists one or more reviewers, each with a `role`, a `tier` (`strong`, `standard`, `light`), a `provider` (`anthropic`, `openai`, `openai_compatible`, `claude_code`, `codex`), a `prompt` and a `budget` (a whole number of at least 1). Every field is required, and the panel comes from the file alone. A reviewer's `provider` must be the provider the project maps that `tier` to under `tiers:`. A panel of any size works, at least one reviewer (an empty list is refused), as long as its policy can be met when every reviewer approves: `one-strong-plus-one` needs at least two reviewers, one of them on the `strong` tier. An unknown policy, tier or provider, a missing, empty or blank field, a budget below 1, an empty reviewer list, reviewers written as a map instead of a list, a provider that differs from the tier's, or a policy the panel can never meet stops Intakt from starting and names the problem. A `projects:` value that is not a map of projects stops Intakt too. A project without a `review:` section has no panel yet.

A `review:` section can also hold `rules`, a list of extra reviewers for bigger or sensitive changes. Each rule has `min_lines` (a whole number of at least 1), `paths` (a list of path patterns) or both, and `reviewers` written like the core ones (the provider again must be the tier's). A rule without `min_lines` and `paths`, rules or paths written as a map instead of a list, or a path pattern that is not text (a number, `true`) or not valid UTF-8 stops Intakt from starting. The quorum policy must be reachable both by the core panel alone and with every rule added, otherwise Intakt does not start either. A rule applies when the change has at least `min_lines` changed lines or touches a path matching one of its patterns; in a pattern `*` stays inside one folder (`src/*` does not match `src/a/b.php`) and `**` crosses folders (`src/**`), any characters included; `/**/` also matches no folder (`src/**/Security/**` matches `src/Security/Door.php`), and a leading `**/` matches the top folder too (`**/Security/**` matches `Security/Door.php`); quote a pattern that starts with `*` (`'**/Security/**'`), since YAML reads a bare leading `*` as a reference. The reviewers of every matching rule join the core panel, which is never reduced, and the project's quorum policy stays. The label `review:full` applies every rule, and a label `model:<tier>` sets the tier of every reviewer of the panel (the last one applied wins; a value that names no tier changes nothing; under the `one-strong-plus-one` quorum a reviewer configured as strong stays strong; a reviewer keeps its configured tier when the project's `tiers` map the labeled tier to another provider than the reviewer's, or to no model). On GitHub, the watcher passes a pull request's labels to the round only when an account with write access applied them and they are still on it; if the labels cannot be read, none counts. Of two values in one family (`priority:`, `class:`, `model:` or `plan:`), Intakt removes the one applied earlier with a comment; it never removes a `review:` label or a label of your own such as `area:docs`. A round keeps the labels it started with. Without labels the panel comes from the configuration alone.

### Reviewer-for-loop mode

A project with `mode: reviewer-for-loop` is one whose pull requests a build loop builds and merges, where Intakt's panel takes the place of the loop's own Claude reviewer. With a `watch` section, Intakt reviews every new head of the project's open pull requests once its CI is green, with the whole panel and judge, as for a live project. Its verdicts are recorded in the ledger with `"mode":"reviewer-for-loop"` and count like a live project's; the dashboard shows them without a mark. Once a round is judged, or its judging has failed for good (after the worker's retries, for example because the judge's tier has no model), Intakt publishes its verdict on that exact head under its GitHub App, and this is the whole contract with the loop:

- a commit status named `intakt/review` on the head: `success` when the panel approves, `failure` when it asks for changes with a finding to act on or when Intakt withdrew its review (see below), `error` when Intakt could not review the head (no quorum and no reviewer asking for changes with a finding on the act list, for example because reviewers failed, or the judging failed for good);
- for a review decision (`success`, or `failure` when the panel asks for changes), first one pull request comment; a withdrawal publishes its `failure` without a comment. The comment is in the shape of the loop's own reviewer comments: the heading `**Review by Intakt** (head <first 12 characters of the head>)`, one line `[P1] file:line: text` (or `[P2]`, `[P3]`) per finding on the act list, and a fenced `json` block `{"verdict":"approve"|"changes","head":"<the full head>","summary":"..."}`, followed only by an invisible marker `<!-- intakt-verdict: <round id> -->`, after the `json` block. An owner ask on a protected head of a pull request [handed to Intakt](#pull-requests-handed-to-intakt) is not part of this comment: each of its parts is a comment of its own. A finding without a line shows only its file, one without a file shows `(no file)`, and line breaks in a finding become spaces. A `<!--` in a finding is written as `&lt;!--`, so it opens no hidden HTML comment that would hide the rest of the comment.

Without a GitHub App, that is with `GITHUB_APP_ID` empty as in the demo, Intakt publishes nothing to GitHub and does not try again; the review page and the MCP tools show the verdict, and Intakt notes this once per start at info level, below the warnings and errors the service output shows by default (see [The log](#the-log)).

The status and the comment always tell the same verdict, as the loop reads it: the loop counts an open P1 or P2 as changes, so an approval whose act list still holds a P1 (for example from the reviewer who was outvoted) is published as changes, with `failure` and `"verdict":"changes"`; an approval with only P2 or P3 findings lists them under a `Follow-ups:` line, which the loop reads as an approval with follow-ups. A reviewer that asked for changes only with P3 findings the judge deferred or rejected counts as approving here too, so it is among the approvals the comment counts (`2 of 2 reviewers approve`).

Each finding line is redacted like a review, as a whole and on its own, so a redaction that runs to the end of its text (an unclosed credential header, for example, even one that starts in the file and ends in the text) ends at that line; the heading and the `json` block hold no reviewer text and are not redacted, so a posted comment always ends with its `json` block and the hidden marker after it. Then the comment is scanned with `gitleaks`; if the scan still finds a secret, no comment is posted and the status is `error` with the description "Intakt could not publish its review of this head." A review stored as a replay (see [Recording a review for the demo](#recording-a-review-for-the-demo)) gets no comment and the status `error` with the description "Intakt does not count a replay as a review of this head.", whatever its reviewers decided, also when it is marked stale (see below), because a replay was never published as a review. A round of a local branch (not a GitHub pull request address) publishes nothing and logs a warning. When GitHub refuses or cannot be reached, the worker tries the publication again later; before posting the comment it reads the pull request's comments, and when one posted by Intakt's own GitHub App already ends with the round's marker (an earlier attempt posted it; a marker quoted inside a finding of another round's comment does not count) it posts only the status; a comment by anyone else that holds the marker does not count. Only one worker publishes a round at a time: a second one finds the round busy and tries again later. So each round leaves one comment. The App needs write access to commit statuses and to pull requests (`statuses:write` and `pull_requests:write` in `GITHUB_APP_PERMISSIONS` when you check the installation). Apart from this (and the owner ask on a protected head of a pull request [handed to Intakt](#pull-requests-handed-to-intakt)), Intakt writes nothing to the forge in this mode: it never merges, pushes, labels or approves a head, and it posts no review. The loop keeps building, merging and asking you as before. To roll back, switch the loop back to its own Claude reviewer and set the project to `live` (which publishes nothing on the loop's own pull requests; a pull request [handed to Intakt](#pull-requests-handed-to-intakt) still gets its verdict published in a `live` project). The watch reads the open pull requests and their CI from GitHub through the App identity, so it needs the GitHub App settings and a `github` repository in the `watch` section; without it the tick fails for that project with a clear message.

A round can be marked stale after it started, when its head is refused later because it no longer boots. The watcher boots a green head only when its pull request author is trusted (a person with write access, Intakt's own GitHub App or the maintainer) and the head is a branch of the project's own repository; it boots that head again each time the listing names its pull request, and marks the round of a head that now fails to boot while main boots. Another author's head, a head from a fork (also a deleted fork, whoever opened it), or one whose trust check fails, is never booted, so its round is never marked stale this way: it is reviewed unbooted, and the ledger records `head_boot_skipped` with the cause `boot_skipped_untrusted_author`. The boot runs the head's `composer install` and `bin/instance` on the host as Intakt's user, not yet in an isolated job container (see above). Its verdicts then stop counting, also while the pull request is still at that head, and Intakt publishes the round again: the status on that head becomes `failure` with the description "Intakt withdrew its review: this head does not boot." and no comment (a round stored as a replay keeps its replay `error` instead), which replaces an approval already published, also when the mark lands while that approval is being posted. When the withdrawal cannot be recorded at once, the watcher keeps it owed and tries it again on the pull request's next check, also when the head boots by then, and starts no new round for that head before it succeeded. Meanwhile the head backs off as a failing head does, and the cause on its `head_failed` card (and on `head_handed_over` after four failures in a row) says that Review refused to withdraw the verdicts of the head's round, followed by Review's own reason. Intakt publishes the verdicts of one pull request one at a time (a publication that finds another one under way is tried again within a minute), so the last status on a commit is always the one of its latest round, withdrawn when that round was marked stale. A publication of a round is dropped when a later round of the same pull request that reviewed the same commit is already judged, because the later round's status is the one that commit stands at. A later round of another commit does not stop it: when a pull request moves from head A to head B and back to A, and A then fails to boot, A's old approval is still withdrawn.

### The status label of a pull request

Intakt keeps one status label on each open pull request that has a round: `status:in-review` while the round is running or its verdict is not yet published, and `status:waiting-on-you` once the forge took the verdict (an `error` status does not count as published). The label comes from the round Intakt stores, never from the labels that are on the pull request. It is reconciled when a worker takes up a round's queued start message (after the round is stored, so a round that was never stored gets no label), right after a verdict is published, and on every watcher pass over the project's open pull requests. Every watcher pass tries the label, but a label someone changed is repaired only by the first pass that writes it successfully (a pass that reaches GitHub without writing does not repair it), and that pass posts one comment saying so when a person's change was undone. The comment is attempted right after the first label write of that pass; if posting it fails it is not retried (so it is never doubled), and any remaining label write of that pass waits for a later pass, which posts no comment. A step that finds another writer holding the change's lock writes nothing and leaves the label to that writer and the next pass; a failed label write never fails the round or its publication.

### Project mode

A project can carry `mode: live`, the same as leaving the key out, or `mode: reviewer-for-loop` (see [above](#reviewer-for-loop-mode)). Any other value stops Intakt from starting and names the key; that includes `mode: shadow`, which is no longer a mode, so set such a project to `live` or `reviewer-for-loop`. Every reviewer's verdict is recorded in the ledger with the project's mode at that moment (`"mode":"reviewer-for-loop"` or `"mode":"live"`); a verdict recorded earlier with `"mode":"shadow"` stays in the ledger as it was written. The mark is a record only: it never stops a verdict from counting, and a round reviewed before a switch to `reviewer-for-loop` is published like any other. Intakt can post a review to GitHub, but in a live project only a pull request [handed to Intakt](#pull-requests-handed-to-intakt) gets its verdict published; the loop's own pull requests get nothing, and nothing merges yet.

`budget.shadow_ceilings` is no longer a setting either; a configuration that still has it stops Intakt from starting and names the key, so remove it.

To see which panel a change gets, without calling any model, run `php bin/console intakt:review:panel <project> --lines=300 --path=src/Foo.php --label=review:full` (`--path` and `--label` can be repeated; all options are optional, and `--lines` must be zero or more). It prints the quorum and one line per reviewer with its tier, provider and budget. Intakt reads the configuration when it starts, so a changed file shows after the cache is cleared. An unknown project fails with a message. A path pattern Intakt cannot evaluate against a path (for example, one too complex for the pattern matcher) fails the same way, with a message that names the pattern. `intakt:review` takes the paths from the branch and no labels.

### Which tier a build starts on

A project can carry a `build:` section that sets the rules for the tier a build starts on. `tier` (`strong`, `standard` or `light`; default `standard`) is the tier for an ordinary build, `escalate_on` lists the setbacks that raise the next build to the strong tier (`failed-attempt`, `blocking-finding`, `stall`; all three by default, a shorter list turns triggers off, and an empty list or `escalate_on: ~` turns every escalation off), and `sensitive_paths` is a list of path patterns (same patterns as a review rule's `paths`) for the files that need the strong tier:

```yaml
projects:
    alpha:
        build:
            tier: standard
            sensitive_paths: [config/**, src/**/Security/**]
            escalate_on: [failed-attempt, blocking-finding, stall]
```

A build that touches a sensitive path starts on the strong tier, whatever its label says. Otherwise, after an escalation review confirmed a recurring P1 on the change's latest head, the next build of that change runs on the strong tier; a rejected escalation changes nothing. Otherwise, after a listed setback (an earlier failed attempt, a blocking P1 finding or a stall) the build is raised to the strong tier; a rework after a round without a P1 stays on the cheaper tier. Otherwise a `model:<tier>` label (for example `model:strong`) sets the tier, and without either the build starts on `tier`. A `model:` value that names no tier, such as a model name, is ignored. The rules look only at the labels, the touched paths, the setback and the change's escalation outcome the loop passes, so a job can never choose its own model. A bad tier, an unknown setback, or sensitive paths or setbacks that are not a list, stop Intakt from starting and name the key.

To see the tier the rules choose, without calling any model, run `php bin/console intakt:build:tier <project> --label=model:strong --path=src/Foo.php --setback=stall` (`--label` and `--path` can be repeated; all options are optional). It prints the tier and why (`default`, `label`, `setback`, `confirmed-escalation` or `sensitive-path`); an unknown project fails with a message. A first build that `intakt:build:task` starts runs on the tier the rules choose from the task's labels alone (a first build has no paths, setback or escalation yet); no rework build asks the rules yet.

With `--change=<change>` (a branch, or a pull-request address such as `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/pull/<n>` in any letter case), both `intakt:build:tier` and `intakt:build:model` also read the escalation outcome of that change's latest head from the ledger. While its escalation review is still queued, they fail with "the escalation review of the change is still pending" and choose no tier. No build lane passes `--change` yet.

To choose the model of a build and record it, run `php bin/console intakt:build:model <project> <task> <attempt> [--label=...] [--path=...] [--setback=...]` (the attempt starts at 1). It applies the same rules, prints the tier, the reason, the provider and the model the project maps that tier to, and writes one `model_chosen` event to the ledger (`php bin/console intakt:ledger:events` shows it). It calls no model. An empty project or task, a project or task that is not valid UTF-8 and an attempt below 1 fail with a message and record nothing. A build runs the Claude Code CLI, so if the project maps the chosen tier to any other provider, the command fails and records nothing. If the ledger cannot record the event, the command fails and the choice does not count. A build never picks its own model: only the rules, your labels, the paths, the earlier round's setback and the change's escalation outcome decide.

### Showing the queue of tasks

To see the order in which Intakt would pick a project's tasks from its GitHub issues, run `php bin/console intakt:queue:show <project>`. It reads the open issues once and builds nothing. It prints the pick order first, one numbered line per task (`#<issue> <title>`, with `(rework of #<pull request>)` when the task has an open pull request), then the held tasks, each with its reason (for example `waits for #43`). The order that runs today puts the work on open pull requests first (a task an open pull request closes, for example with `Closes #<issue>`, or one whose pull request is on its branch, such as `feat/<issue>-<words>`), then the other tasks; inside each group it goes by priority (`urgent`, `high`, normal, `low`), then the earliest milestone due date. So an `urgent` task not started yet comes after the work on an open pull request. A task with an open pull request gets the `(rework of #<pull request>)` suffix. This is not yet the order the [design](../design/05-architecture/03-choosing-work.md) plans. An issue that is not eligible (without the project's eligibility label, or with `idea`) appears in neither list.

When the list of open issues comes back incomplete, or the project has no `watch` section, the command prints no order and exits with an error; the log holds the reason as a warning.

## Looking inside the development install with Mate

On a development install (`composer install` with the dev packages), `vendor/bin/mate` answers questions about the running app from its own container and environment files instead of from reading the code. It is not part of a production install: `composer install --no-dev` leaves it out.

- `vendor/bin/mate tools:list` lists the tools.
- `vendor/bin/mate tools:inspect TOOL` shows a tool's parameters.
- `vendor/bin/mate tools:call TOOL --PARAM=VALUE` runs one; add `--format=json` for machine-readable output.

The useful tools today are `symfony-services` and `symfony-service-detail` (which services the container holds and how one is wired), `symfony-dotenv-check` (which `.env*` file declares a variable and whether it resolves, with the value masked) and `server-info` (PHP version, system and extensions). The profiler tools `symfony-profiler-list` and `symfony-profiler-get` list and read the profiles Symfony's profiler saves.

The development install (`APP_ENV=dev`) saves a profile of every request, by default under `var/cache/dev/profiler/`, and names it in the response's `X-Debug-Token` header. In `test`, a request saves a profile only when its test calls `enableProfiler()` on the test client; two tests use it to pin what a request does: the number of database queries of the reviews page with three rounds, and the messages a press of the dashboard's start button dispatches. A production install has no profiler. A profile holds only what measures the request: the database queries, the messages dispatched, the time and the memory. Of the request itself it keeps the method (such as `GET`) and the URL without the part from the `?` or `#` on: the query string is cut off before the profile is saved, and a URL it cannot read (for example one with a raw space or a non-ASCII character in its path) is not saved at all. It keeps no headers, cookies, session, body or client IP address, and none of the environment variables, so no secret ends up in it. This holds also when the development install runs with `APP_DEBUG=0`. There is no profiler toolbar or `/_profiler` page; read profiles with the Mate tools above.

To read one request: start the install with `APP_ENV=dev`, send it a request at the address it listens on (with `php bin/instance`, the usual way to start one, that address is in `$INTAKT_INSTANCE_URL`, so the request is `curl -si "$INTAKT_INSTANCE_URL/"`), copy the `X-Debug-Token` header and run `vendor/bin/mate tools:call symfony-profiler-get --token=TOKEN --format=json`. The answer names the request and lists its collectors (`time`, `memory`, `db`, `messenger`). `symfony-profiler-list` lists the latest profiles when you have no token. With `bin/instance`, the profiles live in its scratch folder only while it runs, so run Mate as its command: `APP_ENV=dev php bin/instance -- sh -c 'curl -si "$INTAKT_INSTANCE_URL/"; vendor/bin/mate tools:call symfony-profiler-list --format=json'` (Mate looks where `APP_CACHE_DIR` points when it is set, as `bin/instance` sets it for its command). None of this is in the container image. No Mate extension is published for the other bundles Intakt uses (Doctrine, Messenger), so these six are all the tools there are. For the read-only console commands to use beside them, see "Tools for looking inside the app" in `AGENTS.md`.

If Mate stops with a PHP version mismatch, it was started with a PHP other than 8.5 (the version `mate/config.php` pins); run it with the PHP 8.5 the app uses. If a container tool reports "No compiled container found", run `bin/console cache:warmup` first so the dev container exists. If `tools:list` says "No tools found", check whether `vendor/` is a symlink: Mate then looks for its settings next to the folder the symlink points to and finds none, so it needs a real `vendor/` folder in the project. The other cause is missing package information: Mate finds its packages through `vendor/composer/installed.json`, so if that file is missing or does not list `symfony/ai-mate`, run `composer install` again with the dev packages (without `--no-dev`).

`vendor/bin/mate discover` writes files for coding agents: skill copies in `.agents/skills/` and `.claude/skills/`, and a `CLAUDE.md` in the project root; Git ignores all three. It also rewrites the committed `mate/extensions.php` and `mate/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md`; do not commit those changes, and restore the files with `git checkout -- mate/extensions.php mate/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md`.

## Project memory

Intakt can keep short notes about a project, each tied to the files it explains, and look up the notes for the files a change touches within a size limit (in bytes). When a review round completes, every finding a reviewer marked resolved (and that stays resolved) is written as a note, with its cause and the file it concerns; a round that finishes after a newer round of the same change writes none, since its verdicts are left out of the list (see [Finding ids across review rounds](#finding-ids-across-review-rounds)). This needs an OpenAI account for the embeddings (`OPENAI_API_KEY`); without one the note is not written and the message waits in the failed queue with the reason. Create the notes table once with `php bin/console ai:store:setup`. Notes belong to the project whose review wrote them: a review is only ever shown its own project's notes, even when another project on the same install has notes on a file with the same path. Each reviewer's briefing carries the notes for the files in the change's diff (for a renamed file, its old and new path), most relevant first, marked as untrusted text; the whole briefing is at most 4000 bytes. Less relevant notes that do not fit are left out; when the most relevant note alone is too long, it is cut to fit and ends with `[note shortened]`. It comes after the diff as a message of its own, and the reviewer is told the notes may be wrong and must be checked against the code. When the notes cannot be read (no OpenAI account, or the table was not created) the review goes on without them and a warning is logged. Reviewers can only read the notes, never change them.

## The ledger

Intakt has a ledger that can store review events (review started, reviewer started, verdict received, judge done, usage recorded) in one SQLite table, `ledger_event`, and read them back in the order they were stored, even if the system clock is set back in between. Each event gets an id that carries the time it was stored (a UUID version 7) and keeps the moment it was stored in UTC, and reads it back the same whatever time zone PHP is set to. A reviewer started event names the model the reviewer's tier pointed to when it started (none when the tier could not be resolved, and none in events stored before Intakt recorded it, which still read). So far the model calls of the vendor write to it (their token usage), and so do job containers (how each job ended, and containers removed after a crash; see below). Create the table with:

```
php bin/console doctrine:migrations:migrate
```

The same command also creates the tables where Intakt will keep what it read from a project's GitHub issues: the open issues and pull requests of the last full listing, a marker (the listing's entity tag) that lets Intakt reuse the stored listing when GitHub confirms it unchanged, while a changed listing is read again in full, and the reason a task failed for good. The reason stays stored after its issue is closed, so tasks that wait for it stay held. Intakt does not take work from GitHub issues by itself yet: the code that fills these tables from one full listing of the open issues exists, but nothing runs it so far, and the automatic intake is still planned. When it runs, a listing that GitHub answers only in part, or an issue that changes while Intakt reads who wrote and labeled it, changes nothing: no task is picked from it and no wait counts as finished, and the reason goes to the log as a warning. Right before it picks a task, Intakt reads the issue once more. If the issue changed since the listing in a way that steers the queue, nothing is picked that round, and the next round reads the issue and who labeled it again from scratch: a change of its labels or of who applied each label last (for priority labels, also the order they were applied in, because the one applied last counts), or of the issues its text says it waits for or closes. A label put back by someone without write access counts as such a change. A change to the wording alone, or two other labels applied again in the other order, does not stop the pick. When two workers read the same project's listing at the same time, the first to save its listing wins: the other never overwrites it with an older one, and picks from the saved listing instead, after checking those issues against its own configured eligibility label. A worker whose saved listing was replaced again while it checked it picks nothing that round and logs a warning. A worker that finds the other one still saving picks nothing that round, logs a warning, and reads the saved listing on the next one.

### Choosing the database

Intakt uses SQLite unless you set `DATABASE_URL` (in `.env.local` or the real environment) to another database; MySQL, MariaDB and PostgreSQL are tested too, for example `DATABASE_URL="postgresql://intakt@localhost:5432/intakt"`. Intakt finds out the MySQL or MariaDB server version itself on the first connection. For PostgreSQL it does not ask the server and always uses its support for PostgreSQL 12 and later, the newest it has. The test environment appends `_test` to the database name, so tests against `intakt` use the database `intakt_test`. In CI, a pull request runs the tests on MySQL, MariaDB and PostgreSQL only when it touches persistence (the ledger, migrations, Doctrine's configuration, the Composer files or the CI definition under `.github/workflows/` and `.github/ci/`, or adds a line naming Doctrine in `src/`); when that check fails, the CI run fails rather than skipping them, and a full push run on main runs them; a push whose commit already passed as the head of a pull request into main skips them, and the nightly full run on main (#2126) catches what the filter misses, and until that lands the next direct push or release commit. SQLite runs on every change.

Stored events cannot be changed or removed. Intakt refuses to update or delete one with a `LedgerEventsAreAppendOnly` error, and on SQLite the same migration adds database triggers that abort any `UPDATE` or `DELETE` on the table, and any insert that would replace a stored event (for example `INSERT OR REPLACE` with the position or id of an existing event), even from another tool. On other database engines only the refusal inside Intakt applies.

Read the events back, oldest first, with:

```
php bin/console intakt:ledger:events
```

It prints one line per event (the moment in UTC, the id, the kind and the payload) and ends with a count, for example `2 events`. The payload is printed exactly as stored, console color tags included. The ledger has no notion of a run yet, so the command lists every stored event of a known kind; rows of a removed kind stay stored but are not shown and are not counted.

### Pull requests handed to Intakt

While Intakt runs beside the earlier loop, the pull requests Intakt opens itself (as its GitHub App) are handed over to it: the loop leaves them. Each minute's look at a project's pull requests notes every open one that Intakt created with a `change_handed_to_intakt` event (the project, the pull request's address and its head), once per pull request, also when the pull request did not change since the last look. Intakt decides this from its own record of creating the pull request (the `change_opened` event of the push, with `opening` `created`) or from the author GitHub reports, never from the title, labels or branch GitHub shows, so a pull request someone else opened, the loop's included, is never noted, even with the same branch name or title, and even when an earlier version of Intakt's push updated its text (such a record says `updated`). Intakt now leaves such a pull request alone: a push for a branch that has an open pull request someone else opened stops with an error before anything is pushed (see [Pushing an accepted build](#pushing-an-accepted-build)). Letter case in the repository name does not matter. For an open pull request Intakt has no record of opening, Intakt asks GitHub who created it, on each look only until GitHub gives a definite answer, which is then recorded, and notes it when that is Intakt's own App, so a push that stopped after GitHub created the pull request but before Intakt recorded it is still noted without another push, also when the branch moved since. A pull request someone else created, the loop's included, is not noted as handed over: it gets a `change_not_handed_to_intakt` event instead (the same fields), because the author GitHub sets never changes. So the author GitHub sets decides when no record of opening does, and a recorded answer, either way, means GitHub is not asked again about that pull request. A later push also finds Intakt's own pull request open with Intakt's own App as its author, records it as `created`, and goes on. When GitHub shows that open pull request without its author (or with an empty one), the push stops with an error and changes nothing, so it can be tried again. A pull request Intakt opened before this version has a `change_opened` event without `opening`; for such an open pull request, Intakt asks GitHub who created it, only until GitHub gives a definite answer, which is then recorded, and notes it when that is Intakt's own App. An answer without the author's name (or with an empty one) is no definite answer: nothing is recorded and the next look asks again. A pull request someone else created gets the `change_not_handed_to_intakt` event and GitHub is not asked again; while it is open, a push of its branch stops before anything is pushed (see [Pushing an accepted build](#pushing-an-accepted-build)), so no push records it as `created`. A push of the same commits and text whose only record has no `opening` also asks GitHub again and records it. When GitHub cannot answer who created a pull request, nothing is recorded and that pull request alone waits for the next look (Intakt logs a warning), and the other pull requests are noted and looked at as usual; after a rate limit, no further author is asked on that look. The weekly report keeps covering the loop's own pull requests and is not changed by it. See [Pull requests handed to Intakt](../architecture/handed-to-intakt.md).

Intakt's review of a pull request handed to it counts, in any project mode (also `live`): once its round is judged, Intakt publishes the verdict on that head exactly as in [reviewer-for-loop mode](#reviewer-for-loop-mode), the comment ending with the round's marker `<!-- intakt-verdict: <round id> -->` (the round's one-time token, so a retry posts the comment once) and the commit status `intakt/review`. In a `live` project the loop's own pull requests still get nothing on the forge. When Intakt approves a handed-over head that touches a protected path of the project (`protected_paths`, see [Protected paths](../architecture/protected-paths.md)), or whose protected paths cannot be read (Intakt's copy of the repository does not hold that head, for example), Intakt asks you instead of approving on its own:

- after the review comment, Intakt posts a second comment with `**Waiting for the owner** (head <full head>)`, the line "This head touches protected paths, so it merges only once the owner has approved this head.", and either "Protected paths:" with one `- <code>path</code>` line for every path (a line break, a backtick, HTML or markdown in a file name is shown escaped) or, when the paths cannot be read, "Intakt could not read which paths this head changes, so it counts as protected." without the reason; when the list is too long for one GitHub comment, it goes on in further comments headed `**Waiting for the owner** (head <full head>), continued`;
- the status is `pending` with the description "Intakt approves this head; it touches protected paths and waits for the owner.", never `success`;
- the ledger gets one `owner_asked` event per head (project, pull request, head, round, the paths and the unreadable reason), also when the publication is tried again or a later round reviews the same head, and only once the ask is on the pull request: without a GitHub App nothing is posted and nothing is recorded.

A publication that is tried again posts only the comments that are not on the pull request yet; when the protected paths it finds differ from the earlier try's, it posts the new list as an ask of its own (the earlier ask stays), and that new list is the one the `owner_asked` event records. A protected file name that would show a secret is never posted: Intakt then posts no comment at all and the status is `error` with "Intakt could not publish its review of this head.". A pull request that is noted as handed over after the head it was noted at was already judged gets that head's review published then, shortly after the look that noted it; so does one noted as handed over by an earlier version of Intakt, once, on the first look after the upgrade. When several rounds judged the same head, the verdict published is the one of the round with the highest number of the pull request, not the one judged last by the clock, so a clock that went back does not pick an older round. The one exception is a pull request whose only judged round of that head was kept under another letter case of its address by an older version of Intakt: when no round of the pull request's one numbering judged that head, that round's verdict is published. A head Intakt asks changes for, or could not review, asks nothing. Merging a handed-over pull request, and reading your approval, are not built yet.

## Job containers

Intakt has the part that will run each build, check and review in a container of its own; the check before a push uses it ([below](#the-check-before-a-push)), and no build or review uses it yet. Each job gets a fresh container that is thrown away afterwards: its system files cannot be changed, it works in a scratch area that is gone when it ends (its home folder and caches live there too, so every job starts clean), the inputs it is given are read-only, and the only thing it can hand back is what it leaves in its own output folder, which no other job can see. It gets none of Intakt's keys or tokens, and has its own memory and CPU limit, so a job that runs out of memory is stopped alone. What a job hands back is bounded too: at most 1000 files and 16 MiB in total, so a job that writes more fails instead of exhausting Intakt's memory. An image that declares volumes is refused, because a job could write into them. The ledger records how each job ended (`job_ended`): `exited` with its exit code, `oom` when its memory limit stopped it, or `timed_out` when it ran past its time and was stopped (a job that runs out of memory just before it is stopped still counts as `oom`). A container that keeps running even after it was stopped fails the job: no end is recorded, and the container is removed.

A probe job has no network at all. A job that needs to download (the check before a push names the package registries; a build names the AI company's address and the package registries; a review job names the AI company's address alone) gets a short list of host names it may reach, and nothing else: it sits on a network of its own whose only way out is a proxy of its own, and the proxy lets through only `CONNECT` tunnels to port 443 of the hosts on the list; it does not check that what runs inside a tunnel is encrypted. Everything else is refused: another host, an address, plain HTTP or another port. The job cannot reach other services on the Docker machine either, except one published on port 443 at an address a listed host name resolves to; for the dashboard this is an accepted risk of the [threat model](../design/05-architecture/14-threat-model.md). The job starts only once its proxy is ready; a proxy that stops, reports itself unhealthy or is not ready within 30 seconds fails the run before the job starts. Each refused request is written to the ledger as `job_egress_refused` with the method and what was asked for (for example `CONNECT outside.example:443`), each one once and at most 20 per job. Host names on the list are exact and lowercase, without wildcards; a list with an address, a wildcard or a port is refused before the job starts.

Before its own command, every job checks that what it was told it can do works: it writes a small file into its output folder and its scratch area and reads it back, checks that each input it was given can be read, and, for a job with a host list, that each host on the list answers through its proxy (this needs `curl` in the job's image). Each check is written to the job's log with a mark that is new for every run, for example `intakt-start-check 3f9c…: input: /input/memory is not readable`. When a check fails, the job still runs, but in a reduced mode: its log says `reduced mode:` with the failed checks, the job itself finds them in `INTAKT_REDUCED_MODE`, and the ledger gets one `job_start_check_failed` event per failure with the check (`output`, `work`, `input`, `egress`, or `log` when the check's lines in the job's log are missing or damaged) and the reason, at most 20 per job. Intakt reads the result from the job's log before the job's container is removed, and only the lines with that run's mark up to the `full mode` or `reduced mode` line count. The job's command never sees the mark, so nothing the job writes or prints can turn a failed check into a passed one; if the check's own lines are lost (say Docker's log settings rotate them away while the job floods its log), the check counts as failed. A job whose output folder cannot be written is recorded as an `output` failure. The job image needs `/bin/sh`; its own entrypoint is not used.

The proxy comes from its own image, which you build once on the machine that runs Docker:

```
docker build -t intakt-egress-proxy docker/egress-proxy
```

It has not yet run against a real Docker Engine; it is first checked when jobs run on a machine with Docker. Use Docker Engine 25.0.5 or later: older engines answer DNS lookups for outside names even on a network without a way out.

Intakt needs the Docker Engine's socket at `/var/run/docker.sock` for this. Without it a job fails with `The Docker Engine is not reachable: there is no socket at /var/run/docker.sock.`

Containers are removed when their job ends, with the job's proxy and network. If Intakt itself crashed while jobs ran, their containers and networks are removed before the next job starts (`job_container_removed` and `job_network_removed` in the ledger). To clean up at once, run:

```
php bin/console intakt:job:sweep
```

It prints one line per container it removed and a count, for example `1 orphaned job containers removed.`, and leaves the containers of jobs that are still running alone. When the engine cannot be reached it prints the reason and exits with 1.

### Job images

The image a project's jobs will run in comes from the project's last commit (files you have not committed do not count), from the first of these it has:

1. `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`: its `image`, or the Dockerfile its `build` names (comments and trailing commas are fine; a compose setup is not supported yet).
2. A `Dockerfile` at the top of the repository, used as it is.
3. Only `composer.json`: Intakt writes the Dockerfile itself, with the PHP from `config.platform.php` (a three-number version exactly; a two-number one such as `8.4` is a minor, and `require.php` then picks the patch by the rule below) or else the newest of PHP 8.5, 8.4, 8.3 and 8.2 that `require.php` allows (its latest patch only when `require.php` allows every patch from the newest one Intakt knows was released, such as 8.4.25, to the end of that version, otherwise the highest allowed patch up to that known release, or the lowest allowed one above it, so `8.4.1` gives 8.4.1 and `>=8.4.1 <8.4.20` gives 8.4.19; the same applies to a minor from `config.platform.php`, and for a minor with no known released patch it is the latest patch when the last allowed range runs to the end of that version, otherwise the highest allowed patch), Composer, every `ext-*` extension under `require` and `require-dev`, and fixed versions of the Claude Code and Codex command-line tools.

A job never gets another PHP: when `composer.json` names no PHP version at all, no image is built, whatever else the project has. The build itself is not network-free: steps that come from the project (its Dockerfile, or the base image a devcontainer names) run on the engine's host with the engine's default network, without Intakt's egress controls (see the [threat model](../design/05-architecture/14-threat-model.md#job-image-build-inputs)). After the build, when the project has a PHP profile, Intakt starts the image once without network and asks its PHP for its version and extensions; an image whose PHP does not match `composer.json`, or that lacks an extension, is refused with the version or the extension's name. An extension that cannot be installed fails the build with `missing PHP extension: <name>`.

```
php bin/console intakt:job:build-image path/to/checkout intakt-job/my-project:main
```

It prints where the image comes from, the build's output, and for example `Image intakt-job/my-project:main (sha256:...) built: PHP 8.5.12 with every required extension.`, with the id of the image this build made: the check ran in that image, even if another build has since given the tag to its own. To see where the image would come from and the Dockerfile Intakt would write, without building, run `php bin/console intakt:job:plan-image path/to/checkout`. On any failure both print the reason and exit with 1. What goes to the engine depends on where the image comes from. With your own Dockerfile (the one at the top of the repository, or the one a devcontainer's `build` names), the committed files go to the engine as an archive streamed from a temporary file, so a large repository needs disk space in the system temporary folder for the time of the upload, not memory. With a Dockerfile Intakt writes (the generated image, or a devcontainer that names an `image`), only that Dockerfile is sent, and the size of the repository does not matter. In both cases the temporary file is deleted as soon as the engine has received it, and also when the build fails. It needs the Docker Engine's socket like the jobs do, and the Docker machine must reach Docker Hub, Debian's package mirrors and the npm registry for a generated image. Nothing builds images on its own yet, and images are not rebuilt when the project changes.

A review job will run a reviewer's command-line tool, which your own Dockerfile or devcontainer image may not have. Add `--kind=review` to either command to get a review job's image: your Dockerfile (or the devcontainer's `image`) followed by Intakt's steps that install `curl`, `git`, Node.js and the Claude Code and Codex command-line tools with `apt-get` and `npm`, so the image's last stage must be Debian or Ubuntu based. A generated image has them already and stays as it is. A Dockerfile that sets `# escape=` to a backtick in its parser directives at the top (also after another directive such as `# check=skip=all; error=true`; an ordinary comment such as `# maintainer=Example Team` ends the directives, so a backtick line after it is a comment), or whose last line ends with a backslash that continues it (an escaped backslash, `\\`, does not), is refused with the reason.

Give each project the image its review jobs run in, in `config/intakt.yaml` (or the file `INTAKT_CONFIG` names), and run `php bin/console cache:clear`:

```yaml
projects:
    alpha:
        review_job: { image: intakt-job/alpha:review }
```

A `review_job` section without an `image` stops Intakt from starting. A review job runs one reviewer on Claude Code (it may reach `api.anthropic.com` only) or Codex (`chatgpt.com` only), with 4 GiB of memory, 2 CPUs and 30 minutes; a reviewer on another provider, or a project without an image, gets no job. The job does not carry your subscription login yet, so it cannot sign in, and no review uses review jobs yet: reviews still run inside Intakt.

The script a review job runs, `docker/job/review.sh`, is not used by any review yet. Before it starts the reviewer's tool (Claude Code with file reads only, or Codex with its shell and other tools switched off), it checks that the reviewer gets exactly the commit under review and cannot change it: the checkout must be a real folder, not a link, every file and folder of it must be read-only, the checkout must be at that exact commit, with no changed, deleted or extra file (files your `.gitignore` ignores, such as `vendor/`, are fine), and with no repository setting or local attributes file beyond what a fresh clone writes. A folder the commit holds as a submodule must be empty, as a fresh clone leaves it, so nothing can hide there or run from there; a submodule whose path has a control character, a double quote or a backslash is refused. Files are compared byte for byte, so a project whose `.gitattributes` makes git rewrite files on checkout (`ident`, `eol=crlf`, `working-tree-encoding`, or a `filter` such as a Git LFS smudge filter) is refused even when nothing changed. No hook in the checkout's `.git/hooks` runs during the checks, and no attributes file outside the repository (in your home folder or the system's) is read. It also checks that the job has its proxy and host list and passed its start check, and makes sure nothing bypasses the proxy. The checks need git 2.40 or later in the review image: Debian 12 and Ubuntu 22.04 install an older git, so with one of them as your image's last stage every review is refused (`refused: the checkout checks need git 2.40 or later, found: git version 2.39.5`, say); Debian 13 and Ubuntu 24.04 are fine. If any check fails, the reviewer never starts, and the job's `review.log` says why, for example `refused: the checkout differs from the head`.

### The review size limit

The size check does not use this setting yet, so changing it does not change what a review accepts today; a later part of the work makes the check use it. Each project can already set how large a change a review will take, as the number of characters of diff, in `config/intakt.yaml` (or the file `INTAKT_CONFIG` names). Run `php bin/console cache:clear` after a change:

```yaml
projects:
    alpha:
        review_size_limit: 350000
```

A project without `review_size_limit` has the limit of 200000 characters. The value must be a positive whole number: `0`, a negative number, a fraction or text such as `abc` stops Intakt from starting, with `The review size limit of this project (review_size_limit) must be a positive whole number of characters of diff, for example 200000.` The setting is read and checked at start, and other parts of Intakt can read it per project.

### The check before a push

Intakt never trusts a Developer's own run of the checks. Before anything is pushed, it checks the bundle a build returned (as `intakt:hand-back:check` does, secret scan included, see [Hand-back checks](../architecture/hand-back.md); it takes no `--credential-env`, so it redacts no mounted credential, and no pull request text, so it neither scans one nor records a send-back or hand-over) and then runs the project's check on it in a job container:

```
php bin/console intakt:check:run var/mirrors/my-project.git my-task-branch build.bundle intakt-job/my-project:main
```

The arguments are the project's mirror, the task's branch, the bundle and the project's job image ([Job images](#job-images)). The job clones the mirror afresh and applies the bundle on the task's branch. The mirror need not belong to the job's user: the job trusts that one repository for its clone only, and passes that exception on git's command line only, never in a configuration file. When main (the commit the mirror's `HEAD` points to) has commits the branch lacks, the job then merges the branch into main inside its clone, as GitHub's CI merges a pull request, and checks that merge; the bundle stays as it is. That merge reads no git settings of the job image or the job's environment (merge options, signing, hooks, attributes outside the repository), as GitHub's merge reads none of yours; the repository's own `.gitattributes` still counts. The command uses the mirror as it is, so run `intakt:mirror:update` first to check against the latest main. A branch that conflicts with main (git reports a conflict) fails with `the branch conflicts with current main, so the check ran no step`, and a merge that fails without a reported conflict (git refuses it, for example for a branch with no history in common with main, its commit fails, or it leaves no merge commit) with `git refused to merge the branch with current main without a conflict, so the check ran no step`; neither runs a check. Then the job runs `composer install` and then `bin/check --fast` in that clone, with empty caches, so nothing from the build's own folder or downloads is reused. The job may start with a saved copy of the libraries installed for the same lock files, checked against its PHP version first; otherwise it installs them itself as before. It may reach Packagist and GitHub's archives only. `--check-command="make check"` runs your own command instead (it then has to install what it needs itself). The result comes back only through the job's output folder; a job that leaves no clear result there, runs out of memory or runs out of time fails the check.

Checks of heads with the same `composer.json`, `composer.lock` and PHP version share one saved dependency install. A branch that lacks commits of main shares one only when main has the same two files; otherwise its check installs itself. Intakt keeps them in `var/dependency-snapshots`, which only Intakt itself writes (no build gets it, and each check mounts only its own saved install, read-only, and copies it into its own checkout as writable files). It keeps the 10 installs used most recently (`intakt.hand_back.dependency_snapshot_count` in `config/services.yaml`, at least 1) and never removes one a check is using, so it can hold more while checks run and until the next time it saves or hands out an install after they end. If Intakt is stopped while it removes an install, no check ever gets the half-removed copy, and Intakt clears it, with any other leftover of that removal, the next time it saves an install or looks for one (even when it finds none). The rule for which projects may share one is in place too: a project may share an install only when its lock file pins every package's contents. A project is not allowed to share (it then installs for each check, and the check does not fail for this) when its lock holds a package from a local folder (a `path` package, in any letter case), an archive without a checksum (unless its `https` address ends in its own locked commit id), a source locked to anything but a full commit id, a malformed checksum, mirrors, a local or unknown address, a Composer plugin or installer other than `symfony/runtime`, or when your `composer.json` defines a `pre-package-install`, `post-package-install`, `pre-package-update` or `post-package-update` script or is not valid JSON, or when, after Composer ran, a package that was cloned from source is not at the commit the lock names or cannot be checked (a different commit, a `.git` that is missing, a link or a file, a `.git/commondir` file, a source that is not git, or a folder Composer recorded outside the vendor folder). Intakt looks for each clone where Composer recorded installing it, so a package name with capitals or a `target-dir` is checked too. The reason is one line naming the package, file or script; it never shows the refused address, since an address can hold a password.

Before the job starts, Intakt compares each file the change touches with the version the branch started from. A file that keeps less than a fifth of its size and loses more than 50 lines (a removed file counts too) stops the check at once, with the file named, unless the task's plan names it as removed or rewritten: pass each such path with `--rewritten=<path>`. A file the change replaced with a submodule counts as emptied. A file that is only moved, or one that main shrank, is fine; a file moved to a new path and then rewritten is covered by naming its new path. A named path covers one file only: the file that was at that path when the branch started, or, for a path the change created, the file that is there at the end and the path it came from. Moving another file onto a named path does not cover it, and a file that passed through a named path on its way to another path is still checked under that path. A file moved onto a named path that the change then removed and created anew is not covered either, because the new file there is another file.

It prints any lost files (`lost docs/guide.md: 120 lines to 1`), the last 40 lines of the check's output, and `passed` (exit 0) or `failed:` with the reason and, for a failed command, its exit code (exit 1). A refused bundle prints `refused:` and the reason. A mirror that holds an `info/attributes` file (git reads it on top of every other attributes source, so remove it) is reported as one error line with exit 1, and so is a job event the ledger could not record. Without Docker it fails with `The Docker Engine is not reachable: there is no socket at /var/run/docker.sock.` Nothing runs this check on its own yet: the push and the build lane will.

A build's pull request text has its own check: it must name every Evidence item of the task, and its body must stay at or under 4000 characters, so reviewers can read it quickly. The first line is the title and is left out: a reference there does not count, and the cap counts only the body below it.

```
php bin/console intakt:check:pull-request-text task.md pull-request.md
```

The first file is the task's text, the second the `pull-request.md` the build wrote (the title on the first line, then the body). Run like this, the check only reports and records nothing. The Evidence items are the top-level list items under the task's first Evidence heading (`## Evidence`, a heading with more words such as `## Evidence (for this part)`, or `Evidence:`), counted in order as E1, E2, and so on; an introduction line under the heading is fine, and lines indented below an item belong to it and are not items of their own. The list runs to the next heading; a heading indented below an item, in line with the item's text, belongs to that item and does not end the list, and an Evidence heading inside an item (an example in the task) does not start it. Under a plain `Evidence:` label the list also ends at a new label (such as `Acceptance:`) or at an unindented paragraph after a blank line. An item whose bullet line is empty, with its text on the next line, counts too. A tab after the bullet works like spaces up to the next tab stop of four, also when a code block opens right after it: indent that block's lines with a tab or four spaces. A code block opened after a marker such as `10. `, or on a later line of that item, may open and close at the indent of the item's text. A list inside a code block or an HTML comment is not counted, and a code block opened below an item ends with that item even when its closing line is missing. The body must name each one, such as `E2`; `E10` does not count as `E1`, and a mention in the title, only inside an HTML comment, or on a code block's opening line (after its backticks or tildes, such as ```` ```E1 ````) does not count, because none of these is shown. Comment markers inside inline code (`` `<!-- E1 -->` ``) are shown as text, so that mention counts; a backtick written as `` \` `` opens no inline code, and inline code never runs past its paragraph: it ends at a blank line, a heading (also one underlined with `===` or `---`), a horizontal rule, a code block line, the next list item or a line that starts an HTML comment. A body over 4000 characters is refused for its length alone, before its mentions are read. An item the owner proves still needs its line. It prints `passed:` with the number of items and the body's length (exit 0), or `failed:` with the reason, the missing items (`missing: E2, E3`) and the body's length (exit 1). `fixtures/sample-repository/pull-request-text/` has a sample task with a complete and an incomplete text to try it on.

`intakt:build:task` runs this check on its own after a passed check job, as a round that counts the task's misses on the project's mirror and the task's branch. A first miss stops the build at its `pre_push_check` step with `the pull request text is sent back to the builder:`, the reason and the missing items; nothing is pushed and the build's commits are kept. When the task's previous text was sent back too, the miss goes on to the push and the reviewers instead of back again. A pass, or a hand-off to the reviewers, starts the count again. This count is separate from the hand-back check's. Each outcome is recorded in the ledger (`pull_request_text_passed`, `pull_request_text_sent_back` or `pull_request_text_to_reviewers`, with the repository, the branch, the reason, the missing items and the body's length, never the text; see `intakt:ledger:events`). To run that round by hand, add the repository and the branch: `php bin/console intakt:check:pull-request-text task.md pull-request.md mirror.git task-branch`. It then prints `failed, sent back to the builder:` or `failed, handed to the reviewers:` and records the outcome, so it counts toward the task's one go-back just like a build's check: a manual miss followed by the build's own miss sends the build's miss to the reviewers. A ledger that does not take the line or cannot be read, a lock that cannot be taken, a repository that does not exist, and a repository given without a branch are errors and exit with 1.

## Measured use of subscription windows

Intakt can keep, for each AI account, the latest use of its five-hour and weekly windows as the AI company measured it, with the time the window resets and how old the reading is. A reading belongs to its account only: another account, of the same company or another one, never sees it. A reading whose reset time has already passed, or lies further ahead than the window is long, is not kept, and the previous reading stays. The table is created with `php bin/console doctrine:migrations:migrate`.

From these readings Intakt can decide whether one more call may start on an account:

- No call starts while a window is at or above its ceiling. An old reading still counts until its window resets, since use only grows until then; once the reset time has passed, the reading no longer counts. A reading without a reset time stops counting once it is older than its window is long (five hours or a week).
- The weekly window is paced: the pace is the weekly ceiling times the share of the week that has passed, counting the current five hours as passed. While the weekly use is ahead of the pace, the five-hour ceiling shrinks in the same proportion (for example, a weekly use of twice the pace halves it).
- With no call running, one call starts below the ceilings, even with no reading or an old one.
- A second call at the same time needs a reading of both windows taken within the last 5 minutes, and both windows more than 10 points below their ceilings (at a ceiling of 90%, a use of 80% is not enough). Without a reading of both windows from the last 30 minutes, only one call runs at a time.
- Only the account's own readings count. Intakt's estimate of what a call will cost plays no part.

Set the ceilings, in percent from 1 to 100, in `config/intakt.yaml` (or the file `INTAKT_CONFIG` names) and run `php bin/console cache:clear`:

```yaml
budget:
    ceilings:
        five-hour: 90
        weekly: 90
```

Both default to 90; a window you leave out keeps it. A value outside 1 to 100 (`.NaN` included), a window name other than `five-hour` and `weekly`, or another key under `budget` than `ceilings` and `prices` (such as the former `shadow_ceilings`, see [Project mode](#project-mode)) stops Intakt from starting and names the key. The ceilings apply to every account. Nothing records readings or asks this decision before a call yet: reading the windows from Claude and Codex and holding back new work by this decision come in later changes.

### Cooldowns, pauses and outage notices

Besides the ceilings, Intakt can hold work back in these ways (the tables are created by the same `doctrine:migrations:migrate`):

- **Cooldown of one account:** when the AI company answers that an account's usage limit is reached, that account gets no new call until the reset time the answer gave, even if that is days away. Other accounts, of the same company or another, keep working. An answer whose reset time has already passed, or lies more than a week ahead, cools nothing down.
- **Budget back-off:** no new call starts on any account, but pull requests that are already approved still merge, since merging calls no model.
- **Pause:** the same as a budget back-off: nothing new starts, approved pull requests still merge.
- **Failure back-off:** no new call starts on any account and nothing merges (once holds are checked, see below). Intakt starts it by itself for 15 minutes, and only for a fault no single item causes. Today these come from the pull requests Intakt watches: GitHub refuses Intakt's credentials (an answer of 401; a rate limit does not count), the disk is full, the lock store fails while a pull request is being locked or unlocked, two different pull requests fail with the same error within an hour, or handing a pull request over to the Maintainer itself fails. The log gets an error naming the reason.
- **Drain:** nothing new starts and nothing merges, so work in progress can finish before an update.
- **Watchdog halt:** while any card of the watchdog stands, the decision answers `halted` for every start and every merge, of every project ([Halting the project](#halting-the-project-and-clearing-your-card)). It does not end by itself: only you clear it.

When several are in effect, the strictest wins: a watchdog halt, then drain, then failure back-off, then pause, then budget back-off, then an account's cooldown, then the ceilings. Each one except the watchdog halt ends by itself at the time it was given.

When no usage reading of an account has arrived for 3 hours, Intakt writes one warning to its log, for example `No usage of account claude_code.main has been read since 2026-11-02T08:00:00+00:00, so only one call at a time runs on it.` It writes it once for that outage, also with several workers; if writing the warning fails, Intakt tries again at the next decision; after a new reading, a later outage gets its own warning. Intakt also records the outage as a notice, and the dashboard's Waiting on you page lists it until a newer reading of the account arrives (see [The dashboard](#the-dashboard)). Intakt keeps the time of that reading as the end of the outage, so the outage stays off the page even if the reading is removed later. The reading and the end are saved together: if saving the end fails, the reading is not kept either, the outage stays on the page, and the next reading ends it. Only an outage that ended before Intakt kept these ends can show up again when its reading is removed. Sending these notices to your phone comes with the notifier.

Nothing places a cooldown, a budget back-off, a pause or a drain yet: the AI company answers and the admin controls that place them come in later changes. Nothing checks a hold before a call or a merge yet either, the failure back-off and a watchdog halt included: only `intakt:budget:replay` (below) reads them. The replay reads the cards that stand on your install now, not the ones the scripted day had, so while a card stands every start and merge of the replay answers `halted`.

To see what Intakt would decide over a day, write a script and replay it:

```
# one step per line: a UTC moment, then the step
2026-11-02T08:00 read claude_code.main five-hour 20 2026-11-02T13:00
2026-11-02T08:00 read claude_code.main weekly 10 2026-11-08T08:00
2026-11-02T08:00 start claude_code.main 0
2026-11-02T09:00 limit claude_code.main 2026-11-02T13:00
2026-11-02T09:00 start claude_code.main 0
2026-11-02T10:00 hold budget-back-off 2026-11-02T10:30
2026-11-02T10:00 merge
```

```bash
php bin/console intakt:budget:replay my-day.txt
```

It prints each line with its outcome (`recorded`, `cooling-down`, `start` and so on), and the outage warnings where they happen. The steps are `read <account> <window> <percent> <reset>`, `limit <account> <reset>`, `hold <drain|failure-back-off|pause|budget-back-off> <until>`, `start <account> <calls running>` and `merge`; an account is written as company and name, such as `claude_code.main`. The replay runs on your database and your ceilings with its own clock, starting at the first line, and undoes everything when it ends, so your install keeps nothing of it. A line it does not understand stops it with the reason. `tests/Core/Budget/UseCase/ReplayDay/scripted-day.txt` is a whole example day.

## Summarizing a branch

`php bin/console intakt:change:summarize <branch> [--repository=.] [--base=main]` prints the change summary of a branch of a local repository against its base branch: the short base and head commits, the number of changed files with the added and removed lines, and one line per file (`added`, `modified` or `deleted`). A control character in a file name is shown in a visible form such as `\x1b`, and a backslash as `\\`. A `--repository` that is not a directory, an empty branch or `--base`, a branch the repository does not have, a repository git cannot read, or a repository whose `.git/info/attributes` file exists, prints an error and exits with 1. The summary is what the branch has gained since it was cut from the base branch, not what the base branch gained afterwards.

## Checking whether a verdict still counts

A reviewer's verdict counts only for what it reviewed: the branch's latest commit, the commit it was compared with, the task, the check command and the project's panel configuration, including its quorum policy. `php bin/console intakt:verdict:identity <project> <branch> <task> <check-command> [--repository=.] [--base=main]` prints that identity as one line, `identity` followed by five parts separated by dots (the two commits, then fingerprints of the task text, the check command and the panel). The task text and the command are not shown, only their fingerprints.

Pass a printed identity back with `--reviewed=<identity>` to see whether a verdict given on it still counts:

- `current`: nothing changed.
- `stale: head changed` (or any other changed parts): the verdict no longer counts. A new task text, check command or panel always makes it stale, and so does a new commit, unless one of the two cases below applies.
- `kept-for-untouched-parts: ...`: the verdict still counts for everything the change left alone, and each `review again <path>` line names a file to review again. This happens when the base branch was merged into the branch and git merged it by itself (no conflict to resolve, no other edit in the merge commit), or when the new commits touch only files in the repository's `docs/` folder and the base did not move.

A project without Code reviewers, a `--repository` that is not a directory, a branch the repository does not have, or an identity that is not in the printed form prints an error and exits with 1. Every review round remembers the identity it was started on, and reading its verdicts applies the same rules: a stale round's verdicts no longer count. Nothing reads stored rounds that way yet. `intakt:review` does not read an older round: its own round reviews a snapshot of the branch's head instead (see [Reviewing a branch](#reviewing-a-branch)).

## Checking a new Symfony AI release against the known bugs

`php bin/console intakt:upstream:run` runs every reproduction in `upstream/` and prints the installed Symfony AI versions, then `passes` (the bug seems fixed) or `still fails` for each reproduction, and a count. Add `-v` to see why a reproduction fails. The exit code is 1 while any reproduction still fails. A reproduction counts as `passes` only when its PHPUnit tests ran and asserted something, or when its `reproduce.php` script exits with 0 and prints `REPRODUCTION PASSES` as its last line. One without tests or script, with a skipped or incomplete test, or that runs longer than 5 minutes is reported as `still fails`, and the other reproductions still run. A folder that does not exist, cannot be read or cannot be opened (no execute permission) is an error (exit code 2).

For a bug in Symfony AI, a reproduction folder can also hold a prepared fix for the owner to approve: `fix.patch` (one commit for `symfony/ai`) and `PR.md` (the pull request text as it will be posted), or `COMMENT.md` (a comment for an upstream pull request that already fixes the bug, or for an upstream issue that already covers it). A bug that needs a new upstream issue, in Symfony AI, Symfony or Doctrine, holds `ISSUE.md`: the issue text as it will be filed.
