Comparing result files with each other comes later.

**What a run costs.** Each review of a bench change is one round of the project's panel: every reviewer reviews the change once, and the judge is called once when the reviewers raised at least one finding (with no finding the round is decided without it). A run reviews the eight changes `--repeat` times. On the demo panel of the project `sample` (two reviewers, `correctness` and `security`), `--repeat=3` makes 24 rounds: 48 reviewer reviews and at most 24 judge calls, all billed to your keys or token. A reviewer may send several model requests in one review while it reads the change through its tools. The report's `calls` is the number of usage reservations (one per started review call), not of single model requests, so the tokens of those requests are summed under one call and `calls` can be lower than the requests sent. The report and the result file count the reviewers' tokens only, not the judge's. Intakt prints no price for a bench run: multiply the reported tokens by your provider's prices for a figure in money.

**Reading a bench result.** Read the failure causes first: a `harness failure` means the run says nothing about the reviewer (the provider or a tool failed) and is left out of the defect, false alarm and agreement counts (its rounds, calls and tokens are still totaled), and a miss with cause `bench` points at the bench, not the model. Then, per reviewer, the totals line answers the three questions of [the bench page's table](../bench.md#review-bench):

- **Defects found**: `<n> of <m> planted defects found`. The bench plants five defects, so with `--repeat=3` a reviewer that ran without a failure has 15 to find.
- **False alarms**: `<n> false alarms`, findings on the three changes without any defect. A finding on a change with a planted defect that matches none of its defects is not counted as a false alarm; the result file lists it under `unmatched`.
- **Tokens per change**: the total tokens divided by the number of reviews (8 times `--repeat`, for example 24).

The review bench section of `docs/bench.md` also holds one sentence, `Each change ran 3 times.`, which tells the dashboard how many runs the published result covers. The dashboard reads the count only when that sentence appears exactly once in the section; if it appears twice or more, or the number is 0 or written with leading zeros, it shows the published totals without a run count. The command prints no row for the panel as a whole. Its panel row is worked out by hand from the result file: a planted defect counts as found by the panel in a run when at least one reviewer caught it there, and its false alarms and tokens are the reviewers' added together. Under the figures, `runs agree` means a change got the same outcome in every repeat, and `runs disagree` that the answers varied, so one run's figure for that change is a matter of luck. The result file is written inside the install: with docker compose it is in the container under `/app/var/bench/`, outside the data volume, so copy it out (for example `docker compose cp intakt:/app/var/bench ./bench-results`) before you remove the container.

A key checked and accepted from the browser is kept in the data volume, in `var/data/provider-key.json` (`/app/var/data/provider-key.json` in the container), a file only the app user can read (mode 0600). The page shows only that a key is configured, with its provider and at most its last four characters; the key itself is never shown again and never written to a log. The check is one short call to the key's provider with the model your config maps to the `light` tier on that provider (for a Hugging Face token, the `light` tier on `openai_compatible`), taken from the first project that has one; to check with another model, change that tier. Without such a project, as on a fresh install, it is the `light` model the sample project would get. A key the provider refuses, or that could not be checked, is not kept. A value in `.env.local` (or the environment) or in the secrets vault wins over the kept key; the kept key is used for a provider only when there is no such value, and, as with `.env.local`, after the app is restarted or its cache cleared. One case keeps the provider switched off even with a kept key: the secrets vault holds a secret of the same name, and `SYMFONY_DECRYPTION_SECRET` decodes to a key of the wrong length, or there is no decryption key at all (it decodes to nothing and the vault has no key files). The vault is asked before the kept key and fails with an error there, so Intakt does not turn the provider on; repair the decryption key or remove that secret from the vault, then restart the app or clear its cache. An empty secret, or one a wrong key of the right length cannot open, does not stand in the way: the kept key is used. A kept file that cannot be read (damaged, or holding no key of a known form) is ignored, and standard error shows `The kept provider key could not be read, so it is not used.` when the configuration is built; remove the file (then keep a key again from the browser if you want one) and restart the app or clear its cache. A kept Hugging Face token is kept and hidden from logs but turns on no provider.

## The demo with docker compose

```
cp .env.dist .env.local   # optional: set one or two provider keys (or HF_TOKEN)
docker compose up -d --wait
```

No file needs editing to start: without `.env.local` the dashboard opens, and `docker compose up -d --wait` returns once the container reports healthy, which it does only when the dashboard answers and the sample is seeded. A review needs at least one of `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `HF_TOKEN` in `.env.local` (copy `.env.dist`; every value in it is optional), or one key or token pasted on the setup wizard's AI account step (below). When `APP_SECRET` or `MERCURE_JWT_SECRET` is empty, the first start makes a random one (64 hex characters) and keeps it in the volume, in `/app/var/data/app-secret` and `/app/var/data/mercure-jwt-secret` (mode 0600, never printed), so a restart keeps it; a value you set in `.env.local` wins and no file is written for it. A start that cannot write the file stops with `intakt: could not make or read APP_SECRET in <file>; ...`. The image is built from the `Dockerfile`, the dashboard answers on `http://localhost:7781` (on this computer only), and the data lives in the volume `intakt-data`.

The first start seeds the demo, in the volume:

- the sample repository at `/app/var/data/sample-repository`, with the branch `sample-change` to review against `main`;
- the project `sample` in `/app/var/data/intakt.yaml`, with a panel of two reviewers (`correctness` and `security`) that must both approve. With one Anthropic or OpenAI key, both reviewers use that provider; with both keys, `correctness` uses Anthropic and `security` uses OpenAI. With `HF_TOKEN` beside one or both keys, `security` moves to Hugging Face, so the two reviewers use two providers; with `HF_TOKEN` alone, see [Running the demo on a Hugging Face token](#running-the-demo-on-a-hugging-face-token).

Open `http://localhost:7781`. While no review has run yet, `/` sends you to the setup wizard's last step, **Ready to start** (`http://localhost:7781/setup?step=5`), which opens with **See a real review**:

- **Watching a recorded review** needs no key. When the demo run can be played, **Play the demo run** sits under the title as the step's one main button, with "1 minute · no key" above it (see [Playing the demo run in the dashboard](#playing-the-demo-run-in-the-dashboard)); otherwise one plain line says that this install has no recorded review.
- **Running it live with your key**: go to step 3, **AI account** (the line under the title says so while no provider has a key), paste the key or token and press **Keep the key**, restart Intakt (`docker compose restart intakt`) so the sample project gets its reviewers, then go to step 5 and press **Start the first review** under **First review**. It reviews the sample change, the same review as `intakt:demo` below, and opens its live review page, where each reviewer and then the verdict appear as they come in.

Between the two, three small pictures show what a review does: the reviewer (Read), the judge (Check) and the developer (To fix).

Once a review has run, `/` opens the board on a computer and Waiting on you on a phone, or the newest review when every card is Done, nothing is running and nothing waits on you (the board is then at `/?board=1`, which the Now entry of the menu opens), and the Projects page's **Set up Intakt** button leads to the setup wizard's first step. Keeping a repository address, creating the GitHub App and adding more than one key from the browser are not built yet: they are done in configuration today (`config/intakt.yaml` or the file `INTAKT_CONFIG` names, `.env.local`, and [Intakt's GitHub App](#intakts-github-app)).

Once the server's metrics endpoint and the dashboard answer, every start ends its log with one line, `intakt: the dashboard is ready at http://localhost:7781`. `docker compose up -d --wait` returns when the container's health check passes (`intakt:server:health` reports healthy, the dashboard answers and the sample is seeded), which can be a little after that line. The start no longer prints the review command. Print it, and the line that connects an MCP client, when you want them, in the repository folder:

```
docker compose exec intakt php bin/console intakt:sample:show /app/var/data/sample-repository
```

It prints the command that reviews the sample change, in the form your shell runs from the repository folder (it runs the console inside the container):

```
docker compose exec intakt php bin/console intakt:review sample-change --project=sample --repository=/app/var/data/sample-repository --base=main --task="Add a shipping cost by weight and a report of a customer's orders; a cart of 100 or more keeps its discount."
```

After a start without a key there is no sample project to review yet, so in place of that command it prints `No sample project yet: set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or OPENAI_API_KEY or HF_TOKEN and start again to write it, then run intakt:sample:show again.`

Or let one short command run that same review for you, with the sample's values built in:

```
docker compose exec intakt php bin/console intakt:demo
```

It prints what `intakt:review` prints (the outcome, each reviewer, the act list, the tokens and the cost), then the address of this review in the dashboard (`Open the review: http://localhost:7781/reviews/<round id>`) and the line that connects an MCP client (below). It ends with the same exit codes as `intakt:review`: 0 for approve, 10 for changes, 11 for blocked, and 1 when it cannot start, for example `INTAKT_SAMPLE_REPOSITORY is not set` outside the compose demo or a project file without the `sample` project. Use `intakt:review` for your own projects.

The first start also makes the owner's MCP token, unless `MCP_OWNER_TOKEN` is set in `.env.local` (then yours is used), keeps it in `/app/var/data/mcp-owner-token` on the volume, and allows write tools for the demo (`MCP_OWNER_ACCESS` defaults to `mcp_tool_write` in the container). The `intakt:sample:show` command above also prints a line to connect an MCP client, after the review command, and so does `intakt:demo` after its review (both read the kept token file, so they print it also when run with `docker compose exec`; without any token they print one line saying to set `MCP_OWNER_TOKEN` in `.env.local`):

```
claude mcp add --transport http intakt http://localhost:7781/mcp/owner --header "Authorization: Bearer <the token>"
```

Run it, install the [`intakt-review` skill](../../skills/intakt-review/README.md) and say "intakt review the demo": for the sample project the skill uses the values below, and makes its fixes inside the container (a work tree of `sample-change` at `/app/var/data/sample-fixes`, through `docker compose exec intakt`, so run the agent in this folder), because the sample repository lives in the `intakt-data` volume, not on the host. Without the skill, ask your agent to start a review with the values of the review command above: project `sample`, change `sample-change`, base `main`, repository `/app/var/data/sample-repository` (the default `.` is not the sample) and the text after `--task=` as the task. A start that cannot make or read the token file stops before the seed with `intakt: could not make or read the MCP owner token in /app/var/data/mcp-owner-token`; two starts at once on one volume get the same token.

The token is a secret that sits in the volume and in what `intakt:sample:show` and `intakt:demo` print; `docker compose down -v` makes a new one. [Connecting an agent through MCP](mcp.md) says what the client can do.

Later starts keep both as they are once written (the log says `Kept`), also when you add a key. To seed again, for example after adding the second key, remove the volume with `docker compose down -v` (this also removes the database) or delete `/app/var/data/intakt.yaml` in the container and start again. Without any provider key the start goes on: it builds the sample repository, writes no project, warns `Set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or OPENAI_API_KEY or HF_TOKEN to seed the sample project; the next start with one writes it.`, and still reports healthy once the dashboard answers. Add a key to `.env.local` and start again (`docker compose up -d --wait`) to write the project. An `openai_compatible` endpoint other than the Hugging Face router does not count for the seed, because the seeded models are router models.

### Running the demo on a Hugging Face token

`HF_TOKEN` lets you try the demo without an Anthropic or OpenAI key. The demo needs one provider key or one Hugging Face token, and its calls are billed to the key's or the token's owner. Create a fine-grained token on Hugging Face with only the permission "Make calls to Inference Providers". The container hands it to the `openai_compatible` provider, with `https://router.huggingface.co` (the `router_url` of `config/sample-project.yaml`) as the base URL, unless you set `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_BASE_URL` or `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_API_KEY` in the environment yourself (then yours win and the token is not used). A token pasted on the setup wizard's AI account step and kept is handed over the same way, after the restart, unless either variable is set in the environment or in the secrets vault.

The dashboard can also take one provider key at `POST /setup/provider-key` (the form for it is step 3, AI account, of the setup wizard at `/setup?step=3`: one field for an Anthropic key, an OpenAI key or a Hugging Face token, which Intakt recognizes from its form, and a button; each answer comes back to that step, which shows the provider and at most the last four characters once a key is kept, and no field while one is configured). It accepts a key only while none is configured, from `.env.local`, the vault or an earlier key; it needs the form's token; it checks the key with one tiny call to its provider and keeps it only when the provider accepts it; and it never shows the key again, only its provider and last four characters. A key that has the form of no listed provider is refused with a line naming the providers it can take. When two keys are sent at the same time, the first one kept stays and the other is refused as already configured. Restart Intakt for the kept key to take effect. To change or replace a key later, edit `.env.local`: the dashboard has no login, so it does not change a key.

- With `HF_TOKEN` alone, the two reviewers run on models of two different AI labs through it: `correctness` on `moonshotai/Kimi-K3.1-Pro:deepinfra` and `security` on `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813:deepinfra`.
- With `HF_TOKEN` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `correctness` stays on Anthropic and `security` runs on `moonshotai/Kimi-K3.1-Pro:deepinfra`. Two separate providers are the stronger setup: one token is one outage, one rate limit and one bill.

Two things to know when you set up Hugging Face yourself, for example as `openai_compatible` in your own configuration:

- The base URL is `https://router.huggingface.co`, without `/v1` at the end. Intakt adds `/v1/chat/completions` itself; with `/v1` in the base URL the call goes to `/v1/v1/chat/completions` and fails with a plain "Bad Request".
- Name the router provider after the model, as the seeded models do (`:deepinfra`). Every reviewer call uses tools together with structured output, and some router providers refuse that combination: Groq and Cerebras did. DeepInfra accepted it for `moonshotai/Kimi-K3.1-Pro` and `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813`. The router's default provider may be one that refuses, and the error then says only "Bad Request".
- A rate limit from the router provider you named (an answer of 429) pauses that router provider on that account until the reset time the answer gave (its `Retry-After` header, in seconds or as a date), or for one minute without one, at most a day and at least one second (a zero or past time waits one second). Intakt sends it no call while the pause lasts, also from other workers on this computer, and calls it again once the pause has ended. When another account can take the call, the call goes there at once; when none can, a tier with substitutes moves to the first free substitute, and one without them waits until the first account is free again, never less. The account itself also pauses as for any rate limit, so while its own pause runs another router provider or a model without one on that account waits too. Moving on to another router provider instead of waiting comes in a later change. Intakt counts a model name as naming a router provider only in exactly the form `owner/model:provider`, made of plain ASCII letters, digits and punctuation with no space, line break or other character in it; a name such as `ft:gpt-4o-mini:org:custom:id`, `llama3:8b` or `owner/group/model:router` names none.

Project memory still needs an OpenAI key: a Hugging Face token alone gives no embeddings.

### Recording a review for the demo

A recording keeps one real review of a change in one file: its ledger events and the live updates the review page received, with the date the review started, the models it called and the Intakt version that wrote the file. `intakt:demo:replay` plays its ledger events and its live updates back into the demo project (see [The demo project](#the-demo-project) below). Intakt can already store that a project is a replay: each review started in such a project afterwards is stored as a replay too, and a review started before the project was marked stays live. A review stored as a replay never counts as a verdict, whatever its reviewers decided: the verdict reading gives none of its reviewers' verdicts as counting, `intakt:review` ends it as `blocked` with the reason `a replay never counts as a verdict: this round plays a recorded review back`, and a `reviewer-for-loop` project publishes no comment and only an `error` status for it. The only command that marks a project is `intakt:demo:create`, which marks the demo project `demo-replay` (see [The demo project](#the-demo-project) below). The board, the review page and the status command show the mark of each round: a card whose latest round is a replay carries a **Replay** label, the review page puts one beside each replay round (and says under the title that none of the verdicts count when every round is a replay), and `intakt:status` gives `mark` per project and per entry (see [The status document](#the-status-document) above).

1. Before the first round of the change, set `INTAKT_LIVE_CAPTURE` to a file (the recording takes every round of the change and is refused when one of them has no captured update), for example `INTAKT_LIVE_CAPTURE=/app/var/data/live-updates.jsonl` in `.env.local` for the compose demo, and run `docker compose up -d` so the container is made again with it. From then on every live update is also appended to that file, one line each. If the file cannot be written, the warning "A live dashboard update was not captured." is logged and the review goes on. The capture keeps the order the hub received the updates in, because each update is published and appended while holding one lock.
2. Run the review, for example both rounds of the sample change.
3. Write the recording: `php bin/console intakt:demo:record sample sample-change /app/var/data/sample-review.json` (in the compose demo, prefix `docker compose exec intakt`). It takes every round of that change in that project from the ledger and the captured updates of those rounds, and prints how many of each it wrote. In the compose demo, write it under `/app/var/data`, the data volume, so it survives when the container is made again, and copy it out with `docker compose cp intakt:/app/var/data/sample-review.json .`.
4. Set `INTAKT_LIVE_CAPTURE` empty again in `.env.local`, since the file keeps growing while it is set. Do it before the playback install of step 5 starts: both installs read the same `.env.local`, so otherwise the playback install appends to a capture file too.
5. Play the recording on another compose demo with a ledger of its own, not on this one: this ledger already holds the recording's rounds, so `intakt:demo:replay` and the `/demo` page refuse it here (see below). For example, stop this install with `docker compose down` (without `-v`, so its data stays), start a second one under its own project name, which gives it its own data volume, with `docker compose -p intakt-playback up -d --wait`, and copy the file in with `docker compose -p intakt-playback cp sample-review.json intakt:/app/var/data/sample-review.json`.
6. Play it on that install, keeping `-p intakt-playback` on every command: from the console with `docker compose -p intakt-playback exec intakt php bin/console intakt:demo:replay /app/var/data/sample-review.json --factor=10`, or in the dashboard by setting `INTAKT_DEMO_RECORDING=/app/var/data/sample-review.json` in `.env.local`, running `docker compose -p intakt-playback up -d` so the container is made again with it, and pressing **Play the demo run** on the `/demo` page. Both ways are described below.

The command writes nothing and exits 1 when the ledger has no round of that change in that project, when `INTAKT_LIVE_CAPTURE` is empty, when the capture file cannot be read, holds a line that is no captured update (the message names the line) or holds no update of one of those rounds (the message names the round, so a capture started after the first round is refused), or when the recording file cannot be written.

### The demo project

Recordings are played back into a project of their own, `demo-replay`, never into the `sample` project or one of yours. The project exists on the compose demo, marked as a replay, so every review in it is shown with the **Replay** label and never counts as a verdict. Each start of the compose demo creates it after the seed by running `php bin/console intakt:demo:create`, which prints `The demo project demo-replay is marked as a replay: its reviews never count as a verdict.` Running it again changes nothing.

The command refuses on any install that is not the compose demo, which it recognizes by `INTAKT_COMPOSE_SERVICE` (set only by `compose.yaml`): it prints `The demo project is created only on the compose demo, and INTAKT_COMPOSE_SERVICE is empty on this install.`, marks nothing and exits 1. A container started with `docker run` and a sample repository is not the compose demo either, so its start does not create the project.

To play a recording back, run `php bin/console intakt:demo:replay /app/var/data/sample-review.json` (in the compose demo, prefix `docker compose exec intakt`, or `docker compose -p intakt-playback exec intakt` on the playback install of [Recording a review for the demo](#recording-a-review-for-the-demo), and put the file where the container can read it, for example in `/app/var/data/`). It writes the recording's ledger events into the ledger one by one, in the recorded order and with the recorded time between them, counted from the start of the replay so a slow write does not stretch the rest, so the review unfolds as it did. `--factor=10` plays it ten times as fast (`--factor=0.5` at half speed); the default is 1. Each event is written as recorded, except that a project name equal to the recording's project becomes `demo-replay`, so the review shows up in the demo project with its **Replay** label, and the event that starts a review round also records the factor. Between the events it sends the live updates the review page received during the recorded review, each at its recorded time on the same pace and unchanged, so an open review page changes as it did during the real review; an update recorded at the same moment as an event is sent after that event is written. A page opened later reads the events when it loads. The review page shows the speed factor beside each replayed round's **Replay** label, for example "Played 10 times as fast as recorded"; a page that was already open shows it after a reload. It ends with `Replayed <n> ledger events and <n> live updates of change <change> into the demo project demo-replay, <factor> times as fast as recorded.`

It writes nothing and exits 1 on an install that is not the compose demo (`A recording is replayed only on the compose demo, and INTAKT_COMPOSE_SERVICE is empty on this install.`), for a factor that is not a number above 0, for a file that cannot be read or is not a recording, while another replay runs, when the demo project is not there (after `intakt:demo:remove`, until `intakt:demo:create` or the next start creates it again), and when the ledger already holds one of the recording's review rounds: a recording plays back once per install, since a second playback would mix into the first one's review pages. It also refuses when the ledger already holds one of the recording's model calls (by call id), so the replay's usage never mixes with another project's and removing the demo project never takes that project's usage with it. When writing an event fails, it stops there without sending the updates after it, exits 1 and says how many events it wrote. If the very first event failed, nothing was written: the message says so and asks you to replay the recording again, and earlier replays in the demo project stay as they are. Otherwise the events it wrote stay in the ledger, and once the start of a review round is among them the same recording is refused as a second playback; the message says the events stay and names the way out: run `intakt:demo:remove`, then `intakt:demo:create` (or start the compose demo again), before replaying it. `intakt:demo:remove` deletes every replay in the demo project, not only the stopped one.

To remove the demo project with everything played back into it, run `php bin/console intakt:demo:remove` (in the compose demo, prefix `docker compose exec intakt`). It deletes the recorded events of every review in `demo-replay` from the ledger, then the project's replay mark, and ends with `Removed the demo project demo-replay with its <n> ledger rows.` Your other projects, the `sample` project included, keep all their events. This is the only command that deletes events from the ledger, and only those of the demo project. Like the other demo commands it refuses and exits 1 on an install that is not the compose demo, and it also refuses while a replay runs. When `demo-replay` is not marked as a replay (for example after a removal) it prints `The project demo-replay is not marked as a replay, so nothing is removed.` and exits 0. Run `intakt:demo:create`, or start the compose demo again, before the next replay.

### Playing the demo run in the dashboard

The dashboard's `/demo` page (`http://localhost:7781/demo`) plays a recording without the console and without a provider key. `INTAKT_DEMO_RECORDING` names the recording file as the container sees it. Intakt ships one recording, a real one-round review of the sample change by two reviewers on Claude and GPT, at `/app/fixtures/demo/sample-change-review.json` in the container, and the image names it by default, so a fresh `docker compose up -d --wait` with no `.env.local` can play it. To play another one, written with `intakt:demo:record` (above), set the variable in `.env.local` (for example `INTAKT_DEMO_RECORDING=/app/var/data/sample-review.json`, with the file copied into the volume) and run `docker compose up -d` (`docker compose -p intakt-playback up -d` for the playback install) so the container is made again with it; setting it empty there offers no demo run. The page opens with one short line, two labels (**No key needed** and **Replay, not a verdict**) and four pictures of how a review works (the change, two reviewers from two AI labs, the judge checking every finding, To fix). Below the labels it says in one line what it can do:

- On an install that is not the compose demo: `The demo run plays only on the compose demo, and INTAKT_COMPOSE_SERVICE is empty on this install.`
- With `INTAKT_DEMO_RECORDING` empty or naming no file: `No demo recording is installed: set INTAKT_DEMO_RECORDING to a file written by intakt:demo:record.`
- Once a demo run is in the ledger: `The demo run is in the ledger. To play it again, remove it with intakt:demo:remove, then press Play: it creates the demo project again.`
- Otherwise it offers **Play the demo run**. The setup wizard's Ready step (`/setup?step=5`) offers the same button under its title **See a real review** while the run can be played.

The button plays the recording at one and a half times the recorded pace, as `intakt:demo:replay --factor=1.5` would, in a queue worker, so the page answers at once with `The demo run is starting: the caption at the bottom of each page names the stage it is in.` A run that does not play says so on the page: the worker replaces the start line on `/demo` with `The demo run did not play.` and a fixed sentence (the compose demo is gone, the recording setting was cleared, the recording could not be read, is not a valid recording, the replay was refused or the replay stopped), and logs `The demo run was not played.` with the details. The sentence never names a path; the log does. Only the page of that press is updated, so a refusal of a later press does not replace the line of a run that is playing, a press refused because another Play or `intakt:demo:remove` holds the replay lock reads `Another Play or a demo removal is running. Wait until it has ended, reload the page and press Play again.`, a press refused because the ledger already holds the recording's round, or one of its model calls under the demo project in a round the recording names and neither it nor the ledger starts, reads `An earlier Play has already played the run. Follow it in the review list.`, and a press refused because another review (of another project, or another recording played before) already used one of the recording's model call ids reads `Another review in the ledger already used a model call of the demo recording, so the run was not played. The install log names the call.`, each on its own page. A page that connects after the refusal was published does not see it: reload `/demo` to see the state of the ledger. The worker checks the compose demo again before it plays, so a press queued before the install stopped being the compose demo plays nothing either. The dashboard's Play also creates a removed demo project again (under the replay lock, once the recording is accepted, so a refused press changes nothing); the console replay does not: after `intakt:demo:remove` it is refused until `intakt:demo:create` (or a new compose demo start) sets the project up. Two quick presses can queue the run twice: the first plays it and the second is refused as a second playback, so that log line after a double click is harmless.

While the demo project holds a played run, every dashboard page except the error page shows a caption bar at its bottom, after the page's content, with the stage of the run (one dot per stage, the current one wider), one short sentence about it (at the two reviewer stages, followed by the reviewers' names as model and AI lab, such as Kimi K3.1 (Moonshot AI), with the raw model id in a tooltip; a model Intakt does not know is shown by its id alone) and a link to its review, and it rings the part of the review page that stage is about (the reviewers, the judge, and at the last stage the owl's card with the answer and the To fix count) when that page shows the round. There are five stages: the change arrives; the reviewers read it; findings arrive; the judge checks them; the round is done and To fix lists what still needs changing. A later round passes the same five stages again. A page that receives live updates moves the caption on as each recorded event is written. **Pause** (desktop only; the phone caption is one row without it) holds the caption at its stage until **Resume** (a page you open meanwhile shows the stage of that moment), and **Close** hides it in that browser tab until a later round starts. Right after you press **Play the demo run**, the demo page takes you to the review as soon as the first stage shows, unless you paused or closed the caption; opening the demo page later leaves you there. Eight seconds after the last stage shows, the caption closes by itself (a paused caption closes eight seconds after **Resume**). The caption never shows outside the compose demo.

## Keeping a copy of a project's repository

`php bin/console intakt:mirror:update <project> <remote>` keeps one bare copy (a mirror) of a project's repository in `var/mirrors/<project>.git`. The first run copies the whole repository from `<remote>` (a path, a `file://` or an `https://` address; a relative path counts from the mirrors folder `var/mirrors`, not from where you run the command); later runs fetch what is new and drop branches the remote deleted. The mirror's `HEAD` follows the remote's, also when the remote's `HEAD` is a detached commit, even one on no branch or tag, and a SHA-256 repository gets a SHA-256 mirror. If the branch the remote's `HEAD` names is deleted during a run, so the mirror's `HEAD` would point at nothing, the run says so and exits with 1, and the next run sets `HEAD` again. A first copy is built in a hidden folder beside it and only takes the mirror's place once it is complete, so a copy that fails half-way never counts as a mirror; a folder at the mirror's place that is not a bare repository is replaced. The project name must start with a letter or digit and may hold letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`. If git cannot reach the remote, hangs past 10 minutes or is stopped, Intakt waits 10 seconds and tries again, waits 30 more seconds and tries a last time, logging each wait; then it prints git's message (or, for a call that hung or was stopped, the reason in Intakt's own words, never the command with a password that may be in the address) and exits with 1. The mirror keeps neither the remote's address nor a password that may be in it (you name the remote on every run). Delete the mirror and the next run copies it again.

`php bin/console intakt:build:clone <project> <branch> <directory>` makes the copy a build works in, from the project's mirror (run `intakt:mirror:update` first). The new folder `<directory>` holds main (the branch the mirror's `HEAD` names) and the task's `<branch>`, checked out, and nothing else: no other branch, no tag and no remote. A branch the mirror does not hold yet starts at main. The command refuses a `<directory>` where anything already is (a folder, a file or a link, also a folder another `intakt:build:clone` made a moment before, so two runs for the same folder never share it), a project name that breaks the rule above, a project without a mirror, a mirror whose `HEAD` names no branch, a branch name git does not accept and a branch that is main itself; if a step fails after it made the folder, it removes that folder; it prints the reason and exits with 1. On success it prints `clone <directory>`.

A task split from a bigger change can build on an earlier piece of work with a line of its own `Source: <branch>@<sha>` (the full commit id). Give the task's text as a file with `--task=<file>`. Add `--trusted-author` when the task's author has write access to the project: the command then checks in the mirror that the commit is on that branch and adds that one commit to the copy as `refs/source/<branch>`, not as a branch and without any later commit of that branch. A source branch git does not accept, a branch the mirror does not hold, a commit that is not on the branch, an id that names something other than a commit (an annotated tag, for example) and a task with two source lines are refused, and no copy is made. Without `--trusted-author` the source line is ignored: the command prints one line starting with `comment` that says so, and makes the copy without it. A sentence that only mentions a source line, a quoted or indented line, a line inside a fenced code example (between lines of three backticks or tildes), a line inside an `<!-- external -->` ... `<!-- /external -->` block (text copied from outside, which does not count even in a trusted task; a block that is never closed runs to the end of the text) and a short commit id are not source lines. A task file that cannot be read is refused.

## Building one task

`php bin/console intakt:build:task <project> <issue number> --remote=<remote> --branch=<branch> --directory=<folder> --image=<image>` takes one open issue of a project from its text to a build, in this order: it reads the issue from GitHub (a pull request of that number is no task), updates the project's mirror from `<remote>` (as `intakt:mirror:update` does), makes the build clone in `<folder>` for `<branch>` (as `intakt:build:clone` does; the folder must not exist yet) and runs the build job in that clone in a container made from the project's job image `<image>` (as `intakt:build:run` does). When the project has a `github` entry in its watch section, `<remote>` must be that repository's `https://github.com/<owner>/<name>` address (a `.git` suffix and the letter case of the host, owner and name do not matter, but `https` itself must be written in lower case; a user name or token in the address is allowed; an ssh address, another host or a local path do not count). Otherwise the command refuses before it reads the issue, records a step or pushes, reports no step, prints a reason that names the remote (without any credentials in it) and the configured repository, and exits with 1. A project without a `github` entry accepts any remote, a path or a `file://` address included. The build job gets the issue's title and body as the task. <a id="bug-tasks"></a>An issue with the `bug` label must describe the bug in four parts: **When** (the steps or input), **Today** (what happens), **Expected** (what should happen) and **Must keep working** (what the fix must not change). Each part starts at a heading that is exactly its name (`## When`, in any letter case, with an optional colon, also in a list item such as `- ## When`, or, outside a list, `When` underlined with `===` or `---`) or at a line that starts with its name in bold (`**When:** I open the page`, also in a bulleted or numbered list such as `1. **When:** I open the page`), and runs to the next heading or the next line that starts with bold text. Text in fenced code blocks (```` ``` ```` or `~~~`), in HTML comments and between `<!-- external -->` and `<!-- /external -->` does not count; text indented four spaces counts like any other text. Right before the build job, after the mirror and the clone, Intakt checks the four parts; when one is missing or empty, no build job runs: the task is parked with a question that names the missing parts and recommends adding them (the other choice is removing the `bug` label): the command prints the parking warning and stores the question, as for a Developer's question below. Nothing posts the question to the issue yet. A complete bug task's Developer prompt tells the Developer to write the failing regression test first, commit it on its own and only then fix the bug; a bug that no test can reproduce belongs in an issue without the `bug` label, which keeps the usual Evidence rules, because a labeled head with no new test fails the pre-push check. The build job's tier comes from the project's `build:` routing rules in `config/intakt.yaml` ([Capability tiers per project](#capability-tiers-per-project)), so a `model:<tier>` label on the issue counts, and that tier must use the `claude_code` provider. The build job step passes when the Developer made at least one commit on the branch, wrote `PR.md` and left no question (a question comes first, see [Running a build](#running-a-build)). A valid question parks the task instead: Intakt stores the question, its choices and its recommendation, records the build job step as `asked` and a `task_parked` event (both name the task by its number only, never the question), prints `build_job asked: the task is parked until its question is answered` (never the question itself), runs no later step and exits with 0; if the database does not store the question, the step fails with `Issue <n> of project "<project>" was not parked: the database did not store its question`. A later run of the same issue whose build job passes removes the stored question; if the database does not remove it, the step fails with `Issue <n> of project "<project>" is still parked: the database did not remove its question`. A question that breaks the question rule fails the step. Otherwise it fails with the reason `intakt:build:run` gives (for example `the build made no commit on the task's branch`). A project without a `build:` section also fails this step. Then it checks the bundle the build handed back against the project's mirror, as `intakt:hand-back:check` does for the bundle (secret scan included, so `gitleaks` must be installed; see [Hand-back checks](../architecture/hand-back.md)), but without the pull request text and without sending a refusal back to the Developer. A refused bundle fails this step with the refusal's reason (for example `the bundle cannot be read as a git bundle`); an accepted one moves the branch of the build clone in `<folder>` to the checked commits, because the build job commits in a copy of its own inside the container (if the clone cannot take them, this step fails too). A git or gitleaks call that is killed or runs too long fails this step with fixed words such as `the hand-back check was killed by signal 15`. A bundle file that cannot be removed from `var/hand-backs` after the check fails this step with that error's message, also when the bundle was accepted. Then comes the pre-push check: it first updates the project's mirror from `<remote>` again, because main may have moved while the Developer worked (an update that fails fails this step), then runs the project's checks on the same bundle in a check job of its own in a container made from `<image>`, as `intakt:check:run` does without `--check-command`, so for a PHP project the job runs `composer install` and `bin/check --fast` on a fresh clone of the mirror with the bundle applied ([Check job](../architecture/check-job.md)). When main moved since the branch last merged it, the job first merges the branch into current main inside its own clone, as CI merges a pull request, and runs the checks on that merge; the pushed branch stays as the build handed it back. A branch that conflicts with current main fails this step with `the branch conflicts with current main, so the check ran no step` and runs no check; a merge that fails without a reported conflict (git refuses it, for example for a branch with no history in common with main, its commit fails, or it leaves no merge commit) fails it with `git refused to merge the branch with current main without a conflict, so the check ran no step`. A passed check is the prerequisite of this step, and for an issue with the `bug` label the regression rule further down can still fail it. A check that does not pass fails it with a fixed reason, for example `the check command failed (exit code 2)`, never with the check's log, because the log holds what the built code printed. A check job that runs out of its 30 minutes (`the job ran out of time`) or its memory, a bundle the check refuses again, a job the container runner refuses, and a call that is killed or runs too long (fixed words such as `the pre-push check was killed by signal 15`) fail it too. After a passed pre-push check the build clone in `<folder>` holds the checked commits on `<branch>`. For an issue with the `bug` label, a passed check is followed by an attempt at two more jobs from `<image>`: the test files the branch adds, changes or renames (a renamed test under its new path; read from its diff against the base, for a PHP project the paths under `tests/` ending in `Test.php`) run once on the base, the merge base of the branch and the current main (which moves past the commit the branch started from when the branch merges main), with only those test files taken from the branch, and once on the branch itself, each with `vendor/bin/phpunit` after `composer install`. Each run ends as `passed`, `failed` (an assertion failed), `errored` (an error before any assertion, such as a missing fixture, tests that only skipped, or a test PHPUnit calls risky, such as one that checks nothing without declaring `#[DoesNotPerformAssertions]`), `no-result` or `timed-out`; a branch that adds, changes or renames no test starts neither job. The outcomes go to Intakt's log at info level (`The regression tests of a bug head ran.`, with the test paths and both outcomes), and a run that cannot start is a warning there. A run on the branch that cannot start after the test passed on the base is no warning: it counts as `no-result` and the step fails. A branch that adds no test, or whose new test passes on the base, fails this step with that reason and pushes nothing, like a red check; every other outcome leaves the step as the check job gave it ([Check job](../architecture/check-job.md#regression-tests-of-a-bug)). Last comes the push: Intakt signs the checked commits with its own key and pushes `<branch>` to `<remote>`, as described in [Pushing an accepted build](#pushing-an-accepted-build), then opens the pull request of the branch on GitHub, or updates the open one, with the title from the first line of the Developer's `PR.md` and the rest as its text. For an issue with the `bug` label whose pre-push check passed with the outcomes of its regression tests, that text also gets three lines: `- Regression tests: <paths>`, `- On the base: <outcome>` and `- On the head: <outcome>` (an outcome is one of the five above, or `not run`). They go at the end of the text's first `## Evidence` section (a `#` heading or an underlined heading named `Evidence`, also with a colon, outside code; the section runs to the next heading of the same or a higher level, so `### E1` subheadings stay inside it), or under a new `## Evidence` heading at the end when the text has none. A test path with characters other than letters, digits and `_./@+ -` is shown as `a test whose path is not shown`. A `PR.md` whose first line is empty fails this step with `the pull request text has no title on its first line` and pushes nothing. When the mirror's git config rewrites the address of `<remote>` (a `url.<base>.insteadOf` or `url.<base>.pushInsteadOf` value that `<remote>` starts with), or has a remote section named exactly `<remote>` (any `remote.<remote>.*` setting, such as a `pushurl`), the push step refuses, because git would push to another address. This check runs in the push step, so by then the issue has been read and the earlier steps are recorded: the push step is recorded as failed, nothing is pushed and GitHub is not asked. A `<remote>` that names another repository than the configured one is different: it is refused before the first step, as described above. A push the remote refuses fails it with the recorded reason (`the remote refused the push of <branch> (git push exited with <n>)`) and opens no pull request; a branch that conflicts with main fails it with `the branch conflicts with main, so nothing was pushed (conflicting paths: <n>)`, and is not sent back to the Developer here; an unsigned commit, a title or text that trips the secret scan and a failed GitHub write fail it with their message. The first step reads the named issue directly from GitHub with one request; it does not list the project's open issues, so it never waits for a listing slot when the hourly GitHub budget runs low. It fails with `project "<project>" has no open issue <n>` when the issue is closed, missing, moved to another repository, a pull request, or when GitHub answers with another issue than the one asked for. A read GitHub refuses or rate-limits, an answer that is not one issue, and an issue whose state is neither open nor closed fail it with the reason ([Forge contract](../architecture/forge-contract.md)). While one run of an issue reads it and records this step, a second run of the same issue does not wait: it fails this step at once with `another run of this task is starting` and runs no other step, so the dashboard can tell which run recorded its `task` step last; a lock that cannot be taken fails this step with `the start of the run could not be locked`. It prints each step that passed (`task passed`, `mirror passed`, `clone passed`, `build_job passed`, `hand_back_check passed`, `pre_push_check passed`, `push passed`) and the issue's title (once it was read; a control character in the title is printed as `?`, because the title is text anyone who can open an issue writes). It stops at the first step that fails, prints that step and its reason, exits with 1 and runs no later step; nothing is retried. Each step is recorded in the ledger as a `build_step_ended` event with an id of the run (the same for every step of one run), the step and whether it passed or failed, or `asked` for the build job of a parked task (`php bin/console intakt:ledger:events` shows it). An issue number that is not a whole number, a missing option, or a ledger that cannot record a step also fail with a message. A run whose steps all pass ends after the push and exits with 0. The bundle file is not kept (an accepted bundle's commits and their signed copies stay in the mirror, named by no branch, and the unsigned ones on the branch of the build clone), so use `intakt:build:run` when you need the bundle. The push writes its own ledger events (`push_intended`, `branch_pushed` or `push_refused`, `change_intended`, `change_opened`) before the push step's `build_step_ended`. Running the command again for the same issue is not refused yet. `intakt:status` shows the last step of each task and its outcome (see [The status document](#the-status-document)), and the dashboard's task page `/tasks/<project>/<issue number>` shows each step of the task's latest run (see [The dashboard](#the-dashboard)). See [Build task](../architecture/build-task.md).

## Running a build

`php bin/console intakt:build:run <project> <clone> <branch> <task> <image> <bundle>` runs a first build of a task: the Claude Code command-line tool works on the task in a container of its own, made from the project's job image `<image>`, and hands back its commits and the pull request text. Git settings the tool leaves in its working copy (including files that tell git to read objects from elsewhere), or in a repository it makes around it, are not used to pack the commits: the job packs only its working copy, writes fresh settings there first, and when it cannot, it hands back no commits and says so in its log. `<clone>` is a copy made with `intakt:build:clone` for the same `<branch>`, and `<task>` is a file holding the task's text. Add `--decision="..."` once for each of your decisions on the task, in order. The model comes from the project's tiers in `config/intakt.yaml` ([Capability tiers per project](#capability-tiers-per-project)): `--tier=strong`, `standard` (the default) or `light`, and that tier must use the `claude_code` provider.

The Developer gets the copy's branches, the [instructions](#what-a-developer-is-told), and a network that reaches only Anthropic's API and the package registries. A question comes first: a build that ended with a valid question exits with 0 and writes no bundle, whatever commits or `PR.md` it also left (see [the question paragraph](#a-build-that-ends-with-a-question)); only a job that ran out of memory or time, or whose output was too big, not plain files or not cleared, fails before its question is read. When it left no question, made at least one commit on the branch and wrote `PR.md`, the command writes the commits as a git bundle to `<bundle>`, prints `bundle <bundle>`, `pull request text:` and the text, and exits with 0. Check the bundle with `intakt:hand-back:check` before you trust it ([Checking what a build hands back](#checking-what-a-build-hands-back)). Otherwise (it accepted neither a valid question nor a complete change) it prints the end of the build's log and the reason (no commit, which is also what you see when the job could not prepare the copy of the branch, so check that the copy exists and holds the branch; no pull request text, out of memory, out of time after an hour, an output too big or not plain files, or files the Developer put in the output folder itself that the job could not remove), writes no bundle and exits with 1. A second build of the same branch of the same project is refused while one runs.

The job image must provide `git` and `timeout` in the fixed system folders (`/usr/local/sbin`, `/usr/local/bin`, `/usr/sbin`, `/usr/bin`, `/sbin` and `/bin`), as files the Developer cannot change: when one is a link, every hop of the way to its file (each link, each folder on the way and the file itself) must be outside the job's work area, `TMPDIR`, `HOME` and the output folder. The job looks both up there before the Developer runs, and after the session it uses only those folders. When one is missing, or any hop lies in one of those folders, the job stops before the Developer runs, writes `the image's timeout cannot bound the bundle step` or `the image's git is not in its system folders` to `build.log`, and the build fails with no commit.

<a id="a-build-that-ends-with-a-question"></a>When the Developer ended with a question instead (the file `QUESTION.json`, see [What a Developer is told](#what-a-developer-is-told)), the command prints `question: <the question>`, one `choice: <choice>` line per choice and `recommendation: <the recommended choice>`, writes no bundle and exits with 0, also when the Developer made commits or wrote `PR.md` as well. In these texts, a line break or another control character, a format character (such as a zero-width space), a line or paragraph separator, a space other than the plain one, an unassigned or private-use code point and a character Unicode marks as default-ignorable (such as U+034F, a variation selector or a Hangul filler) is printed as its code point (a line break as `\u{A}`, a tab as `\u{9}`, U+034F as `\u{34F}`), and a backslash as `\\`. So each text stays on its own line, and two choices that differ only in such a character print differently. Letters that only look alike (a Latin and a Cyrillic "a", for example) are printed as they are. A `QUESTION.json` the branch already held when the build started counts only when the Developer changed the file's bytes as they were checked out (the job compares a SHA-256 digest of the file's bytes taken before and after the session, which no git setting of the clone affects); when the file's bytes cannot be read and digested before or after the session (for example because the file cannot be read), the question does not count and the build's log says why. Keys other than these three are ignored. Intakt checks the question first: it must be a JSON object of at most 8192 bytes, with at most 7 arrays or objects nested in one another counting the question object itself (also under a key that is otherwise ignored; a deeper one refuses the question with `question refused: the question is not a JSON object`), with a non-empty `question` text, a non-empty list (a JSON array, not an object) of non-empty `choices` texts, no two of them the same once surrounding spaces are removed, and a `recommendation` that is one of the choices. A question that breaks this rule is refused: the command prints the end of the build's log, which then ends with `question refused: <the broken rule>` (for example `question refused: the recommendation is not one of the choices`), and `failed: the build ended with a question that breaks the question rule`, and exits with 1. In `intakt:build:task` a build that ends with a valid question parks the task (see [Building one task](#building-one-task)); posting the question on the issue comes later.

A build session gets at most 100 turns and 3000 seconds (50 minutes); these are fixed for now. When it reaches either limit, the session ends, but the commits it made before are kept: the build then ends as described above, with the bundle of those commits when the Developer also wrote `PR.md` and left no question (a question it left comes first, and then no bundle is written). `build.log` then says `claude cut after 3000 seconds, its commits are kept` when the time ran out. A session that something else kills (for example the out-of-memory killer in the job) does not wait for the rest of the 3000 seconds: the build script packs the commits at once, and `build.log` says `claude exited with 137`, never the cut message when the kill lands before the time limit (a kill that lands just as the time limit is reached can be logged as the cut, with the same outcome). When the job then exits normally, the build goes to the hand-back under the same rules. When the job runner reports that the job was killed at its memory limit, the build ends as `the build job ran out of memory` and no bundle is read. The hour limit above is only a backstop for a job that hangs after the session: a job stopped by it hands back nothing. Packing the commits gets 30 seconds of its own, so something the Developer left in its working copy that makes git wait (for example a named pipe in place of a branch file) cannot hold the build until that hour is up: the job then hands back no commits, ends as soon as the 30 seconds are over, and `build.log` says `no bundle is handed back: git bundle create was cut after 30 seconds`.

Intakt works out how many gates the Developer's `bin/check` may run at once: `max(2, processors / build slots)`, and never more than 8. The division rounds down to a whole number: 11 processors and 3 slots give 3. The processors are the online processors of the machine Intakt runs on. The build slots are the number of builds you let run at the same time on that machine: set `intakt.build.slots` under `parameters:` in `config/services.yaml` (a whole number of at least 1; 1 by default) and run `php bin/console cache:clear`. With 12 processors, 1 slot gives 8, 2 slots give 6 and 3 slots give 4; with 2 processors and 3 slots it is still 2. The number is a ceiling: the build job hands it to `bin/check` (as `INTAKT_GATE_SLOTS`) only when you set more than one slot and the number is lower than what `bin/check` picks by itself from the job's CPUs and memory ([Quality gates](../architecture/quality-gates.md)). It never raises that pick, because more gates at once than the job's memory holds could get the build killed. In every other case, and always with 1 slot, the job hands over nothing and `bin/check` picks as it always does (it still reads an `INTAKT_GATE_SLOTS` that the job image itself sets). Today every build job runs with 2 CPUs and 4 GiB of memory, where `bin/check` picks 1 gate at once, and the lowest number Intakt works out is 2. So for now `intakt.build.slots` does not change a build job's check: the job's CPU limit is what keeps Developers on one machine apart. The setting starts to matter once build jobs get larger limits. The reason for the setting, measured on Developers that ran without such limits: three Developers on 12 processors that each ran 8 gates at once loaded the machine so much that one check took 7 to 9 minutes; checks that ran past the Developer's 10-minute tool limit went to the background, and the Developers spent most of their time waiting on their own checks.

Intakt also gives the build session a tool time limit: one command the Developer runs (its `bin/check --fast`, for example) may run up to 1500 seconds (25 minutes) inside its own tool call. This is the time limit of the Claude Code command-line tool's Bash tool, not the session's watchdog (the 3000 seconds above): a command that runs past it does not end the session, but it no longer finishes inside the call that started it (in the measurement above, such commands went to the background). The build job sets both `BASH_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `BASH_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS` to that limit in milliseconds when it starts the session, so the Developer gets that limit by default for a command and as the most one command may ask for. It exports them as environment variables and also passes them to the command-line tool as command-line settings (`--settings` with both values in its `env`), because Claude Code applies the `env` of the project's own `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` after it starts, and command-line settings rank above those files: a project cannot raise the limit that way, so the Developer cannot ask for more. To change it, set `intakt.build.tool_time_limit_seconds` under `parameters:` in `config/services.yaml` (a whole number of seconds, at least 1 and below the session's 3000 seconds) and run `php bin/console cache:clear`; any other value, including one that is not a whole number such as `true`, stops a build before its job starts, with a message that names the rule. The reason is the same measurement as above: with three Developers on 12 processors one `bin/check --fast` took 7 to 9 minutes, close to or past the tool's 10-minute default. Checks past it went to the background, and the Developers then polled for them: over 12 builds they waited 146 minutes on their own checks and 15 minutes on the model.

It needs a Docker engine (see [Job containers](../architecture/job-containers.md)). The container does not get your Claude login yet, so a real build cannot sign in; running one is not possible until that part is built.

## Checking what a build hands back

A build returns its commits as a git bundle, and the text of its pull request. `php bin/console intakt:hand-back:check <repository> <branch> <bundle> <pull-request-text> [--merge-main] [--credential-env=<variable>]...` checks both before anything trusts them. `<repository>` is a bare repository whose `HEAD` names main, such as the project's mirror; `<branch>` is the task's branch; `<pull-request-text>` is a file holding the pull request text (one that cannot be read is an error and exits with 1). A mirror that holds an `info/attributes` file is also an error with exit 1: the message names the file, and you remove it before the repository is read. The bundle is refused when:

- it is not a git bundle Intakt can read (a missing file, a folder, another file, a broken pack, or a filtered bundle);
- it updates main;
- it names any ref other than the task's branch;
- it builds on a commit that is neither on the task's pushed branch nor on main (also when its header does not list that commit, or it names a commit another branch holds without carrying it);
- it leaves out a commit, folder or file its new commits need (one the repository holds on another branch counts as left out);
- it is not a fast-forward of the pushed branch, or, when the branch was not pushed yet, it does not start on main (any commit of main counts, so main moving while the build ran refuses nothing);
- it holds a merge commit. With `--merge-main` (the task asked for a merge of main), a merge whose other parents are all on main is allowed; a merge of anything else is still refused;
- one of its new commits is malformed, so git itself would not write it (for example a commit with no author);
- a file or file name it adds holds the value of a credential mounted into the build job (see `--credential-env` below), also when a later commit deletes it again;
- the secret scan (`gitleaks` with its built-in rules) finds a secret in anything it adds: a commit and its message, a folder listing or a file, also one a later commit deletes again. Scanner settings, ignore lists and inline allow comments the build committed are ignored, and so are `GITLEAKS_CONFIG` and `GITLEAKS_CONFIG_TOML`.

When the bundle passes, the pull request text is refused when it holds the value of a mounted credential, or when the same secret scan finds a secret in it. The text is refused, not cleaned, so the Developer writes it again without the value.

A refusal is sent back to the Developer once: it prints `refused, sent back to the builder:` and a fixed reason. When the task's previous hand-back was sent back too, the refusal hands the task over instead and prints `refused, handed over:` and the reason. An accepted hand-back, or a hand-over, starts the count again. Either way the command exits with 1, and the reason never holds a ref name, commit id, secret or other text from the bundle or the pull request text. Each outcome is recorded in the ledger (`hand_back_accepted`, `hand_back_sent_back` or `hand_back_handed_over`, with the repository, the branch and the reason or the accepted head; see `intakt:ledger:events`), and a task is counted by its repository and branch. A ledger that does not take the line is an error and exits with 1. So is a ledger that cannot be read to count the task's earlier hand-backs (for example a missing `ledger_event` table): the command prints an error that says the round was not recorded because the ledger could not be read, exits with 1 and records no round. Nothing yet passes the reason to a Developer or the hand-over to the Maintainer; the build lane will. A branch name git does not accept, a branch that is main, and a path that is no such repository are errors and exit with 1 as well. So is a secret scan that cannot run or fails: the bundle is then not accepted, and nothing is recorded, because that is no fault of the build. The scan runs the `gitleaks` that `INTAKT_GITLEAKS` names (`gitleaks` from the path when it is unset or empty), as before a review is posted.

Name each credential that was mounted into the build job with `--credential-env=<variable>`, the environment variable that holds its value (the value itself never goes on the command line, where other users of the machine could see it). Its value is replaced by `[redacted]` in every new commit's message and in its author and committer, which gives that commit a new id like a stripped one. A named variable that is unset or empty is an error and exits with 1.

An accepted bundle loses its attribution: from each new commit (main's own commits are left as they are) the `Co-authored-by:` trailers and the lines that start with `Generated with`, `Generated by` or `Generated-by:` (in any case, after an emoji or other mark) are removed. A message written with Windows line endings keeps them: the collapsed blank line and the final newline use the same ending as the rest of the message. A changed commit, and every new commit after it, gets a new id and no signature, because Intakt signs the commits itself before it pushes. The command prints `accepted <head>`, `base <commit>` (the pushed branch it checked against, or `base none` when the branch was not pushed yet) and `rewritten <count>`, and exits with 0. Nothing is pushed and no ref is changed: the commits are only added to the repository's objects. A refused bundle adds nothing, because its objects are kept apart until every check passed. An accepted bundle also adds its commits as the build wrote them (with attribution or a credential in the message) beside the rewritten ones, in the repository only: no branch names them, so nothing pushes them; that is also so when the bundle passes and only the pull request text is refused.

## Pushing an accepted build

Intakt pushes an accepted build's branch itself: [`intakt:build:task`](#building-one-task) does it as its last step; the build lane that will call it is not built yet. The push signs every new commit with Intakt's own SSH key (the file `/app/var/data/keys/signing_key`, on the volume you mount at `/app/var/data`, which `ssh-keygen` from OpenSSH reads; a build never sees it; it must be an ed25519 key, any other type stops the push) and checks that every commit of the branch carries a signature of that key alone, so a commit signed by another key, or not signed, stops the push. Before anything is pushed, main is merged into the branch in the repository's object store only; a real conflict pushes nothing. `intakt:build:task` records the push as failed with the number of conflicting files (never their names, which the Developer chose) and ends the run; it does not send the branch back to the Developer yet. Only one push of a branch runs at a time; a second one fails with `another push of this branch is running` and can be tried again later. When the lock store (where Intakt notes the running push) fails, `intakt:build:task` records the push step as failed with `the lock store failed:` and the store's message. That can also happen after the branch was pushed and the pull request written, when only freeing the lock failed (Intakt tries that twice); running the build again then finds the push and the pull request already there and does not push again. A lock that could not be freed ends by itself after 5 minutes. When the push already failed for another reason, that reason is recorded instead. The push is never forced: a remote branch that is not where the build was checked is refused. Intakt reads the branch on the remote every time, also for a build whose push is already in the ledger: when the branch has moved since (a later build was pushed, someone else moved the branch, or it is gone), that earlier build is refused, nothing is written to its pull request and no new ledger entry is made, so a late retry of an earlier build never replaces the title and text of the build that followed it. Before anything is pushed, Intakt asks GitHub for every open pull request of the branch, whatever branch it targets: when any of them was opened by someone other than Intakt's own GitHub App (a person, the earlier loop or another App), when GitHub does not name its author, or when its author cannot be checked against the App, the push stops with an error, nothing is pushed and no ledger entry is made, so Intakt does not move the head of a pull request that someone else had open when it asked. This is not a full guarantee, because GitHub cannot tie this question to the push: a pull request someone else opens of the branch after the question and before the push does have its head moved by the push; Intakt then asks again before it writes to GitHub, stops with an error and leaves that pull request's title and text alone. An open pull request that GitHub lists without its branch name, or with an empty one, stops the push the same way. A project without a `github` entry in its watch section stops at this point too, and so does a branch with 100 or more open pull requests, because GitHub's answer then may not list them all. A rate limit on that question stops the push the same way, and it can be tried again after the reset. Intakt then opens the pull request as the GitHub App with the Developer's title and text, or updates its own open one of that branch to main (asking GitHub the same question again just before), and refuses text that trips the secret scan. Its own pull request of the branch to another branch is never retargeted: Intakt opens a new one to main instead, and stops with an error when GitHub lists its own pull request without the branch it targets. A rework that corrects only the title or the text and keeps the commits updates the pull request too: with `change_opened` the ledger keeps a digest of the text (never the text itself), and the pull request is neither looked up nor written again only when the commits and the text are the ones last recorded and no later write to GitHub was started. Before Intakt writes to GitHub it notes that in the ledger (`change_intended`), so a title or text that reached GitHub while its own record failed is never taken for the one recorded before it: the next push sends its text again. The phases are written to the ledger (`push_intended` before the push, `branch_pushed` after it, `change_intended` before the pull request is written, `change_opened` after it), so a crash neither pushes twice nor opens a second pull request. When the remote refuses the push (for example a local git forge repository that has the task branch checked out), the ledger gets `push_refused` with the reason (`the remote refused the push of <branch> (git push exited with <n>)`, never git's own message, which may name the remote's address), no pull request is written, and the next push of the same build tries again. A `git` call of the push may run for 10 minutes and an `ssh-keygen` call for one; a call that runs longer is stopped, and a call that is stopped, killed or cannot start ends the push with a reason that names the program and never the remote's address or the key's file (`The branch was not pushed: git push timed out after 600 seconds`). Intakt does not try such a call again on its own: the next push reads the branch on the remote first and pushes only when the stopped push did not arrive. GitHub does not show these commits as "Verified": GitHub only trusts a signing key registered on a user account whose email is the commit's committer, and the commits keep the Developer as committer. Git itself verifies them against the public key of Intakt's signing key.

## Listing the protected paths a head touches

The files a change may only touch with an approval (a project's checks, its CI setup, its agents' instructions) are your list, kept in `config/intakt.yaml` (or the file `INTAKT_CONFIG` names), never in the project's repository, so a change can never edit the list that judges it. Give each project a `protected_paths` list and run `php bin/console cache:clear`:

```yaml
projects:
    alpha:
        protected_paths: [bin/check, config/gates/, .github, AGENTS.md]
```

An entry names a file or a folder from the repository's root, and protects that file, or everything in that folder, at any depth; a trailing `/` changes nothing. Letters must match exactly, also in case, so `bin/check` does not protect `bin/checker` or `BIN/check`. An entry that starts with `/`, has an empty, `.` or `..` part, or is not text stops Intakt from starting and names the entry. `.gitmodules` at the root and every submodule are always protected, also without an entry; a project without a list protects only those.

`php bin/console intakt:protected:list <project> <head>` lists the protected paths the head (a full commit id) touches, read from the project's mirror (run `intakt:mirror:update` first), where main is the commit the branch the mirror's `HEAD` names points at when the check starts (a mirror update during the check does not change it). It reads the whole change from where the head left main to the head, and each commit the head has that main lacks, also those a merge brought in from another branch, so a protected file changed and changed back, or reverted or deleted by a merge made by hand, is still listed. What a merge of main brings in is main's own and is not listed: a file a commit leaves exactly as the current main has it is never listed for that commit, while a merge that sets a file back to an older main's version is. It prints `protected <path>` for each path, then `touches protected paths: yes` or `no`, and exits with 0. When it cannot read the change (no mirror, a mirror whose `HEAD` names no branch or a branch without a commit, a commit the mirror does not hold, an id that names an annotated tag, a shallow mirror, a missing object), it prints `unreadable` and the reason, and the head counts as touching protected paths. A head that is not a full commit id, such as a branch name, and a project name that breaks the rule above, are refused with exit 1. Holding a protected head for your approval is not built yet.

## The watchdog's rules for main

Intakt has the rules its watchdog will use to judge each new commit on main, and it can read main's commits and check their signatures. You can run the check by hand with `intakt:watchdog:check` (below), or with `intakt:watchdog:guard`, which also halts the project and gives you a card for each violation (see [Halting the project](#halting-the-project-and-clearing-your-card)); nothing runs either on its own yet. A merge Intakt recorded passes before any signature is looked at, and so do the commits that merge brought to main, also when main simply moved forward to the merged branch so that its commits sit on main one by one. A commit main already had before that merge is not covered by it. Any other commit passes only when it is signed with a key you registered, or with the key of another loop you give as a peer ([Running beside another loop](#running-beside-another-loop)). Owner keys are SSH public keys (one `<type> <base64>` line each, as in a `.pub` file; an Ed25519, RSA or ECDSA key, also one on a security key; a key that is cut short, of another type, an RSA key shorter than 1024 bits or with an extra leading zero byte in its exponent or modulus (`ssh-keygen` writes none) or, for ECDSA, not an uncompressed point on its curve is refused), and a signature counts only as an SSH signature that `ssh-keygen` verifies against those keys; a PGP signature, or a check that fails or times out, counts as a key you did not register. A commit without a signature, a commit signed with a key you did not register, and a rewrite of main's history (a force push) are violations. A recorded merge names its head and base as full commit ids; an abbreviated id or a branch name is refused. Main is read only from the repository itself: the top folder of a checkout, or the folder of a bare repository. A plain folder inside a checkout (not a checkout of its own, such as a linked work tree), a path that is a file, a shallow clone (one made with `--depth`, which lacks older commits) and a repository with a `.git/info/attributes` file are refused as unreadable, never judged clean or rewritten. The rules are in [Watchdog over main](../architecture/watchdog.md).

`php bin/console intakt:watchdog:check <repository> <last-seen-head> [--recorded-merge=<base>..<head> ...] [--owner-key=<key> ...] [--peer-key=<key> ...]` checks the commits on the repository's `main` branch after `<last-seen-head>` (a full commit id). `--recorded-merge` names one merge Intakt recorded, its base and head as full commit ids with two dots between them, base first; it is optional and you can give it more than once. `--owner-key` is one of your SSH public keys (`<type> <base64>`, in quotes because of the space); it is optional and you can give it more than once. Without it, every signed commit counts as signed with a key you did not register (unless it was signed with a peer key, below). The command prints `main is clean` and exits with 0, or prints `violation <commit> <reason>` for each violating commit, oldest first (`unsigned`, `unknown_key` or `history_rewritten`), and exits with 1.
